# Round 3 review: findings and fix status

Code, QA and security review of StitchBench (9ba25a4) and StitchHub (28842a1), 27 Sep 2026. Each finding was checked by an independent verifier (high ones twice). Interactive version: https://claude.ai/artifact/8fjYFHAaZMz4gEuHasQvEG

Totals: 259 findings (high 6, medium 86, low 139, info 28); 14 refuted by verifiers (listed at the end).

Status on 30 Sep 2026 (branch claude/remaining-tasks-l5s9q3 in both repos): high 6/6 fixed; medium 79 fixed, 7 partly done; low 116 fixed, 15 partly done, 8 open; info 4 fixed, 13 partly done, 11 open. The open and partly done ones say what is left and why (most need an owner decision, a feature, or a spec that needs more test setup). Verified with a build, every migration, the drift check, the unit tests, the Playwright specs (smoke, auth, hub, inventory, market, partners, register) and the hub smoke test.

Status on 30 Sep 2026, evening (branch round3-tail, after the deploy): high 6/6 fixed; medium 80 fixed, 6 partly done; low 124 fixed, 7 partly done, 8 open; info 6 fixed, 13 partly done, 9 open. The round3-tail pass finished M12, L22, L51, L90, L117, L121, L130, L133 and L139, and added the second company and tech user to the e2e setup with tests/tenant.spec.ts, tests/roles.spec.ts and more of tests/auth.spec.ts (I4, I5, I19, I20).

Status key: FIXED = committed; PARTLY DONE = edits exist but unfinished; OPEN = still to fix. Plan agreed with the owner: fix all high + medium first, then low/info.


## High

### H1. package-lock.json is out of sync: `npm ci` fails on every platform, so the documented Railway deploy/update breaks at install
- **Where:** stitchbench `package-lock.json:2605` (build-test-infra; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (8698ffd: lock regenerated, @emnapi listed as optional deps, npm ci in the installers and a new Checks workflow; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The lock file has no top-level entries for `@emnapi/core` or `@emnapi/runtime`, but it needs them. `@tailwindcss/oxide-wasm32-wasi` (line 2605) depends on `@emnapi/core ^1.11.1` and `@emnapi/runtime ^1.11.1`. `@img/sharp-wasm32` (line 1390, a non-dev optional dependency of sharp/next) depends on `@emnapi/runtime ^1.7.0`. Only nested copies exist under `@unrs/resolver-binding-wasm32-wasi/node_modules`. npm 10 and npm 11 both refuse `npm ci` with this lock. Commit 6ec3f62 ('Lock files in step with package.json (npm ci on a clean machine)') did not fix it. ad2243e then switched the installers workflow to `npm install`, with a comment that blames 'a lock file written on Windows'. But the failure reproduces on Linux with a clean copy of the committed lock. INSTALL.md Option 2 deploys with `railway up` or Deploy-from-GitHub, and railway.json has no build section, so Railway's default Node builder installs with `npm ci` because a package-lock.json is present. Every hosted install and every hosted update therefore fails before `npm run build`. The CI workaround also means installer builds use whatever `@emnapi/*` version is newest at build time, not a locked one.
- **Scenario:** A shop follows INSTALL.md Option 2 (`railway up --service stitchbench --ci`), or an existing hosted shop redeploys to pick up the round-1/round-2 security fixes. The build's install step exits with EUSAGE 'Missing: @emnapi/runtime@1.11.3 from lock file', so nothing is deployed. The hosted site cannot be installed or updated.
- **Fix:** Regenerate the lock on Linux (`rm -rf node_modules && npm install` with npm 11, then check that `npm ci` passes in a clean checkout), or add `@emnapi/core` and `@emnapi/runtime` as explicit optional dependencies so they get top-level lock entries. Put the installers workflow back on `npm ci`, and add a CI job that runs `npm ci` on ubuntu-latest so the lock can't drift again. Optionally pin Railway's install command in railway.json.

### H2. Part-source remove/prefer writes are scoped by the caller's partId on a table with no shopId, and nothing checks the argument is a string
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/parts.ts:135` (gap; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** removePartSource and preferPartSource prove ownership with ownPart(partId), which runs db.part.findFirst({ where: { id: partId, shopId } }), a filter query. The write then uses the caller's original partId and sourceId in partSource.deleteMany / updateMany, not the id the ownership query returned. PartSource has no shopId column (schema.prisma:436), so the partId filter is the only thing keeping the write in one shop. Server-action arguments are not type-checked at runtime (no typeof/zod checks anywhere in src/actions), so the write's scope depends on the argument being a plain string, and the code never enforces that. Most other actions happen to be safe because their writes go through findUnique/update({ where: { id } }) or use the DB-returned id. This pair is the exception.
- **Scenario:** A signed-in user of shop A calls the server action with id arguments that are not plain strings. The findFirst ownership check still matches a part in shop A, and the following deleteMany/updateMany on PartSource, with no shopId to scope it, can reach supplier rows that belong to other shops on the hosted multi-shop install (deleting them, or flipping their preferred flags).
- **Fix:** Reject non-string arguments at the top of every server action (a small assertId helper, or zod). In these three actions use the id ownPart returns (p.id) in every write, and scope sourceId through the relation: deleteMany({ where: { id: String(sourceId), part: { id: p.id, shopId } } }). Better still, add shopId to PartSource, as AGENTS.md requires for business tables.

### H3. Cash refunds at the register always fail (regression from round 1)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/register.ts:222` (register-money; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Round 1 replaced the old change check with `const cash = sum of CASH tenders; ... if (totals.change > cash) throw new UserError("Only cash gives change...")`. On a refund (total < 0), every tender has to be negative, so `cash` is negative whenever any cash is paid out, while `totals.change` is always 0 for refunds. `0 > negative` is true, so any refund that includes a cash tender is rejected. The client never checks this: it enables "Complete refund" as soon as paid === total. Paying a customer back in cash from the drawer, the usual way returns are settled at a fabric or notions counter, cannot be done. The only workaround is recording the refund as Card, Check or Other, which puts the wrong figures in the register-sales report's "Taken in" by-method totals and the cash-drawer count. Before 8b3e5f2 the check was `totals.change > 0 && !tenders.some(CASH)`, which never fired on a refund.
- **Scenario:** Owner or staff turns on Return mode, scans QA-SPOOL-1 ($4.99 + 6.35% tax = $5.31 refund), clicks Cash (tender -531), then clicks Complete refund. The toast reads "Only cash gives change. Take the exact amount on the card or check." and nothing is saved. The same refund recorded as Card goes through.
- **Fix:** Apply the check only to sales: `if (totals.total >= 0 && totals.change > cash) throw ...` (or compare against `Math.max(0, cash)`). Add an e2e test or a register-math unit test covering a cash refund, a card refund and a mixed exchange.

### H4. Open sign-up with a partner code connects the whole install to a hub of the signer's choosing, skipping the install-admin check
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/signup/page.tsx:51` (auth-accounts; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** redeemPartnerCode() connects the entire install to the hub named in a long partner code when the install isn't on a hub yet. Its own doc comment (src/lib/partners.ts:180-181) says callers must check who may do that (requireInstallAdmin). enterPartnerCode does check (src/actions/partners.ts:55: `if (!(await hubJoined())) await requireInstallAdmin();`). The /signup server action calls redeemPartnerCode(shop.id, shop.name, email, code) for an anonymous visitor with no such check. The new shop is never an install admin on a multi-company install, because shop.count() > 1 once it exists. After the join, doSync (src/lib/hub.ts:618-622) takes `scope` from the hub's /v1/me answer, so the hub can switch the install from 'partner' to full 'member'. From then on pushPending() uploads every company's queued shared notes and photos to that hub, and the hub's links, posts, listings and Discord link reach every company (the nav shows chatUrl once scope != 'partner').
- **Scenario:** The hosted multi-shop install runs with ALLOW_SIGNUP=true and hasn't joined a hub. A visitor opens /signup and enters a long code `stitchpartner:<base64 {url:'https://hub.attacker.tld', code:'AAAABBBBCCCC'}>`. hubState row 1 becomes url=https://hub.attacker.tld with the installKey that host returned. Every other company's hub traffic (shared notes and photos, partner invites, usage) then goes to that host, and its content is shown to every company until the platform admin notices and uses Leave the hub. A short code with no URL still joins DEFAULT_HUB_URL on the operator's behalf.
- **Fix:** In the signup action, only redeem the code when the install is already on a hub (hubJoined()), or when this is the first company (firstOnly / shop.count() was 0 before the create). Otherwise save the code and tell the new owner to enter it under Partner shops once the administrator has joined a hub. Separately, don't let /v1/me upgrade a 'partner' join to 'member': keep the locally chosen scope unless an install admin joins as a member.

### H5. One partner (or ex-partner) can permanently wedge a victim install's partner sync and OOM the shared hub with big messages
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/partners.mjs:190` (hub-authz-sweep; security, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** GET /v1/partners/messages returns up to `limit` (StitchBench always asks 200, hub caps at 500) whole messages in one JSON response, each up to MAX_MESSAGE = 4 MB (partners.mjs:13, enforced at :179). Nothing caps the total bytes per page or the number of messages a partner may pile into one seq window. A member on any partner link can send ~130+ messages of ~4 MB each (the pmsg limit is a generous 3000/hour, partners.mjs:172) at the victim. When the victim's install next syncs, the hub tries to build a single ~800 MB JSON string for the page and throws `RangeError: Invalid string length` (V8 max string), returning HTTP 500. StitchBench's pullMessages hard-codes limit=200 (src/lib/partners.ts:532) and, on the 500, records partnerError and does NOT advance partnerCursor (src/lib/partners.ts:562/611), so every subsequent sync re-fetches the same poisoned page and 500s again: the victim install's ENTIRE partner channel (all its partnerships, not just the attacker's) is permanently stuck until the messages age out after KEEP_DAYS=365. The feed query selects messages across every link the install is on (partners.mjs:197-204), so poison on one link blocks all links. Because ended links keep accepting new messages (myLink at partners.mjs:102 and the messages route never check link.status), an attacker can do this even after the victim ends the partnership. Serialising even one 200x4MB page spikes the single Railway hub process to ~1.4 GB, so one member can also OOM the shared hub for the whole community.
- **Scenario:** Malicious retail partner (valid install/partner key) on link L with victim repair shop V. Victim ends L. Attacker POSTs 130 messages of type job.update with data {junk: '<4MB string>'} to /v1/partners/links/L/messages (all accepted, HTTP 200). Victim's next partner sync calls GET /v1/partners/messages?since=<cursor>&limit=200 -> hub 500 (RangeError). Cursor never advances; V's partner sync is dead for all partners; hub RSS spikes toward OOM.
- **Fix:** Cap the feed by total serialized bytes, not just message count: accumulate messages until a byte budget (e.g. 8-16 MB) is reached and return `more:true` so the client pages through, and/or have StitchBench request a small limit and fall back to limit=1 on a 500. Also cap per-link message volume/rate and stop accepting new non-final messages on ENDED links (or a much lower cap once ended). Consider streaming the response instead of JSON.stringify of the whole page.

### H6. Unauthenticated /v1/register (and every non-partner route) reads an unlimited body into memory: remote memory DoS. The round-3 body-limit fix only covered partners.mjs
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/server.mjs:126` (hub-core; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Commit 28842a1 added hono bodyLimit, but only to the routes in partners.mjs. server.mjs has no global body limit, and @hono/node-server imposes none. /v1/register is anonymous and calls `await c.req.json()` before it checks the join key, so it buffers the whole request, converts it to a string and parses it. The only guard in front of it is the X-Forwarded-For rate limit, and the client can spoof that header (see the separate finding). Member routes do the same after authentication: PUT /v1/figures/:id reads `c.req.arrayBuffer()` (line 458) and only then checks the 2 MB cap; /v1/posts calls parseBody (line 383) before checking the size of each photo; /v1/links, the parts routes and the others call c.req.json() with no limit; /v1/admin/import (line 600) reads the upload with no size or decompression limit.
- **Scenario:** An anonymous internet client sends POST /v1/register with a 300-600 MB body (Content-Type can be text/plain, so a malicious web page can make visitors' browsers send it with no preflight). Each request drives the hub to about 1.3 GB RSS. Several at once exhaust the container's memory, and the hub is OOM-killed for every shop. The join key is not needed.
- **Fix:** Register a global limit before any route: `app.use('*', bodyLimit({ maxSize: 64*1024 }))`. Give larger per-route limits where needed: posts about 12 x 3 MB plus overhead, figures 2 MB, datasets and import as admin-configured and counted as the body streams. Keep /v1/register at a few KB. Check Content-Length first and use bodyLimit for chunked bodies, as partners.mjs already does.


## Medium

### M1. Restoring a backup made before a program update leaves the older specs and factory settings in place for good
- **Where:** stitchbench `desktop/backup.mjs:145` (files-library-desktop; security, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by the merged fix-agent/hub-core work; re-checked in round-3 part 2)
- **Problem:** restore() truncates and reloads the REFERENCE tables (TensionSpec, FactorySetting, LibraryDoc) from the backup. reference.mjs decides whether to reload the shipped set by comparing the table comment (`seed:<hash>`) with the shipped file's hash. TRUNCATE doesn't touch table comments, so after a restore the comment still says the new set is loaded while the rows are the backup's older set. Every later start then skips loading, until an update ships a different seed file.
- **Scenario:** The shop updates to a version with corrected tension specs or factory settings (loaded at start, comment = new hash). Later the owner restores last month's backup, or moves to a new PC and restores. The old values and labels come back, settings added in the update disappear, and ticket readings keyed by those new ids lose their labels. Restarting the program doesn't fix it, and nothing warns the owner.
- **Fix:** In restore(), for each REFERENCE table that is loaded from the backup, run `COMMENT ON TABLE ... IS NULL` inside the same transaction, so the next start reloads the shipped set. Alternatively, don't restore TensionSpec, FactorySetting or LibraryDoc (the program ships them); restore only FigureImage and ModelImage, which don't ship. main.mjs could also call loadReference right after a restore.

### M2. backupsToKeep = 0 (or any non-number) deletes every backup, including the one just made, and still reports 'Backup saved'
- **Where:** stitchbench `desktop/backup.mjs:98` (files-library-desktop; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by the merged fix-agent/hub-core work; re-checked in round-3 part 2)
- **Problem:** Pruning is `listBackups(root, flavor).slice(keep)` with keep taken straight from the user-editable config.json (INSTALL.md documents backupsToKeep). With 0 (a common way to write 'keep all'), null, or a non-numeric string like "all" (slice(NaN) is slice(0)), every backup of this program is deleted, including the one just written. backup() still returns its path, so the tray says 'Backup saved.' and the log says 'saved to <dir>'. Negative numbers delete the oldest backups instead of keeping any number.
- **Scenario:** The owner sets "backupsToKeep": 0 in config.json, meaning unlimited. The next nightly backup deletes all 30 existing backups and then its own. Months later the disk dies and there's nothing to restore. Nothing in the tray or the log showed a problem.
- **Fix:** Validate the value: `const keep = Number.isInteger(n) && n > 0 ? n : 30` (or treat 0 as 'never prune'), and always leave out the backup just created (`.filter(d => d !== dir)`) when pruning. Log and notify when the setting is invalid.

### M3. Quit and Restart hang forever once the embedded Postgres has already exited (failed start or crash), so the program stays running with no way to close it
- **Where:** stitchbench `desktop/main.mjs:640` (files-library-desktop; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by the merged fix-agent/hub-core work; re-checked in round-3 part 2)
- **Problem:** quit() does `await db?.stop()` with no timeout. embedded-postgres 17.10.0-beta.17 stop() returns early only when `this.process` is unset. Otherwise it waits for that process's 'exit' event (`this.process.on('exit', resolve)` followed by taskkill on Windows). When start() fails, the spawned postgres has already exited and emitted 'exit', but `this.process` is still set. main.mjs:206-211 swallows that failure on purpose ('trying the running one'). A postgres that dies while the program runs leaves the same state. From then on quit() never reaches app.exit(0).
- **Scenario:** Case 1: postgres fails to start (port 5439 taken, disk full, antivirus) and the fallback connect fails. The 'couldn't start' dialog shows, then `return quit()` (line 673) hangs. The process keeps the single-instance lock and the tray icon, and every later launch just calls showWindow() on a dead server. Case 2: postgres crashes, or the program adopted one left running. Tray 'Quit' and 'Restart StitchBench' (app.relaunch()+quit()) do nothing. Further clicks return early because `quitting` is already true. The user has to use Task Manager.
- **Fix:** In quit(), race db.stop() against a timeout (e.g. 10 s). Skip stop() when `db.process?.exitCode != null` or start() failed; in that case clear db.process and stop an adopted server with `pg_ctl stop -D <dir> -m fast`. Always call app.exit afterwards.

### M4. The first-run sign-up close restarts the server mid-sign-up, which can burn the partner code and leave the owner on a 404
- **Where:** stitchbench `desktop/main.mjs:369` (files-library-desktop; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (a5964f6)
- **Problem:** Every 15 s, once a Shop row exists, the program calls restartServer(), which kills the Next server (TerminateProcess on Windows) with no regard for in-flight requests. The signup server action commits the Shop first (createShopWithOwner). It then calls redeemPartnerCode(), a network call to the hub with a 30 s timeout on a fresh install, and then signIn(). A tick that lands in that window kills the request. The restart is not needed for safety: createShopWithOwner({firstOnly}) already refuses a second company under pg_advisory_xact_lock(7710001) (src/lib/shops.ts:48-51). After the restart the program calls `win?.reload()` instead of loading home(), so the window reloads /signup?setup=<token>, which now 404s because ALLOW_SIGNUP=false.
- **Scenario:** StitchShop first run (the documented path) with a partner code: the Shop commits, the hub's /v1/partners/join call is in flight, and the 15 s poll fires and kills the server. The hub has consumed the one-time code and registered an install whose key never reaches HubState. The owner is not signed in, and the window reloads to a 404. Re-entering the code fails as already used, and the repair shop now has a partner install that never answers. The chance is roughly the redeem-plus-signIn time divided by 15 s, so a few percent up to about 15%.
- **Fix:** Remove the restart and rely on firstOnly, which is already enforced under an advisory lock. Also have the signup page notFound() when SETUP_TOKEN is set and a shop already exists. If a restart is still wanted, do it only when the window has left /signup and no request is in flight, and use win.loadURL(home()) rather than reload().

### M5. release seed (--if-empty) never loads updated tension specs / factory settings on hosted installs, and an interrupted first load stays partial forever
- **Where:** stitchbench `prisma/seed.ts:173` (data-model; code, qa, security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** `npm start` runs `prisma migrate deploy && tsx prisma/seed.ts --if-empty`. With --if-empty, step() skips a reference table whenever count() > 0. So (1) a release that ships corrected or added specs never reaches a hosted install after its first deploy. README.md:107-108 says 'npm start ... loads any missing reference data before the server starts'. (2) The first load inserts in non-transactional 1000-row createMany chunks (seed.ts:45 and :99). If that start is killed or loses the DB mid-way, the next deploy sees count > 0, prints 'already loaded' and never finishes. The Windows program does this correctly (desktop/reference.mjs: content hash stored as a table comment, reload in one transaction), so hosted and desktop installs drift apart. The reference data does change: 6 commits touch prisma/seed-data, including db3261c 'second OCR pass' (factory_settings.json +76,996/-20,457 lines, with values capped or blanked after checking). A hosted shop deployed before that keeps the old OCR values.
- **Scenario:** A hosted install was first deployed with the earlier factory_settings.json. A later release fixes a wrong hook-timing value and adds 1,800 settings. Every redeploy logs 'Factory settings: already loaded', so techs on the hosted install keep seeing the old wrong value and none of the new models. Separately, a first boot interrupted after one chunk leaves 1,000 of 14,392 settings permanently.
- **Fix:** Use the desktop approach in seed.ts: hash each JSON file and compare it with a stored marker (COMMENT ON TABLE, or a small ReferenceVersion table). When it differs, run deleteMany(notIn) plus upsert/createMany inside a single transaction, and write the marker last. Keep --if-empty only for the first-shop step, or drop it.

### M6. Case-insensitive part-number lookups use unescaped ILIKE, so '_' and '%' act as wildcards: import, scan, tickets and create/update pick the wrong item
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/inventory-import.ts:71` (parts-inventory; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Every 'same number in any case' lookup uses Prisma `partNumber: { equals: x, mode: "insensitive" }`. With Prisma 7 plus adapter-pg this turns into an ILIKE pattern, and '_' and '%' in the value are not escaped. '_' matches any one character and '%' matches any run of characters. The import then orders the candidates by partNumber and takes the first (`orderBy: { partNumber: "asc" }`), so a wildcard match can win even when an exact match exists. The same pattern is used in scan.ts:19 (findPart), parts.ts:51 (createPart exists check), parts.ts:72 (updatePart clash check), tickets.ts:289 (addLine, a findFirst with no ordering), register.ts:56, and market.ts:147/177.
- **Scenario:** The shop has items 'Q-7' (Dash item) and 'Q_7'. It imports a line sheet row 'Q_7, Real underscore item v2, qty 3, retail 2.00'. The import reports '1 updated', but it overwrote Q-7's description and price and received 3 into Q-7; Q_7 is untouched. Scanning 'Z%' in Receive mode (no exact match) received +1 into an arbitrary item, Z00001. createPart for a new 'AB_1' when 'AB-1' exists says 'AB-1 is already in inventory' or redirects to AB-1, so the new item can't be created. On tickets, addLine for 'Q_7' can bill and decrement 'Q-7'.
- **Fix:** Stop using `equals`+`insensitive` for identifiers. Options: (1) add a generated lower(partNumber) column or functional index and compare with plain equality (raw `lower("partNumber") = lower($1)`), or (2) escape the value before the lookup (`s.replace(/[\\%_]/g, "\\$&")`) and re-check candidates in JS with `p.partNumber.toLowerCase() === pn.toLowerCase()`. In the import, prefer the exact case-insensitive match explicitly rather than relying on `orderBy partNumber asc`. Apply the same fix to tickets.ts, register.ts, market.ts, parts.ts and scan.ts (and the machine serial/brand lookups that use the same pattern).

### M7. Spreadsheet import matches existing items only by exact part number, not by barcode or other numbers, so it creates duplicate items and splits stock
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/inventory-import.ts:67` (parts-inventory; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Before creating a row, importItems looks for an existing item only where partNumber equals the row number (or '<number> (own)'). It never checks the row's barcode against altNumbers or numberKeys, although the scanner, register and ticket lookups all match on numberKeys. A common path goes like this: a product is scanned, it isn't in parts, 'add as a new part' creates it with the UPC as its number, and later the supplier's line sheet (SKU + UPC) is imported. That creates a second item for the same product. The two items share a barcode, the delivery quantity and new prices land on the second one, and scanning the barcode keeps finding the first (exact-number match ranks first), which now shows the wrong stock and no price.
- **Scenario:** Shop B: /parts?new=074427173148 > description 'Clover seam ripper', qty 4 > Add part. Then import [{SKU 3148, UPC 074427173148, 'Clover Seam Ripper', cost 4.10, retail 8.25, qty 6}]. Observed: 'added 1, updated 0'. Two items now exist: 074427173148 (qty 4, no price) and 3148 (qty 6, $8.25, alt 074427173148). Scanning 074427173148 reports '4 on hand' with no price. Expected: the row matches the existing item through its barcode and updates it (qty 10, price $8.25), or the import at least warns about the conflict.
- **Fix:** When no item has the part number, also look up own items (consignLinkId null) whose numberKeys contain numberKey(barcode) or numberKey(partNumber). If exactly one matches, update it and add the SKU as an alt number (or rename it). If several match, skip the row and list it in the result so the user can resolve it.

### M8. On a multi-company install, every company lists, orders, rates and posts on the hub under the name and reputation of whichever company joined
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/market.ts:67` (gap; security, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The hub knows one identity per install: `inst.name` is fixed at /v1/register from the name of the company whose admin joined. It stamps that name on everything:
- listings (stitchhub market.mjs:229-230, shop_name = inst.name);
- orders (market.mjs:335, buyer_shop/seller_shop from installs.name);
- shared notes (server.mjs:408);
- link and xpart revisions (byShop);
- Discord announcements.
Ratings and reputation are keyed to the install id (order_ratings.rated_install_id; listings.sellerId). StitchBench sends no per-company identity: createListing, placeOrder and pushPending only use the company to scope local rows. So on a hosted multi-company StitchBench, any user of company B (a TECH, or anyone who signed up when ALLOW_SIGNUP=true) appears to the whole community as company A. B's listings show A's name and A's seller rating. B's orders arrive at sellers as buyer 'A'. Ratings B earns (good or bad) land on A. B's notes are credited to A. This is a different root cause from the per-install quota findings already reported: it is identity and reputation, not budget.
- **Scenario:** A hosted install holds the established 'E2E Test Shop' (it joined the hub) and 'Tenant B Shop'. Tenant B lists a part. Every hub member sees it as sold by 'E2E Test Shop', with that shop's rating. A buyer orders, pays the payment instructions B sets at accept, and B never ships. The buyer's 1-star rating and complaint land on E2E Test Shop. Tenant B can do this at scale, trading on A's name.
- **Fix:** Give each company its own hub identity: its own install key or a hub-side sub-account per company, with the company name, and ratings and reputation keyed to it. Until then, allow the marketplace, shared notes and ratings only for the company that joined the hub, or on single-company installs, and send the company's own name with each listing, order and note.

### M9. Editing a listing always sends the form's stale quantity, overwriting accepts and restocks made since the page loaded (overselling)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/market.ts:93` (gap; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The 'Change your listing' form (market/[id]/page.tsx) always posts qty with defaultValue = the local copy's qty, and updateListing forwards it whenever it parses (body.qty = qty). The hub's PUT /v1/listings/:id sets qty to that absolute value (market.mjs:252-262) with no expected-version check. Hub-side changes after the page loaded, or before the next 10-minute sync, get overwritten when the seller only meant to change the price or status: accept decrements qty and a cancel after acceptance restocks it (market.mjs restock). An accept made on another device is undone and the listing takes orders for units already committed. A buyer's cancel is wiped out and the restocked units disappear.
- **Scenario:** Listing qty 5, seller page open. Buyer orders 2 and seller accepts from another device (hub qty 3). Seller changes the price on the open page and saves (qty still 5). Hub qty goes back to 5, ACTIVE. A second order for 5 is then accepted, so 7 units are committed against 5 on the shelf.
- **Fix:** Only send qty when the seller changed it: carry the loaded qty in a hidden field and compare. Better, have the hub accept an optimistic check (e.g. expectedQty or updatedAt) and return 409 when the listing changed, then re-sync and show the current quantity.

### M10. placeConsignment stores negative shelf prices and unbounded/over-stock quantities, corrupting the repair shop's own inventory and permanently diverging from the partner
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/partner-orders.ts:185` (partners; qa, security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The repair-shop consignment action validates far more weakly than the message applier that consumes it. qty (line 181-183) is only checked for `qty > 0` with no upper bound and no check against qtyOnHand, and price (line 184-185) is only rejected when falsy (0/null) — a negative shelf price passes `if (!price)`. So the repair shop can place a negative-priced consignment, or a quantity larger than its stock or larger than the partner will accept. The item is written to ConsignItem (with the bad price/qty) and the repair shop's Part.qtyOnHand is decremented unconditionally (line 188), driving it negative. The corresponding consign.place hub message is then rejected on the sewing-shop side, because receiveConsigned uses `int(it.priceCents, 0, MAX_MONEY)` and `qtyOf` (|qty| <= MAX_QTY=100000) in src/lib/partners.ts — so the placement silently half-completes: recorded on the repair side, quarantined on the sewing side, and shown as if the *partner* sent bad data.
- **Scenario:** Repair shop opens Partners > (partner) > Consignment, picks a part with 10 on hand, enters qty 3 and shelf price -9.00 -> 'Placed on their shelf'. DB: Part.qtyOnHand 10->7, ConsignItem placed=3 priceCents=-900, outbox consign.place priceCents=-900. On the next sync the sewing shop sets the message aside ('shelf price is out of range') so the item never appears on their shelf, and it lands under 'Messages that didn't get through'. Entering qty 1000000 similarly succeeds locally (stock -> negative, e.g. -999982) and is quarantined ('qty is out of range').
- **Fix:** Validate before placing: reject price <= 0 or > MAX_MONEY (e.g. `if (!(price > 0) || price > 10_000_000) throw UserError`), cap/round qty to the same MAX_QTY the applier enforces, and reject qty greater than part.qtyOnHand (or explicitly allow negative stock, but then the applier must accept the same values). Mirror the limits in src/lib/partners.ts so both sides agree.

### M11. partnerOrderAction checks the order status outside the transaction and updates without a status condition: two concurrent Delivered or Received clicks move stock twice
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/partner-orders.ts:93` (partners; code, security, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The order is read before the transaction (line 79). The status checks (lines 89-104, 111) use that stale copy, and tx.partnerOrder.update (line 146) has no status in its where clause. Two submits of 'Delivered' from two tabs or two staff both pass, both decrement stock for every line (line 101), and both queue an order.update. The same applies to 'Received' on the buyer side (stock added twice). Mixed actions misbehave too. Accept and Deliver clicked together can leave the order ACCEPTED after its stock was taken, so it can be delivered, and decremented, again. Cancel and Deliver together can leave it CANCELLED with the stock gone.
- **Scenario:** The repair shop has the order open in two tabs, or two employees work the order queue, and both press 'Delivered' on an order of 4. Stock drops by 8, two SOLD stock moves are written, and the sewing shop gets two DELIVERED updates.
- **Fix:** Inside the transaction, first claim the transition. Run tx.partnerOrder.updateMany({ where: { shopId, id: o.id, status: { in: allowedFrom } }, data: { status: next, ... } }) and throw a UserError if the count is 0. Only then move stock and queue the message. Alternatively, SELECT ... FOR UPDATE the order row in the transaction and re-check its status.

### M12. Hub quotas are per install, so on a hosted multi-company install one company can use up partner codes and messages for all
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/partners.ts:37` (partners; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round3-tail: link and cross-reference revisions carry shop_ref/shop_name (hub migration 011); StitchBench sends shopRef + shopName on every link and cross-reference change; history byShop is the company, falling back to the install name; the hub smoke test checks it)
- **Problem:** All companies on a hosted StitchBench share one hub install key. The hub limits invites (30/h), redeems (20/h) and partner messages (3000/h) per install (stitchhub partners.mjs:111,134,172). The bench has no per-company quota: makePartnerCode, enterPartnerCode and any action that queues a message (countConsigned and returnConsigned only need requireUser) can be repeated freely. One company's admin, or even a TECH user, can exhaust the budget. Every other company's partner messages then get 429 and back off for up to 30 minutes, and their partner codes fail.
- **Scenario:** A user of company A submits the shelf count form about once a second for an hour. The install's 3000 messages per hour are used up, and company B's job.paid and job.new messages are delayed (429, exponential back-off).
- **Fix:** Add per-company rate limits on the bench for code creation, redemption and message-producing actions. Have the hub key its limits by (install, shopRef) as well as by install.

### M13. The part page's Receive and Set count work on a partner's consigned item, and Add part tells users to do exactly that, so the partner gets paid for the shop's own stock
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/parts.ts:80` (parts-inventory; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The scanner refuses to receive into or use a partner's consigned stock (scan.ts:49, 'it isn't received or used here'), and the spreadsheet import never imports into it (it creates '<number> (own)'). moveStock (part page Receive and Set count) has no consignLinkId check. createPart's duplicate check is case-insensitive and includes consigned items, so entering the same number with an opening quantity gives the error 'X is already in inventory. Open it and use Receive stock to add N.' That sends the user to the consigned item's Receive form. The scan station's 'add as a new part' link on a consigned refusal leads to the same place. saveCounts (count mode scans consigned items through lookup) and Set count also rewrite a consigned item's qtyOnHand without telling the owner. At the register every unit sold from a consigned part becomes a consignSale and a consign.sale message. Neither side caps sold at placed for a positive sale (register.ts:280-283; partners.ts:956-975), so the owner is credited, and the holder is billed on the statement, for goods the holder actually owns.
- **Scenario:** A sewing shop holds 2 units of partner A's item XA-100 on consignment. It buys 4 of its own and types XA-100 (qty 4) into Add part. It gets 'Open it and use Receive stock to add 4', opens XA-100 and receives 4, so qtyOnHand is 6. It sells all 6 at the register: 6 consign.sale units go to partner A and the monthly statement says the shop owes A for 6 units, 4 of which A never placed. Set count or Save counts on consigned stock also silently puts the shelf quantity out of line with the ConsignItem placed/sold/returned figures.
- **Fix:** In moveStock (and saveCounts) refuse, or route to the consignment screens, when p.consignLinkId is set, matching scanCode. In createPart, look for an existing own item with consignLinkId: null. When only a consigned item has the number, create '<number> (own)' as the import does, instead of pointing the user at the consigned item. Hide the Receive and Set count forms on consigned part pages. Optionally cap consignItem.sold at placed - returned for positive sales on both sides.

### M14. Trade-account commission % isn't validated: '10%' saves as NaN, silently pays $0 commission and shows 'NaN' in the form
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/records.ts:104` (bench-workflow; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (started in 5d0d336, finished and checked in round-3 part 2; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** saveCustomer stores `Number(commission)` without checking it. '10%', '10 %' or '1O' become NaN (Postgres float8 accepts it), and negative or >100 values are also accepted. ticketTotals uses `commissionPct ? ... : 0`, and NaN is falsy, so every ticket through that quilt shop silently earns $0 commission on the ticket page, the customer page and the sales log, with no error. `kind` is also cast without a check (an unknown value makes Prisma throw, giving a generic error).
- **Scenario:** Owner adds trade account 'QA Quilt Shop' with commission '10%'. It saves without error; the DB has commissionPct = NaN and the edit form shows 'NaN'. Commission on its collected tickets reads $0 until someone notices.
- **Fix:** Parse with the same strip-% logic users expect, require Number.isFinite and 0 <= pct <= 100, and throw UserError('Commission is a percent between 0 and 100.'). Validate kind against ['RETAIL','TRADE'].

### M15. The tech-role price lock at the register can be bypassed with a 100% line or whole-sale discount; unpriced items sell at $0
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/register.ts:191` (register-money; qa, security, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Round 1 added 'Only an owner or admin can change the price' for TECH users. The server checks unit price only. The UI disables the Price field but leaves 'Disc %' and 'Discount on the whole sale' enabled, and the server accepts any discount (line 179: `disc = Math.max(0, Math.round(Number(l.discountCents ?? 0)))`) and any order discount (line 216) from a TECH. A tech can therefore sell anything for $0, consigned stock included, which also reports a $0 price to the consigning partner.

For an item with no price, the server lets a TECH set one (`part.priceCents != null && ...`), but the client disables the Price field for TECH. The item goes on the sale at $0 while the toast tells the clerk to 'Type the price on the line'.

The same gap exists for repair tickets: removePayment was made admin-only 'like voiding a register sale' (tickets.ts:370), but a TECH can add a negative payment of the same amount through addPayment (tickets.ts:353-357), with the same effect.
- **Scenario:** Sign in as TECH, scan QALAST ($25.00) and set Disc % to 100, then scan QANOPRICE (no price). Total is $0.00, Complete sale is enabled, and sale #1002 is saved. On the ticket page, the TECH enters -25 as the amount, clicks Record payment, and gets 'Payment recorded'.
- **Fix:** In completeSale, when user.role === 'TECH', reject non-zero discountCents and orderDiscountCents, or cap them at a shop-configured maximum. Allow a TECH to set a price only when part.priceCents == null, and enable the client Price field for that case, e.g. `disabled={(!canSell && l.priceWasSet) || ...}`. In addPayment, require an admin for amounts < 0.

### M16. A whole-sale discount is not passed on to consigned items: the consigning partner is credited the undiscounted price
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/register.ts:264` (register-money; qa, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Consigned lines count toward the whole-sale discount base (discountable is true and lineNet > 0), so the customer pays less for them. The consign.sale message and the ConsignSale row, however, use `Math.round(lineNet(l) / l.qty)`, which applies the line discount but not the whole-sale discount. The register sales report's 'Consigned stock sold' uses the same pre-discount amount. The partner terms describe the shop's share as a share of 'what consigned stock sells for', and a line discount is passed through, so the two discount kinds are treated inconsistently.
- **Scenario:** A consigned $24.00 cutter is sold with 50% off the whole sale, so the customer pays $12.00 plus tax. The repair shop is credited 1 x $24.00, and at the 30% share the sewing shop owes $16.80, more than it took in for the item.
- **Fix:** Allocate the whole-sale discount to each eligible line in proportion, as the tax reduction already does, and use the allocated net for consigned lines. Alternatively, state in the terms and UI that whole-sale discounts come out of the shop's own share.

### M17. Scanning an exact part number rings up a different cross-referenced item when the exact one shows 0 on hand (round-1 regression)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/register.ts:60` (register-money; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Round 1 replaced 'exact partNumber first, then numberKeys' with a single ranking: `(p.qtyOnHand > 0 ? 0 : 4) + (p.consignLinkId ? 2 : 0) + (exact(p) ? 0 : 1)`. It was meant for an own item versus a consigned twin, but it applies to every numberKeys match. Being in stock (weight 4) outweighs being the exact number (weight 1), so any other part that lists the scanned number as an alt number wins whenever the exact item's count is 0 or negative. The register then silently charges the other item's price and decrements the wrong item's stock. The register never blocks overselling, so counts at or below 0 are common.
- **Scenario:** QALAST ($25.00 scissors, qtyOnHand 0) and QAGENERIC ($9.99, qtyOnHand 5, altNumbers ['QALAST']). Scanning the barcode QALAST puts 'QAGENERIC Generic scissors $9.99' on the sale. The customer is undercharged $15.01, QAGENERIC's stock drops, and QALAST's does not.
- **Fix:** Rank an exact partNumber match first. Apply the stock/consigned preference only among candidates that are the same item (the own/consigned twins created by import, e.g. same consignItemKey or partNumber vs 'partNumber (own)'). For example: `rank = (exact?0:8) + (qty>0?0:4) + (consigned?2:0)`, special-casing the twin pair.

### M18. Messages the sender accepts but the receiver rejects are silently lost: e.g. a machine intake with a negative pre-approved amount never reaches the repair shop, and the sewing shop is never told
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/repair-jobs.ts:84` (partners; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The sender validates less than the receiver's apply code. takeInMachine stores preApprovedCents = toCents(formData.get('preApproved')) without a range check (the intake field is a free-text inputMode=decimal). newJobFromPartner requires optInt(d.preApprovedCents, 0, MAX_MONEY) (partners.ts:1075/1099), throws PartnerDataError, and pullMessages sets the message aside immediately (partners.ts:550). The same happens with placeConsignment: toCents can be negative ('if (!price)' only rejects 0; partner-orders.ts:184-185), and receiveConsigned requires int(0..MAX_MONEY) (partners.ts:1133). Messages dropped as NEW_WORK after a link ends (partners.ts:692) also vanish without a trace. The sender has already committed local state (a tagged job, or stock taken off its shelf) and its outbox row is marked sent. The only trace is a 'set aside' line on the receiver's Partners page. Every later message about the same record (job.cancel, job.paid, consign.sale...) is then applied against missing state and silently ignored (e.g. 'if (!job?.ticketId) return').
- **Scenario:** The sewing shop takes a machine in and a clerk types '-20' as the pre-approved amount. The customer gets a claim tag and the machine goes on the shelf for pickup, but the repair shop never gets a ticket. The sewing shop's job stays 'Here, waiting for the repair shop' indefinitely and nobody on the sewing side is warned.
- **Fix:** Share one validator module between the sending actions and applyMessage, and reject bad input at the sender: preApproved 0..MAX_MONEY, a consignment price > 0 and <= MAX_MONEY, qty bounds, and so on. Also make rejection visible to the sender: when the receiver sets a message aside or drops it as NEW_WORK, queue a 'msg.rejected' reply {type, id, reason}. The sender should flag the local record (job, order, consignment) and list it under 'Messages that didn't get through'.

### M19. Clearing or lowering 'Next ticket number' rewinds the counter, and on a busy shop every check-in then times out
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/settings.ts:43` (auth-accounts; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (started in 5d0d336, finished and checked in round-3 part 2; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** num() turns a blank field into 0 (Number("") === 0), so `Math.max(1, Math.round(num("nextTicketNumber", ...)))` stores 1. Any lower number, or a stale Settings tab saved later, is also accepted. saveShop rewrites nextTicketNumber on every save, even when only the phone changed. createTicket (tickets.ts:104-106) and partner job intake (partners.ts:1062-1064) then step past used numbers one at a time: an UPDATE plus a SELECT per number, inside an interactive transaction with Prisma's default 5 s timeout. With a few thousand existing tickets the transaction expires and rolls back, the counter never moves, and every later check-in fails the same way.
- **Scenario:** The owner clears 'Next ticket number' (or types 1) and clicks Save shop, and the toast says 'Saved'. With a few thousand tickets (fewer on Railway, where each query is slower), the next 'Check in a machine' runs for more than 5 s and the page is replaced by 'This page couldn't load'. The counter stays at 1, so no tech can check in any machine until someone fixes the setting. With fewer tickets, new tickets instead get low numbers (0001, 0002...) out of order with the paper log.
- **Fix:** In saveShop, treat a blank field as 'keep the current value' and refuse a number at or below the highest existing ticket (`SELECT max(number)`) with a UserError. Only write nextTicketNumber when the field actually changed from the value the form loaded. In both allocation loops, jump straight to max(number)+1 when the counter hits a used number instead of stepping one at a time.

### M20. Shop settings save negative or absurd tax, labor, markup and day values; '8.25%' is silently dropped
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/settings.ts:25` (auth-accounts; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (started in 5d0d336, finished and checked in round-3 part 2; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** num() accepts any finite number, maps blank to 0, and silently falls back to the old value for anything unparseable. toCents() accepts negatives and values beyond the Int column. Nothing checks ranges. The tax rate is copied onto every new ticket (tickets.ts:120) and register sale (register.ts:245), and ticketTotals computes tax as taxBase*taxRatePct/100, so a bad rate produces wrong money on every sale. abandonedAfterDays=0 flags every ready machine as abandoned with the storage-fee notice (page.tsx:40,68). The partner terms already clamp this value to 14-365 (partner-terms.ts:63); shop settings don't.
- **Scenario:** An admin types '-5' for sales tax: every new ticket and register sale subtracts 5% tax. They type '8.25%': the toast says 'Saved' but the old rate is kept. '1e6' is stored as 1,000,000%. Labor '-45' is stored as -$45.00/hr. Labor '99999999' overflows the Int column and the Settings page is replaced by 'This page couldn't load', losing what was typed.
- **Fix:** Use one parser for these fields: blank keeps the current value; strip '%', '$' and ','; reject NaN with a UserError naming the field; enforce ranges (tax 0-25, markup 0-1000, warranty and abandoned days 0-3650 or 14-365, money 0 to $100,000). Make toCents callers reject negatives and anything over 2^31-1.

### M21. Offline mode is incomplete: sharing a note and 'Check for updates' still wait the full 30 s every time when the hub hangs
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/shared.ts:43` (hub-market; qa, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Round 2 promised that once the hub stops answering, anything a person asks of it says 'No connection to the community hub' within seconds. But syncHub() always runs inBackground (src/lib/hub.ts:673), which skips the 15 s offline short-circuit and the 4 s health check. sharePost saves the note locally and then awaits syncHub() (shared.ts:43). With a silent hub, /v1/me waits its full 30 s timeout every time, even when the install already knows the hub is down. The same applies to refreshOrders ('Check for updates', market.ts:247), Settings 'Sync now' (hub.ts:41), and the post-accept and post-cancel sync (market.ts:240), which also waits for any long sync already running. tests/offline.spec.ts covers only partner Sync now and createListing.
- **Scenario:** The shop's internet line goes silent (packets dropped). A tech shares a note: the Share button shows 'Saving...' for 30 s. They share another one a minute later, when the install already shows 'Offline since', and it takes 30 s again. 'Check for updates' on Orders likewise takes 30 s before saying there is no connection.
- **Fix:** In sharePost (and after accept or cancel), don't await the sync. Fire it in the background with void syncHub().catch(...), or await it only when hubOfflineSince() is null. For user-pressed Sync and Check for updates, apply the same quick offline check as reach() before starting a full sync, and add these actions to tests/offline.spec.ts.

### M22. A tech can still cancel a payment and change a closed ticket's bill, which the round-1 fix meant to block
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/tickets.ts:357` (bench-workflow; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (started in 5d0d336, finished and checked in round-3 part 2; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Round 1 (8b3e5f2) made removePayment owner/admin-only ("like voiding a register sale"). It also stopped a TECH from calling removeLine on a closed ticket ("only the owner or an admin can change its bill"). Both controls can be sidestepped. addPayment (line 353) uses requireUser and only rejects 0 (`if (amount == null || amount === 0)`), so any role can enter a negative payment that cancels an earlier one. addLine (line 264) has no closedAt or role check, so a TECH can add a DISCOUNT, a negative FEE or a negative-priced SERVICE/PART line to a COLLECTED ticket. The scan station (src/actions/scan.ts:61) only adds to tickets in OPEN_STATUSES, so the bench form is the odd one out.
- **Scenario:** The owner rings up ticket #51 for $129 by card and sets it to Collected. A TECH opens the ticket and gets the expected errors from 'Remove line' and 'Remove payment'. The same TECH then records a payment of -129.00 CASH and adds a Discount line on the closed ticket, and both are accepted. With a -$129 cash payment plus a $129 discount, the ticket shows a $0 balance and the month's CASH total drops by $129. That hides $129 taken from the drawer, which the admin-only removal was meant to prevent.
- **Fix:** In addPayment, reject amount < 0 unless user.role !== 'TECH', or send refunds and corrections through an admin-only path, the same as removePayment. In addLine, apply the removeLine rule: `if (t.closedAt && user.role === 'TECH') throw new UserError('The ticket is closed: only the owner or an admin can change its bill.')`. Also consider refusing negative SERVICE/PART/FEE prices for TECH.

### M23. addLine accepts Infinity or huge quantities and hours: stock becomes -Infinity then NaN, ticket balance shows $NaN
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/tickets.ts:293` (bench-workflow; security, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (started in 5d0d336, finished and checked in round-3 part 2; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The PART and LABOR branches of addLine parse numbers with Number() and only check the minimum. `Math.max(1, Math.round(rawQty || 1))` passes Infinity through, and LABOR only checks `hours > 0`, which Infinity and 1e300 pass. qty is a Float column, so Postgres stores Infinity. The part's stock is then decremented by Infinity to -Infinity. Removing the line later increments by Infinity, giving NaN, which later +/- adjustments cannot repair. Ticket totals become Infinity/NaN, and those values flow into the sales report and the job.update snapshot sent to partners. The register (register.ts:176) checks `Number.isFinite(qty) && |qty| <= 10000` and the scan station caps at 999; the bench path has no cap. saveCustomer has the same gap (records.ts:309 `Number(commission)`, no finite or 0-100 check), and DISCOUNT has no cap, so a discount larger than the bill makes the invoice negative.
- **Scenario:** A tech (or a mistyped or pasted value) enters qty 'Infinity' for stocked part QA-INF-1 (10 on hand), or labor hours '1e300'. Both are accepted. The part shows -Infinity on hand, and after the line is removed it shows 'NaN on hand'. The ticket page shows 'Balance $NaN', and the low-stock list, sales page and partner bill all carry the bad numbers until someone runs a manual shelf count.
- **Fix:** Validate every numeric input in addLine the same way completeSale does. Require Number.isFinite; cap PART qty at 1..10000 (yards 0.125..10000) and LABOR hours at 0<h<=100 or similar. Bound unit prices (e.g. |price| <= 10_000_000 cents) and cap a DISCOUNT at the current pre-discount total. In saveCustomer, require 0 <= commissionPct <= 100 and a finite number.

### M24. Reopening a closed ticket keeps its old closedAt, warrantyUntil and readyAt, which skews the sales log, the warranty and the storage-fee warning
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/tickets.ts:146` (bench-workflow; code, qa, security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (started in 5d0d336, finished and checked in round-3 part 2; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** setStatus only ever sets readyAt, closedAt and warrantyUntil, keeping the first value (`t.readyAt ?? now`, `t.closedAt ?? now`, `!t.warrantyUntil`). Moving a ticket back to an open status (mis-click, customer returns it, repair failed) never clears them. Consequences: (1) the Sales log counts COLLECTED tickets by closedAt (sales/page.tsx:18), so a ticket collected in September after a stray COLLECTED or ABANDONED in August is billed to August; (2) the warranty runs from the first, wrong collection; (3) the invoice prints 'Returned <date>' while the machine is on the bench; (4) removeLine still treats the reopened ticket as closed, so techs can't fix its bill; (5) the dashboard storage-fee 'overdue' warning uses the first readyAt after the machine went back on the bench.
- **Scenario:** Owner sets ticket #7000 to COLLECTED by mistake and then back to ON_BENCH. The DB still has closedAt and warrantyUntil set, the invoice header says 'Returned Sep 26, 2026', and a TECH trying to remove a line gets 'The ticket is closed: only the owner or an admin can change its bill.'
- **Fix:** In setStatus, when the new status is in OPEN_STATUSES clear closedAt (and warrantyUntil if it was set by that close). When leaving the READY statuses for ON_BENCH, WAITING_* or IN_TRUCK, clear readyAt. On a later close, set closedAt and warrantyUntil fresh. Optionally keep a status history (events) for the audit trail.

### M25. Saving a ticket's text overwrites every field from a stale form: a second user's approved amount and notes (or a partner shop's relayed approval) are silently erased
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/tickets.ts:165` (bench-workflow; qa, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (started in 5d0d336, finished and checked in round-3 part 2; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** saveTicketText writes all eight fields (serviceRequested, problem, diagnosis, workDone, notes, preApprovedCents, approvedCents, techId) from whatever the browser submits, with no version or updatedAt check. Two people with the same ticket open overwrite each other without any warning. The partner flow makes this likely: when the sewing shop answers an estimate, the job.reply handler (partners.ts ~800) writes ticket.approvedCents and appends a line to ticket.notes. A tech who has the ticket open and then clicks Save sends the old notes and a blank approved amount, which erases both.
- **Scenario:** The tech opens ticket #0054 and starts typing a diagnosis. Meanwhile the owner enters 'Customer approved ($) 250' and the note 'Customer called 2pm: OK up to $250, replace bobbin case only', and saves. The tech then saves the diagnosis and gets a 'Saved' toast. The approved amount and the note are now gone (both NULL).
- **Fix:** Send the ticket's updatedAt with the form and update with `where: { id, updatedAt }` (optimistic concurrency). If the ticket changed, return a UserError such as 'Someone else changed this ticket, reload'. At minimum, write only the fields that changed. Keep partner-relayed notes in a separate field or log that this form does not submit.

### M26. A tech can still change a closed ticket's bill by adding discount, negative-fee or negative-price lines (round-1 lock only covers removing lines)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/tickets.ts:266` (bench-workflow; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (started in 5d0d336, finished and checked in round-3 part 2; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Round 1 (8b3e5f2) made removeLine refuse TECH users on a closed ticket ("The ticket is closed: only the owner or an admin can change its bill."). addLine has no such check: after ownTicket() it goes straight to creating the line. A TECH can therefore lower a closed and fully paid bill however they like, with a DISCOUNT line, a FEE with a negative amount, or a SERVICE/PART with a negative price (toCents accepts negatives). That is the same result the lock is meant to prevent. addPayment and saveTicketText have no closed check either.
- **Scenario:** The owner bills a Bernina (service $159, tax 8.25%), takes $172.12 by card and sets the ticket to COLLECTED (closedAt is set). A TECH opens it. Removing the service line is refused. Adding the fee/discount form with Discount of $100 succeeds, and the bill drops to $63.87 total on a closed ticket. The sales log's collected service and tax figures change with it.
- **Fix:** Put the closed-ticket rule in one helper, e.g. `assertBillEditable(ticket, user)`, and call it from addLine, removeLine and addPayment (and from the approved/pre-approved amounts in saveTicketText if those count as the bill). Reject negative prices for SERVICE/PART/FEE in addLine: discounts should only be possible through the DISCOUNT kind.

### M27. Timing and tension readings are silently dropped: negative mm specs (189 settings), '85 g' and '22,5' are discarded, and an existing value is wiped, with a 'saved' toast
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/tickets.ts:193` (bench-workflow; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (started in 5d0d336, finished and checked in round-3 part 2; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** grams() returns null for anything that isn't a plain non-negative number. saveMeasurements uses it for mm/degree timing readings and skips those fields. saveTension writes null over the stored value. Neither returns an error, so the UI says 'Timing readings saved' / 'Readings saved'. 189 of 13,505 measurable factory settings have negative ranges (e.g. Bernina B 570 QE 'Hook drive belt tensioner position' -0.5 to 0 mm, Melco X home -0.4 to 0.4 mm), so valid readings for them can never be recorded. A tech who types the unit ('85 g', which matches the 'g' placeholder) or a decimal comma loses the reading, including one saved earlier.
- **Scenario:** On a Bernina B 570 QE ticket the tech enters -0.3 as found and -0.2 as left for 'Hook drive belt tensioner position' and clicks Save timing readings. The toast says 'Timing readings saved', but after a reload both fields are empty (measurements = {}). Upper-as-found was saved as 85, then re-entered as '85 g' and saved. 'Readings saved' appears and upperFoundG is now NULL.
- **Fix:** Use a separate parser for mm/deg readings that allows negatives. Strip a trailing unit and accept a decimal comma. Return a UserError that names the field when a non-empty value can't be parsed, instead of saving null or skipping it.

### M28. Any user (including techs, on any company of a shared install) can remove a community cross-reference entry, and StitchBench has no way to see or restore removed entries
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/xref.ts:71` (hub-commerce; qa, security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** removeCrossRef needs only requireUser(). It sends DELETE /v1/xparts/:id and deletes the local copy. Every other install deletes its copy on the next sync (saveXParts removes deleted parts). The hub keeps the row and has POST /v1/xparts/:id/restore, but the app never calls it. /xref/<id> of a removed entry is 404, so its History and 'Roll back' buttons can't be reached. The file header and README promise 'every change keeps the whole entry... any version can be restored', but in practice one click by any user removes a community part for all shops, recoverable only by someone with API access.
- **Scenario:** A TECH user on a hosted install opens Cross-reference > entry > Remove > 'Remove for everyone?'. The part disappears from every shop, and /xref/<id> answers 404 on every install. The hub row is deleted=true with revisions 'added' then 'removed', and there is no UI to restore it.
- **Fix:** Keep removed entries locally (soft-deleted, hidden from search) and add a 'Recently removed' list on /xref with a Restore button (POST /v1/xparts/:id/restore). Consider requiring admin/owner for Remove and Merge, and showing who removed it.

### M29. 'Add it to the cross-reference' publishes the part's private notes to every hub member, along with local '(own)' numbers and free-text 'fits' marked 'Fitted by a tech'
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/xref.ts:110` (parts-inventory; qa, security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** addPartToCrossRef sends notes: p.notes. That is the shop's internal Notes field on the part form, and nothing on the form says it may be shared. It goes to the community hub, where every member reads it in the entry and its history (and cannot delete revisions). Consigned items carry notes such as 'On consignment from <partner>'. The partNumber is sent unchanged, so the app's own local suffixes ('X (own)', 'X (<Partner name>)') become community part numbers. Each comma-separated fragment of the free-text 'Fits models' field is published as a fit with evidence 'tested' ('Fitted by a tech', the strongest evidence), under brand 'Generic' when the part has no brand. Elsewhere the app promises that only part numbers and counts are shared (most-used page footer).
- **Scenario:** Create part 'QAPI7K3X-77 (own)' with Notes 'PRIVATE: we pay $1.10 from Joe Smith 555-0100; Mrs Jones owes us' and Fits 'most Janome, ask Bob'. On its page click 'Add it to the cross-reference'. Another hub member (a fresh install key) reading GET /v1/xparts/<id>/history receives notes 'PRIVATE: we pay $1.10 from Joe Smith 555-0100; Mrs Jones owes us', number 'QAPI7K3X-77 (own)', and fits [('ask Bob','tested'), ('most Janome','tested')].
- **Fix:** Don't send p.notes; send notes: null or let the user review the entry first by prefilling the XrefForm on /xref instead of posting directly. Strip a trailing ' (own)' or ' (<suffix>)' from partNumber, and send the base number the alt list holds. Send fits with evidence 'listed' or not at all, and require a real brand.

### M30. Cross-reference edits overwrite the whole entry from a stale local copy (lost updates, including the supplier scan's where-to-buy links)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/xref.ts:40` (hub-commerce; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** saveCrossRef rebuilds the entry from the install's local XPart copy (form text plus prev.fits/prev.sources) and PUTs it. PUT /v1/xparts/:id (stitchhub src/parts.mjs:240-258) replaces numbers, fits and sources with no version or updatedAt check. The local copy is refreshed only every 10 minutes (instrumentation.ts:12), or later when offline. Any hub-side change made after that sync is silently wiped by the next edit from the install. This includes other shops' edits, admin imports and the admin supplier scan (/v1/admin/xparts/sources/import).
- **Scenario:** The admin runs the supplier scan, which attaches 'Sewing Parts Online, $12.99' to Janome HK-…. Within the next 10 minutes a tech whose install hasn't synced yet corrects the part name. The PUT carries the pre-scan sources ([]), and the scan's link is deleted.
- **Fix:** Send the base version, for example `baseUpdatedAt: prev.updatedAt` or the id of the last revision seen. The hub should answer 409 when the entry changed since then, and StitchBench should reload the entry and show the conflict. Alternatively, fetch the current entry from the hub right before building the PUT, and send only changed fields and sources.

### M31. Invoices 'Unpaid' tab and 'Still owed' total silently leave out every unpaid ticket older than the latest 500
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/invoices/page.tsx:40` (bench-workflow; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (started in 5d0d336, finished and checked in round-3 part 2; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The page loads the 500 highest-numbered tickets that have lines (`orderBy: { number: "desc" }, take: 500`) and only then filters by balance in memory. Once a shop has more than 500 billed tickets, which takes a few months for a busy bench, older unpaid invoices disappear from the Unpaid tab. The 'Still owed' and 'Billed' totals are understated, and the page can say 'Nothing owed. Every invoice is paid.' Old debts such as abandoned machines and trade accounts that pay late are exactly the ones this view is for. Nothing on the page says the list is truncated.
- **Scenario:** Shop has 506 billed tickets. #1 is unpaid ($100) and #2-#506 are paid. /invoices?show=unpaid shows 'Invoices shown 0, Still owed $0.00' and 'Nothing owed. Every invoice is paid.' Searching q=1 finds the $100 unpaid invoice.
- **Fix:** Do the balance filter in SQL, e.g. a raw query or a view that computes line and payment totals per ticket and filters balance > 0 before LIMIT. Or keep an owed/paid flag on Ticket, updated by addLine, removeLine and addPayment. Paginate instead of silently cutting at 500, compute 'Still owed' with an aggregate over all tickets, and show 'latest N' when the list is limited.

### M32. The parts list stops at 1,000 rows before the low-stock filter and the value total, so low-stock items and the shelf value go missing for larger inventories
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/parts/page.tsx:60` (parts-inventory; security, qa, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** db.part.findMany({ take: 1000, orderBy: partNumber }) runs first. Only after that are 'Low stock only' (line 62) and the value total (line 63) computed in JS. A shop with more than 1,000 items (easy for a sewing shop: the import takes 5,000 rows at a time) gets a list that silently leaves out low-stock items after the 1,000th alphabetically, a header item count stuck at 1,000, and an understated '$ on the shelf at cost'. Nothing says the list is truncated.
- **Scenario:** The shop has 1,003 items. The out-of-stock item 'ZZZ-LOW-1' (qty 0, reorder at 2) never appears under 'Low stock only', and the header reads '1000 items · $1000.00'.
- **Fix:** Do the low-stock filter in SQL, e.g. a raw `WHERE "qtyOnHand" <= "reorderAt"` or a Prisma field-reference comparison. Compute the count and the value with an aggregate query over all of the shop's parts. Page the list, or show 'showing 1,000 of N'.

### M33. Receipts, register sales times, sales log dates and the sales CSV use the server's time zone (UTC on hosted), not the shop's
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/register/sales/[id]/page.tsx:42` (register-money; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Round 1 made the report ranges use the shop's zone (monthRange, todayIn), but the displayed dates and times are still formatted in the server's zone. Receipt time (line 42) and the void time (line 30) call toLocaleString without timeZone. So do the register sales list (register/sales/page.tsx:132), the sales-log header (sales/page.tsx:49; `end - 1ms` is the NEXT day in UTC for any US zone) and its payment dates (:122), the register-sales header (register/sales/page.tsx:49), and the price list 'Effective' date (price-list/page.tsx:49). The sales CSV date column is `p.paidAt.toISOString().slice(0,10)` (api/sales/route.ts:19), a UTC date, so an evening payment is filed under the next day, or the next month at month end.
- **Scenario:** Hosted server (TZ=UTC), shop in America/New_York, 1:20 PM EDT. The printed receipt says '9/26/26, 5:20 PM'. /sales?from=2026-09-01&to=2026-09-25 shows the header 'Sep 1, 2026 to Sep 26, 2026'. A payment taken at 9 PM EDT on Sep 30 appears in the September CSV dated 2026-10-01.
- **Fix:** Pass the shop's zone everywhere a date is shown: add fmtDate(d, tz) and fmtDateTime(d, tz) helpers that set `timeZone`, select shop.timeZone on the receipt page (sale.shop is already loaded), and in /api/sales use `iso(zonedParts(p.paidAt, tz))` for the date column. For the range headers, format fromStr/toStr directly rather than instants.

### M34. A second estimate on a partner repair never reaches the sewing shop: 'approval' is never reset, so the answer form and to-do stay hidden
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/repairs/[id]/page.tsx:44` (bench-workflow; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The sewing shop's 'needs the customer's OK' form shows only when `j.status === "WAITING_CUSTOMER_OK" && !j.approval`, and the dashboard PartnerTodo filters `approval: null`. The approval is set once by answerEstimate (or job.reply on the repair side) and never cleared. The job.update handler (src/lib/partners.ts:754-789) updates status, lines and totals but not approval or approvedCents. If the repair shop needs the customer's OK again (more damage found after the first approval, or a cheaper offer after a decline) and sets the ticket back to 'Waiting on customer OK', the retail job goes to WAITING_CUSTOMER_OK but still carries the old APPROVED or DECLINED. The sewing shop is never prompted and sees only the stale 'Customer approved up to $X' pill. The repair side's ticket page also keeps showing the old approval pill.
- **Scenario:** Round 1: customer approves up to $150. The tech finds a cracked hook, adds $120 of parts and sets 'Waiting on customer OK' again. On the sewing shop, /repairs/<id> shows no approve or decline form, the dashboard to-do doesn't list it, and the job sits in 'Needs the customer's OK' with a pill saying it was already approved. The repair stalls, or the tech goes ahead on the stale approval.
- **Fix:** When a job moves into WAITING_CUSTOMER_OK from another status, clear approval and approvedCents on both sides: in the retail job.update handler when status changes to WAITING_CUSTOMER_OK, and on the repair side in setStatus or pushJobUpdates for the job's RepairJob. Or record the time of the approval and compare it with the time of the latest WAITING_CUSTOMER_OK event, showing the form when the request is newer.

### M35. Sales log totals leave out closed tickets returned unrepaired, so their taxable diagnosis fees and tax are missing
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/sales/page.tsx:18` (register-money; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Service, Labor, Parts, 'Sales tax' and commission on /sales come only from tickets with status COLLECTED closed in the range. setStatus also closes RETURNED_UNREPAIRED (and ABANDONED) tickets. The default service menu has 'Diagnosis & estimate' at $49, and SERVICE lines are taxable by default (TicketLine.taxable @default(true); addLine SERVICE doesn't override it). So a declined estimate billed and paid on a returned-unrepaired machine shows up in 'Money taken' but not in 'Sales tax' or 'Service'. The tax figure the owner files from is understated.
- **Scenario:** Customer declines the estimate. The ticket bills 'Diagnosis & estimate' $49 + 6% tax = $51.94 and is paid, then marked RETURNED_UNREPAIRED. On /sales, 'Money taken' includes $51.94, but 'Sales tax' omits the $2.94 and 'Service (collected)' omits the $49.
- **Fix:** Select tickets by `closedAt` in range with `status: { in: ["COLLECTED", "RETURNED_UNREPAIRED", "ABANDONED"] }` (or any status whose ticket has lines), and rename the tiles from '(collected)' to '(closed)'. Note separately: reopened tickets keep their old closedAt (DB shows ON_BENCH tickets with closedAt set), so their work is dated to the first close.

### M36. Trade-account commission is recomputed from today's percentage: editing an account rewrites 'commission owed' for every past month
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/sales/page.tsx:37` (gap; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** For machines that come in through a trade account (quilt shop or dealer), commission is worked out at display time as `service * viaAccount.commissionPct`, using the account's current percentage. Nothing is stored on the ticket when it closes. Changing the percentage, or switching the account to Retail (which nulls commissionPct), silently changes the commission owed on every closed ticket in every past month, on the Sales log and on the customer page. Round 1 froze partner-shop commissions at payment (jobSnapshot keeps commissionCents once paid), but the trade-account path was left live. The reported '10%' NaN finding is a different root cause (parsing).
