- **Scenario:** Reproduced: create trade account 'Quilt Shop QA' at 10%, check in a machine via it, add a $100 service, set Collected. /sales shows Shop commission owed $10.00. Edit the account to 25%: /sales for the same period now shows $25.00 for the already-closed ticket. Expected: $10.00 stays for that ticket; the new rate applies only to later tickets.
- **Fix:** Store the trade commission percentage (or cents) on the ticket when it is created or closed, as is done for partner jobs, and use the stored value in reports. Only open tickets should follow an edited account.

### M37. 'Your data' export can't take a repair shop's records with it: no tickets, ticket lines, machines or service history, and 'Repair payments' exports only the current month
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/api/export/[what]/route.ts:75` (gap; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Settings -> Your data promises 'This company's own records as spreadsheets (CSV), to keep or to take to another system', and the route's header comment says 'This is what a shop takes with it if it moves'. The only exports are customers, inventory, register-sales and repairs (RepairJob, i.e. the partner-shop jobs). A repair bench's core records (tickets with lines, diagnosis and work done, machines and serials, readings, payments across all dates) can't be exported: /api/export/tickets and /api/export/machines return 404 'Unknown export.'. The 'Repair payments (sales log)' button links to /api/sales with no dates, and monthRange defaults to the 1st of this month through today, so it silently exports only this month's payments. The reported export finding is about missing columns in the register-sales CSV, not about these missing records.
- **Scenario:** Signed in as a shop owner: GET /api/export/tickets -> 404 'Unknown export.'; GET /api/export/machines -> 404. Pressing Settings -> Your data -> 'Repair payments (sales log)' downloads sales_2026-09-01_to_2026-09-27.csv, only September, although the button suggests the whole payments log. A shop moving to its own install or another system loses its whole ticket and machine history.
- **Fix:** Add tickets (one row per line, plus status/dates/diagnosis/work done/tech), machines, payments (all dates) and model notes exports. Make the Settings 'Repair payments' link export all dates (e.g. from=0001-01-01) or label it 'this month'. Consider one zip of every CSV.

### M38. FigureImage is a table shared by all shops: an owner or admin of any company can overwrite the factory-setting figures every other company sees (and any tech can plant the first copy)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/api/figures/route.ts:33` (data-model; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** FigureImage (prisma/schema.prisma:535) has no shopId; its id is sha1(sourcePath|page|box). POST /api/figures only checks that some FactorySetting has that sourcePath and figurePage. It then upserts: any role creates the first copy, and any non-TECH user of any company replaces the image bytes (`update: user.role === "TECH" ? {} : { width, height, data }`). On the hosted multi-shop app, one company can therefore swap the timing/adjustment diagrams every other company's techs look at. Three things make the damage stick. Once a figure is stored, the UI shows it through 'Show figure' and never redraws or re-uploads it (manual-figure.tsx:131 and 147-163). /api/figures/[id] is served with `Cache-Control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable`, so browsers keep a bad copy for a year. The upload is also pushed to the hub (shareFigure), where the first write wins. Other problems in the same handler: `box` is never matched against the setting's figureBox (any number of extra 1.5 MB rows can be stored per known page), the JPEG bytes are not checked, and a non-array box raises a 500.
- **Scenario:** Shop A (a self-signed-up company on a hosted install, or any tenant) posts a wrong or offensive JPEG for sourcePath 'Sewing\Baby-Lock\...\BL-BL137A-ServiceManual.pdf', page 202, box [0,0.016,1,0.589]. Every other company's 'Show figure' for that hook-timing setting now displays shop A's image, and techs adjust machines from the wrong diagram.
- **Fix:** Accept only the exact figure a setting defines: look up the FactorySetting by sourcePath + figurePage and require figureId(sourcePath, page, setting.figureBox) === id. On an install with more than one company, allow replacing an existing figure only for isInstallAdmin/platform admin, or store figures per shop (add shopId and read own-then-shared). Check the JPEG magic bytes. Drop `immutable`, or version the URL, so a corrected figure reaches browsers.

### M39. Upload size pre-checks are bypassed by chunked requests; the server buffers the whole body in memory (300 MB grew RSS from 430 MB to 1.2 GB)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/api/library/file/route.ts:41` (files-library-desktop; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** /api/library/file (150 MB limit) and /api/figures (2 MB limit) refuse large uploads only by reading the Content-Length header. They then call `req.formData()`, which reads the whole body into memory. A request sent with `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` has no Content-Length, so `Number(null ?? 0)` is 0 and the check passes. Next's route handlers impose no body limit of their own (the 16 MB serverActions.bodySizeLimit applies only to server actions). The file.size checks run only after the body has been buffered. src/app/api/photos/route.ts:12 has the same pattern (outside my area). On the Windows program any signed-in device on the LAN can do this. On hosted, /api/figures and /api/photos are reachable by any user of any tenant, and memory runs out for the whole container.
- **Scenario:** A signed-in user (any role) streams a few GB of chunked multipart to /api/library/file on the shop PC (or /api/figures on Railway). Node buffers it at about 2.5x the body size and the server runs out of memory. On the shop PC every device loses the app until main.mjs restarts it. On hosted, every company is down.
- **Fix:** Don't rely on Content-Length. Refuse requests that have no Content-Length (or Transfer-Encoding: chunked) on these routes, or stream-parse the body with a hard byte cap (e.g. busboy with limits.fileSize/fieldSize, or wrap req.body in a counting TransformStream that aborts past the limit) before calling formData(). Apply the same to /api/photos. On the Windows program, also cap the size and count of _inbox uploads per user.

### M40. Photo upload reads a chunked body of any size into memory (the size check only looks at the Content-Length header)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/api/photos/route.ts:12` (bench-workflow; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (started in 5d0d336, finished and checked in round-3 part 2; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The route's early 413 check relies on the Content-Length header (`Number(req.headers.get('content-length') ?? 0) > MAX_BYTES + 100_000`). A request sent with Transfer-Encoding: chunked has no Content-Length, so it passes. `await req.formData()` then reads the whole body into memory before `file.size > MAX_BYTES` is checked. Route handlers have no framework body limit (the 16 MB serverActions.bodySizeLimit does not apply to them). Any signed-in user can use this, and on a hosted install with ALLOW_SIGNUP anyone who signs up can, to exhaust the memory of the one server process every shop shares. /api/library/file/route.ts:41 and /api/figures/route.ts:10 use the same header-only pattern (outside this review's scope).
- **Scenario:** A signed-in user streams a 200 MB multipart body with chunked encoding to POST /api/photos. The server reads all of it and only then returns 413. In the test the process RSS rose from 318 MB to 1 GB for one request, so a few parallel requests would get a Railway container OOM-killed and take every shop offline.
- **Fix:** Do not trust Content-Length alone. Reject requests without Content-Length (411) or count bytes while reading req.body (a ReadableStream reader that aborts past MAX_BYTES + overhead) before parsing multipart. Put the same limited reader in a shared helper for /api/library/file and /api/figures. A per-shop photo quota would also help.

### M41. Receipts, sales lists and the sales CSV use the server's clock (UTC on hosted), not the shop's time zone
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/api/sales/route.ts:19` (register-money; security, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Round 1 made report ranges start at the shop's midnight (monthRange/zonedMidnight), but the dates and times shown or exported for each row still use the server's local zone, which is UTC on Railway. /api/sales writes `p.paidAt.toISOString().slice(0, 10)`, the UTC date. The receipt (register/sales/[id]/page.tsx:42, and :30 for the void time), the register-sales Time column (register/sales/page.tsx:132) and the Sales log dates (sales/page.tsx:122, via fmtDate) all call toLocaleString/toLocaleDateString without timeZone. The page headers `fmtDate(start)` (register/sales/page.tsx:49, sales/page.tsx:49) show the day before for any shop east of UTC, because the shop's midnight is the previous day in UTC. The register-sales export also writes createdAt as a UTC ISO timestamp.
- **Scenario:** A New York shop takes an $85 cash payment at 9:30 PM on Sep 25. GET /api/sales?from=2026-09-25&to=2026-09-25 correctly includes it, but its date column says 2026-09-26, so a month-end CSV lists next-month dates and spreadsheet totals by date don't match the page. A register sale at 1:11 PM EDT prints "9/26/26, 5:11 PM" on the customer's receipt, and after 8 PM EDT receipts show tomorrow's date.
- **Fix:** Pass the shop's timeZone to every date and time formatter on these pages (toLocaleString("en-US", { timeZone: shop.timeZone, ... })), for example by giving fmtDate a timeZone parameter. In /api/sales, write the date as todayIn-style zoned parts: iso(zonedParts(p.paidAt, tz)). Format the header dates from fromStr/toStr instead of from the instants.

### M42. One labels GET can render 9,000 barcodes (24 MB of HTML, about 14 s of server time)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/labels/page.tsx:18` (parts-inventory; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The labels page takes up to 300 ids and up to 30 copies, and calls bwip-js toSVG once per label instead of once per part. Any signed-in user can make the server spend about 14 s and send 24 MB per request, and repeat it. (Checked: the SVG never contains the part-number text, includetext:false, so the dangerouslySetInnerHTML at :98 is not an XSS.)
- **Scenario:** A signed-in user loops GET /labels?format=thermal&copies=30&id=<300 ids> and keeps a CPU core busy on a small hosted instance.
- **Fix:** Render each part's SVG once and reuse it for its copies, and cap the total labels per page (e.g. 600).

### M43. Public sign-up can claim an email reserved for the platform admin (the round-1 fix only covered addUser)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/signup/page.tsx:36` (data-model; security, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Platform-admin rights come only from an email match (isPlatformAdmin(email) against PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS), and User.email is unique across the whole install. Round 1 added a guard to addUser (settings.ts:105-106, "The StitchBench administrator's address carries install-wide rights: nobody else may take it for a login"). The public /signup action, and createShopWithOwner (src/lib/shops.ts:36-64) that it calls, never make that check. So when ALLOW_SIGNUP=true, anyone can sign up with a listed address that has no account yet and become platform admin. Session checks then treat them as admin: requirePlatformAdmin (session.ts:43-47), requireInstallAdmin (session.ts:53-58), isPlatformAdmin in deleteSharedPost and removeHubPost.
- **Scenario:** Hosted install with ALLOW_SIGNUP=true and PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS=real-admin@x,boss@x, where boss@x has not signed in yet (a second admin, or a typo'd or not-yet-seeded address). An anonymous visitor opens /signup, enters boss@x and any shop name, and is signed in as platform admin. From there they can: read every company with its owner's name and email (/companies); create companies; join or leave the community hub for the whole install (joinCommunityHub/leaveCommunityHub use requireInstallAdmin), which points every shop's hub trust (partner links and messages, manual links, images) at an attacker hub; and delete any shop's shared notes.
- **Fix:** Put the reservation check inside createShopWithOwner so every path gets it (signup, createCompany, the partners set-up form). Refuse when isPlatformAdmin(email) unless the caller is already a platform admin, for example with an opts.allowReserved flag set only by the platform-admin actions. Better still, stop deriving admin rights from an unverified email: add a User.platformAdmin boolean that the seed or the operator sets, and have isPlatformAdmin read the DB row.

### M44. Login pacing is checked before the failure is recorded, so a burst of parallel wrong passwords skips pacing entirely
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/auth.ts:50` (auth-accounts; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: parallel bursts were already counted; the failure window now slides. Left: 20 queued tries still refuse the right password too (needs a per-address design without TRUST_PROXY)
- **Problem:** paced() reads the per-account failure count when a request arrives (`const n = count(`e:${email}`); if (n < 5) return fn();`). The count only goes up in fail(), after the bcrypt compare finishes (bcryptjs, cost 10, async). Every request that arrives while fewer than 5 failures are recorded runs straight away and in parallel, however many there are. The per-address limit (`if (ip && count(`i:${ip}`) >= 50) return null`, line 79) has the same check-then-act gap. The window also resets 15 minutes after the first failure (count() compares against `since`), so the same burst works again every window. The round-2 fix ('pacing per account instead of a lockout') is therefore incomplete: the number of guesses per window is limited by server CPU, not by the policy of 5 free tries followed by growing waits.
- **Scenario:** Hosted app, target account owner@shop. Many sign-in attempts for that email arrive at once through /api/auth/callback/credentials or the login action. All of them see n=0 because none has recorded a failure yet, so all are checked right away instead of 5 followed by 1 s, 2 s, 4 s ... 30 s waits. Without TRUST_PROXY there is no per-address cap at all, and with it the cap has the same gap.
- **Fix:** Reserve the slot before doing the work: count the attempt at entry (for example an in-flight counter per email and per address, added to count() in the decision) and release it on success. Or send every attempt for an email through the per-email queue, not just those after 5 failures, and cap in-flight attempts per email at 1. Use a sliding or decaying window instead of a reset 15 minutes after the first failure.

### M45. Accounts are created with emails that sign-in's zod .email() rejects: they can never sign in, and the Windows program's first run gets stuck
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/auth.ts:9` (auth-accounts; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (a5964f6)
- **Problem:** Sign-in validates with zod 4's strict `z.string().email()`, which needs a dotted TLD and a narrow local-part charset. Account creation does no format check: signup/page.tsx:33, createShopWithOwner (shops.ts:40), addUser (settings.ts:100) and createCompany only trim and lowercase, and the forms use `<input type=email>`, whose browser rule accepts `sue@bench`, `owner@myshop`, `j!m@x.com` and `a..b@x.com`. Such accounts are created with a success message but authorize() returns null for them every time. In the sign-up action, the automatic `signIn(...)` after createShopWithOwner then throws CredentialsSignin, which is not caught, so the user sees an error page.
- **Scenario:** Windows program first run: the owner types a local-looking login such as owner@myshop (a shop PC needs no real mailbox). The company and owner are created and the sign-up page errors. Every sign-in says the password is wrong. Signing up again is refused because firstOnly sees one company (shops.ts: 'This program already has its company'), and the desktop program has no password or account reset tool, so the install is stuck until someone edits the database. The same happens to an admin who adds a tech as sue@bench under Settings -> People.
- **Fix:** Use one email normalizer and validator everywhere (create and sign-in), for example a shared `normalizeEmail()` that trims, lowercases and requires a single '@' with non-empty sides, and in authorize use `z.string().min(3).max(254)` rather than `.email()`. At creation, reject what the sign-in path would reject, with a UserError. In signup, catch AuthError from signIn and redirect to /login instead of showing an error page.

### M46. Any failed server action (a server error or a dropped connection) replaces the whole page with 'This page couldn't load', and whatever was typed is lost
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/action-form.tsx:45` (gap; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** ActionForm, ActionButton (action-form.tsx:45, 107), StatusSelect and Checklist (ticket-controls.tsx:23, 77) call the server action inside startTransition with no try/catch. The Next 16 docs (01-app/01-getting-started/10-error-handling.md) say unhandled errors inside startTransition bubble to the nearest error boundary. The app has no error.tsx or global-error.tsx, so the default boundary replaces the entire page. safe() only turns UserError into {error}, so every other failure reaches this path. That includes the many generic 500s already reported (enum/int overflow, P2002 races), a DB hiccup, and a fetch failure while the Windows program restarts its server or the LAN drops.
- **Scenario:** A tech has typed a long diagnosis on a ticket, then adds a fee while the shop PC's server is restarting (or the Wi-Fi drops). The page turns into 'This page couldn't load / Reload' and the unsaved diagnosis is gone. Likewise, any server-side error on the check-in form wipes the whole intake form instead of showing a toast.
- **Fix:** Wrap the awaited action in try/catch inside ActionForm, ActionButton, StatusSelect and Checklist, and show a toast ('Couldn't save: check the connection and try again') while keeping the form state. Also add app/(app)/error.tsx and app/global-error.tsx so render errors keep the navigation.

### M47. Import stays armed after a successful import: pressing it again receives every quantity a second time
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/import-items.tsx:230` (parts-inventory; qa, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** After success the pasted text, the preview and the enabled 'Import N items' button all stay on screen, and 'Add the quantities to stock (received)' is checked by default. Nothing marks the batch as done. The round-2 comment in inventory-import.ts:81 names this exact risk ('pressing Import again would receive twice') but only guards the partial-failure case. A second press (checking whether it worked, or pressing Import again after re-reading the preview) silently doubles on-hand for every row and writes a second set of RECEIVED stock moves. Nothing can undo an import.
- **Scenario:** Paste 4 rows (QTY-C qty 24) > Import 4 items > 'Imported: 4 added, 0 updated, 0 skipped'. The button is still enabled. Click it again > 'Imported: 0 added, 4 updated, 0 skipped'. QTY-C now has 48 on hand and two 'Imported' RECEIVED moves. Expected: after success the button is disabled or the form is cleared (for example 'Imported. Paste another sheet'), or the server refuses a repeated batch id.
- **Fix:** After a successful import, clear the text or keep the button disabled until the pasted text changes. Also send a client batch id (crypto.randomUUID() fixed per pasted text) and record it (for example in StockMove.note or a small ImportBatch table) so the server refuses a batch it has already applied.

### M48. Complete sale fails silently on any server error, and nothing stops a duplicate sale when the cashier retries
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/register.tsx:121` (register-money; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** finish() uses try/finally with no catch. completeSale returns { error } only for UserError; everything else rejects the promise, and the button just switches back to "Complete sale" with no toast. Examples: a Prisma error such as an Int overflow or P2025 when a part is deleted mid-sale, a transaction timeout, the hosted server being unreachable, or a lost response. The cart is still there, so the cashier clicks again. completeSale has no idempotency key, so if the first call committed and only the response was lost, the retry creates a second sale, decrements stock twice and queues a second consign.sale to the partner under new line ids. The unit price also has no upper bound, so a typo such as 30000000 overflows the Int columns and hits this silent path.
- **Scenario:** Owner adds a misc item priced 30000000 with a Card tender and clicks Complete sale. The server logs 'Value out of range ... "3000000000" is out of range for type integer'. The UI shows no toast and no status; the button is back to 'Complete sale' with the cart unchanged. The same silent result occurs when the network drops during a sale.
- **Fix:** Add `catch { toast.error("The sale wasn't saved (no connection or a server problem). Check Register sales before ringing it again.") }` in finish(), and similar handling in lookup and pickupJobs. Generate a client key (crypto.randomUUID()) per cart, send it with completeSale, store it on Sale with @@unique([shopId, clientKey]), and on a unique conflict return the existing sale. Bound unitPriceCents and qty*unit to a sane maximum (e.g. MAX_MONEY) with a UserError.

### M49. A handheld scanner types into whatever field has focus, so scanned items are silently lost or change another line's discount or yardage
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/register.tsx:337` (register-money; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The scan box only gets focus when the page loads, after a search result or misc item is added, and after a sale. After the clerk edits a line's Qty, Price or Disc % field, or clicks a tender button, focus stays there. A keyboard-wedge scanner types its code and presses Enter into the focused element. In the Qty field (type=number) the digits are appended to the yardage. In Disc % they are clamped to 100, so the line becomes free. On a focused tender button, Enter clicks it again and adds a tender for the balance. In every case the scanned item is never added. README/INSTALL promise that any scanner that 'types the code and presses Enter works in the register's scan box', and 'scan the bolt, type the yardage, scan the next item' is the core fabric flow. The server does not catch any of this: it accepts any fractional yardage (only non-yd items must be whole numbers) and any discount.
- **Scenario:** (1) Scan QAFAB, type 2.375 in its Qty field, then scan the next item (UPC 012345678905). The qty becomes 2.375012345678905 (still shown as '2 3/8 yd') and the second item is never rung up. (2) Type 10 in a line's Disc % field, then scan. The field becomes 100 and the line shows $0.00. Complete sale is enabled for a $0.00 total. (3) Type 3, click Card, then scan. No item is added and a second Card tender of $2.32 is recorded even though the card was never run.
- **Fix:** Route scanner bursts to the scan box: add a window keydown listener that treats fast keystrokes ending in Enter as a scan (by inter-key timing) and calls lookup(), wherever focus is. Also return focus to #register-code after addTender and after a qty/price/discount edit is committed (on Enter or blur). On both client and server, round yard quantities to the eighth and reject anything else: `Math.abs(qty*8 - Math.round(qty*8)) > 1e-9`.

### M50. Printed dates and times use the server's time zone (UTC), not the shop's: evening check-ins and payments show the next day on invoices; repair history and 'commission this month' are shifted too
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/format.ts:22` (bench-workflow; qa, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Round 1 added Shop.timeZone and used it for 'taken today' and the sales range. fmtDate still calls toLocaleDateString('en-US') without a timeZone, so on the hosted (UTC) server every date it renders is a UTC date: the invoice 'Received'/'Returned'/'Paid <date>'/warranty 'through', and the ticket, invoice and machine lists. The sewing shop's repair History uses `new Date(e.at).toLocaleString("en-US")` (repairs/[id]/page.tsx:164) in UTC. The repairs page's 'commission earned this month' boundary is `new Date(y, m, 1)` in server time (repairs/page.tsx:27) rather than monthRange(tz). The Checklist client component renders `new Date(s.at).toLocaleString()`, which differs between server and browser, so every ticket page with a ticked item throws a React #418 hydration error and re-renders on the client.
- **Scenario:** A shop in America/New_York checks a machine in at 9:30 pm on Sep 24 and the customer pays at 10:15 pm on Sep 25. The printed invoice says 'Received Sep 25, 2026' and 'Paid Sep 26, 2026 (Card)'. On the last evening of a month after 8 pm, /repairs already reports the next month's commission.
- **Fix:** Give fmtDate (and a fmtDateTime) a timeZone parameter and pass shop.timeZone from the pages; use `timeZone` in the Intl options. Use monthRange(tz) or startOfMonth(tz) on /repairs. In the Checklist, render the timestamp from a server-formatted string, or format it only after mount, to avoid the hydration mismatch.

### M51. Hub client follows cross-origin redirects with no SSRF guard (malicious/compromised hub reaches the install's private network)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/hub.ts:154` (hub-market; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Every hub request goes through reach()/hubFetch() using the global fetch() with its default redirect:"follow". The hub base URL is validated once at join time (checkHubUrl), but after that the install trusts the hub completely and follows any 3xx Location it returns to ANY host. hubPicture() (kind "listing"/"photo"/"figure", lines 680-708) fetches images this way, stores the bytes locally, and serves them at /api/listing-photos, /api/hub-photos, /api/figures. A hub that answers /v1/listing-photos/:id (or any hubFetch route) with a 302 to an internal address (http://127.0.0.1:*, http://169.254.169.254/…, a Railway private service) makes the bench server issue that GET. safe-fetch.ts (used only by downloadManualLink) blocks private addresses and re-checks every redirect, but the hub image/JSON client has none of that protection. The Authorization: Bearer <installKey> header is dropped by undici on the cross-origin hop, so the key is not leaked, but the internal GET still happens and its response body is cached and rendered to signed-in users as an image (imageResponse forces image/* + nosniff + CSP sandbox, so it is not XSS, but the raw bytes are shown).
- **Scenario:** An install admin joins (or is tricked via a stitchhub: invite into joining) a hub the attacker controls, or the legitimate hub is compromised. The hub returns 302 Location: http://127.0.0.1:5733/… (an internal service or cloud metadata endpoint) for a listing/figure/photo fetch. The bench server (on Railway or the shop LAN) issues the GET to that internal host and stores/serves the response. Reproduced locally: joined a private bench to a hub simulator; GET /api/listing-photos/evilphoto1 caused the bench to fetch http://127.0.0.1:5733/latest/meta-data/… and return the internal service's body 'INTERNAL-ONLY: db_password=hunter2' as image/jpeg.
- **Fix:** Give the hub client the same protection safe-fetch already implements: set redirect:"manual" (or "error") on the hub fetch and either refuse redirects outright or only follow them when the new URL's origin equals the configured hub origin; and/or route hub fetches through the safe-fetch private-address lookup. Do not blindly follow hub-supplied Location headers to arbitrary hosts.

### M52. Order for a listing that was already removed locally is saved with no company and nobody sees it (multi-company install)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/hub.ts:531` (hub-market; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** saveOrder finds the owning company of a new sale only from the local Listing row (`owner = (await db.listing.findUnique({ where: { id: o.listingId } }))?.shopId`), then falls back to soleShopId(), which is null when an install has more than one company. removeListing deletes the local listing row straight away (market.ts:109), and a REMOVED listing arriving in the feed is deleted too (hub.ts:482-484). The hub does not refuse removal while orders are open. So if a buyer orders and the seller removes the listing before the next sync (every 10 minutes), the order is stored with shopId NULL. It is missing from /market/orders for every company, its page returns 404, and it can never be claimed, because `have?.shopId` stays null on later updates. The buyer waits on REQUESTED forever. The same attribution gap orphans a buyer's order when placeOrder's response is lost (the order still exists on the hub; placeOrder has no idempotency key, so a retry also makes a duplicate). It also orphans a listing when createListing's response is lost: it arrives through sync as mine with shopId null, no company can see or manage it, and it can still be ordered.
- **Scenario:** On the shared multi-company install, my company listed an item. Another hub member ordered it through the API. I then removed the listing in the UI and pressed 'Check for updates' on /market/orders?role=seller. The sale does not appear, and /market/orders/<id> returns 404. The DB row has shopId NULL.
- **Fix:** Keep the owner on the hub: send a shopRef with createListing and placeOrder, and have the hub return it on listings and orders (see the identity finding). As a short-term fix, keep a tombstone for removed listings, e.g. mark the local row REMOVED instead of deleting it, so saveOrder can still find the company. Refuse DELETE /v1/listings/:id on the hub while REQUESTED/ACCEPTED/PAID orders exist (or have it decline them). Add a client idempotency key to POST /v1/orders and /v1/listings, as posts already have with clientId.

### M53. Leaving and rejoining the hub, or moving to another hub, orphans the shop's own listings, orders and notes and keeps stale rows from the old hub
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/hub.ts:276` (hub-market; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: leaving refused while orders are open, own listings taken off the hub, synced copies cleared on a new registration. Left: reuse the old install key on rejoin; HubFigure cleanup
- **Problem:** leaveHub clears the install key and cursors but keeps every synced Listing, MarketOrder, ManualLink and HubPost row. joinHub registers a brand-new install (new installId and key) and restarts the cursors at 0. After a rejoin, the next sync recomputes 'mine' against the new install, so the shop's own listings become another shop's: they disappear from My listings, can't be edited or removed (the hub answers 403), and the shop can order from itself. Open orders now belong to the old install, so every order action gets 404 and they are stranded. Rows the new hub doesn't have (after joining a different hub) are never removed: they show on /market and /links, and acting on them fails. The 'Leave the hub' confirm (src/app/(app)/settings/page.tsx:53) says only 'Disconnect from the hub?' and nothing blocks leaving with active listings or open orders. tests/hub.spec.ts leaves and rejoins on every run, so the shared e2e environment keeps accumulating this state.
- **Scenario:** A shop with 4 active listings and an open sale clicks Leave the hub, then joins again with the same invite. My listings shows 0. Browse shows its own listing as another shop's with a Send order form, and the order goes through (the shop buys from itself). The open sale can no longer be accepted or shipped from the bench.
- **Fix:** On leave, or on joining a different hub URL, delete the synced copies (ManualLink, HubPost/HubPhoto, Listing, ListingPhoto, XPart, HubFigure) and mark local MarketOrders archived. Refuse to leave while the install has ACTIVE listings or open orders, or warn clearly in the confirm. Better still, keep and reuse the install key when rejoining the same hub instead of registering a new install.

### M54. Local manuals folder: permission is requested after the click's user activation is consumed or expired, so Open and Save-scan fail after a browser restart
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/local-manuals.ts:206` (files-library-desktop; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** openLocal() calls `window.open("", "_blank")` first. In Chromium that consumes the click's transient activation. It then calls fileAt() -> ensurePermission() -> `h.requestPermission()`. When a restored directory handle's permission is back to 'prompt' (after a browser restart, unless 'Allow on every visit' was chosen), Chrome/Edge reject requestPermission without transient activation. The code comments themselves say 'Browsers may re-ask after a restart'. saveToLibrary() has the same problem: it asks for 'readwrite' only after the scanner has built the PDF, which takes several seconds for multi-page scans, so the activation has expired. ensurePermission() throws rather than returning false, so the download fallback (lines 247-253) is never reached. The only UI that requests 'readwrite' is connectFolder: Re-index requests just 'read'.
- **Scenario:** On a hosted install (manuals come only from each PC's connected folder), a tech restarts Chrome and clicks Open on a machine's manual. The new tab closes and the toast says 'User activation is required to request permissions.' This repeats on every click until they happen to use Show figure or Re-index. Saving a multi-page scan fails the same way every time until they Forget and reconnect the folder.
- **Fix:** In the click handler, call queryPermission first and, if needed, requestPermission before window.open. If a prompt was shown, open the file in the same tab or ask for one more click. Request 'readwrite' at the very start of the scanner's Save click, before building the PDF. Make ensurePermission catch SecurityError and return false so saveToLibrary falls back to download.

### M55. One bad partner message holds up every incoming partner message for every company on the install
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/partners.ts:550` (partners; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** pullMessages only sets a message aside straight away when it throws PartnerDataError. Any other error (a database rejection, a Prisma error) leaves the cursor just before that message and throws. That stops the whole pull for the install, across all links and all shops, and the message is retried for 3 syncs before it is skipped. The validators let through values the database refuses. For example, dateOf (line 68) accepts any JS-valid date such as "-100000-01-01T00:00:00Z", and Postgres rejects it with error 22009. A hostile partner with a link to any one company on a hosted multi-shop install can therefore keep inbound partner traffic stalled for every company there. Each poisoned message costs about 2 sync intervals, and the hub allows 3000 messages per hour. Side effects: hubState.partnerError (install-wide) is shown on every company's Partners page and carries the Prisma error text, including the source path and values. The 'N messages couldn't be sent' count at line 605 is install-wide, so a company can see a count while its own list is empty.
- **Scenario:** The partner (retail side) posts settle.paid with at="-100000-01-01T00:00:00Z", followed by an ordinary job.new. Sync 1 throws and the cursor stays before the poisoned message. Sync 2 does the same. Only sync 3 marks it skipped and applies the job.new. Every other message for every shop on the install waits behind it. Repeating one poisoned message about every 2 minutes stalls intake indefinitely.
- **Fix:** Bound dates in dateOf (for example 2000-01-01 to now+1 year) and throw PartnerDataError otherwise. Run applyMessage plus partnerSeen.create in one transaction. On any non-transient error (Prisma known-request or validation errors), park the message as a failure and continue, rather than stopping the install-wide pull; hold back at most that link. Keep partnerError and the stuck count per shop, and show a generic message instead of raw Prisma text.

### M56. settle.paid from a partner is booked with no confirmation, no cap and no sign check, so the victim's books show it owes up to $100,000
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/partners.ts:1006` (partners; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: a payment the partner reports counts only after "Confirm it arrived" on the statement (Settlement.confirmedAt). Left: consign.return still moves stock back without a confirmation step
- **Problem:** By design, the shop that pays records a settlement and the other shop just applies it. applyMessage accepts netCents anywhere in ±10,000,000 from either side. It does not check that the sign matches the sender (retail should be > 0, repair < 0), does not cap the amount at the running balance, and has no step where the receiver confirms the payment arrived. The balance on /partners/[id] then flips to 'You owe <partner> $100,000.00', and the settlement form on the statement page is pre-filled with that amount (statement/page.tsx: defaultValue when balance < 0 on the repair side). consign.return works the same way: the sewing shop's claim moves stock back onto the repair shop's shelf and is capped only at the on-shelf count.
- **Scenario:** A hostile sewing shop sends settle.paid {netCents: 10000000, note: 'paid in full'} without paying. The repair shop's balance goes from 0 to -10,000,000, meaning 'You owe Evil Sewing $100,000.00', and the Record payment form proposes paying it.
- **Fix:** Require the sign to match m.fromSide. Store incoming settlements as 'claimed' and leave them out of the running balance until an admin of the receiving shop confirms receipt, or at least cap an unconfirmed claim at what the sender owed. Show claimed payments separately. Do the same for consign.return (pending until the owner confirms the stock is back).

### M57. Buyer's copy of a partner order is never reconciled with the seller's rebuilt lines and prices, so the two statements disagree
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/partners.ts:886` (partners; security, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** placePartnerOrder (partner-orders.ts:33) prices the buyer's copy from its local catalog copy. On receipt, order.new rebuilds each line from the seller's current Part (current wholesale price, items no longer offered left out) and only adds a note. order.update messages (partner-orders.ts:156-163) carry status, carrier, tracking and notes, never lines or total. The buyer's PartnerOrder.lines and totalCents therefore stay as ordered. The buyer's statement and running balance use its totalCents. 'Receive' (partner-orders.ts:108-140) adds the buyer's lines to inventory, including items the seller left out. pushCatalogs only re-sends a changed catalog after 10 minutes (line 451), so after any price change or un-offer the buyer orders from a stale catalog for at least that long.
- **Scenario:** The repair shop raises the partner price of item X from $14 to $16, or un-offers item Y. Within the catalog window, the sewing shop orders 2 X and 1 Y. Seller copy: 2 x $16 = $32 (Y left out). Buyer copy: 2 x $14 + Y. After delivery and receipt, the buyer's statement and balance show a different amount from the seller's, and the buyer's inventory gains a Y that was never delivered. Neither side is told the totals differ.
- **Fix:** Have the seller send its rebuilt lines and totalCents back: in an 'order.accepted' or on the first order.update, or in DELIVERED, which is what counts on the statement. Have the buyer replace its lines and total with them before DELIVERED or RECEIVED is applied, and receive only what the seller delivered. Also send a changed catalog price immediately, rather than throttling price changes along with stock counts.

### M58. Replacing a terms proposal reuses its version number, so an accept of the old proposal splits the two shops' terms permanently (one shows 'both accepted' with withdrawn terms); accept also binds to whatever proposal is in the DB, not what was shown
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/partners.ts:305` (partners; code, security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: accept carries the version and terms seen; a replaced proposal can't be accepted. Left: the accepter keeps the withdrawn terms until it accepts the newer proposal
- **Problem:** proposeTerms always uses version = termsVersion + 1 (line 305), so 'Proposing again replaces it' (agreement page) reuses the same number. Suppose the partner accepts the first proposal while the replacement is in flight. acceptTerms (line 325-330) adopts it locally as the new version and records the proposer as having accepted too. The proposer's terms.accept handler sees the key mismatch and sets the accept aside (line 718, PartnerDataError). The accepter's terms.propose handler then ignores the replacement because version <= termsVersion (line 700). The error text says 'propose them again', but every re-proposal is again termsVersion+1 = 2 and is ignored the same way. The two sides now run on different terms. The sewing shop recomputes repair commission from its own copy (job.update, line 772) and its consignment share at the register, while the repair shop uses its copy, so the statements disagree. Separately, AcceptForm (agreement/page.tsx:92) posts only name and agree. acceptTerms reads l.proposedTerms fresh (line 321), so if a newer proposal arrived after the page loaded, the click accepts terms the user never saw.
- **Scenario:** The repair shop proposes a 20% commission (v2). The sewing shop, with v2 on screen, accepts, while the repair shop replaces its proposal with 25% before the next sync. Result: the sewing shop is at v2, 20%, and shows 'Both shops accepted version 2'. The repair shop is at v1, 15%, still 'waiting'. Proposing again changes nothing. From then on the sewing shop credits itself 20% commission and the repair shop 15%.
- **Fix:** Never reuse a proposal number: version = max(termsVersion, proposedTerms?.version ?? 0) + 1. Make the proposer's confirmation authoritative. On an accept whose key doesn't match, the proposer should answer (for example, re-send its current proposal with a higher version, or send 'terms.reject'), and the accepter should reopen instead of staying 'agreed'. The accepter should not record the proposer's acceptance until that confirmation arrives. In terms.propose, don't silently ignore version <= termsVersion when the key differs from the current terms; treat it as a conflict to show. Put the displayed version and termsKey in hidden inputs on AcceptForm, and have acceptTerms refuse if the stored proposal no longer matches.

### M59. Install-level hub refusals (401/403) dead-letter each partner message one by one, and later messages go out ahead of them
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/partners.ts:495` (partners; security, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by the merged fix-agent/hub-core work; re-checked in round-3 part 2)
- **Problem:** pushOutbox treats any 4xx other than 408/429 as 'refused for good' for that message: it sets deadAt and does not hold back the partnership. A 401 (hub restored or moved without this install) or a 403 (install blocked, then unblocked) is not about the message. While it lasts, every waiting and newly created message is dead-lettered. Afterwards each must be re-sent by hand ('Send again', 30 shown at a time), in whatever order the user clicks, after later messages have already gone. For example, job.paid can go out before its job.new, and the receiver silently drops messages for jobs it doesn't know.
- **Scenario:** The hub admin blocks the install for an hour, and meanwhile the sewing shop takes in three machines and rings up a payment. All four messages are marked dead. After the unblock nothing is sent until someone re-sends each one, and a job.paid re-sent before its job.new is lost on the repair side.
- **Fix:** Treat 401 and 403 (and 404 'No such partner link') as install-level or link-level conditions: stop the push, keep the messages waiting (not dead), and show the cause. Reserve deadAt for message-specific refusals (400, 413). When a dead message is re-sent, keep holding later messages of the same link until it is sent.

### M60. Voiding a consigned sale leaves the two partners' consignment balances out of step (rounding and terms changes)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/partners.ts:987` (register-money; code, security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** voidSale (register.ts:349-351) records the void's shopKeepsCents as exactly the negative of the original sale's. The repair shop's consign.sale handler validates the '-void' id against the original but then recomputes shopKeepsCents as Math.round(gross * terms.consignSharePct / 100) from today's terms. Math.round rounds -x.5 toward +infinity, so any sale whose share lands on a half cent comes out 1 cent different after a void (e.g. a $10.05 item at 30%). If the consignment share was changed between the sale and the void, the difference is the whole share delta. Statements and runningBalance on each side sum these rows, so the partners' 'owed' totals disagree and settlements will not net to zero.
- **Scenario:** The sewing shop sells a consigned $10.05 item (share 30%): both sides record shopKeeps 302. The owner voids it. The sewing shop records -302; the repair shop records Math.round(-301.5) = -301. Sewing shop net owed for sale+void is 0; repair shop's is -1. If the terms moved to 40% before the void, the repair shop's net becomes +$1.00 for a sale that no longer exists.
- **Fix:** In the consign.sale handler, when the id ends with '-void' and `original` is found, store `shopKeepsCents: original.shopKeepsCents != null ? -original.shopKeepsCents : -Math.round((Math.round(original.qty*original.priceCents) * terms.consignSharePct)/100)`. Better still, put one shared share helper (round half away from zero) in partner-terms.ts and use it on both sides.

### M61. job.paid marks the job paid and freezes its commission even when the sewing shop collected less than the repair shop's current bill; the remainder can never be collected through the partner flow
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/partners.ts:828` (partners; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The sewing shop's register charges repairDue() from its local copy of totalCents (register.ts:66,186) and sets paidAt. On the repair shop, job.paid sets paidAt: job.paidAt ?? at whatever the amount (line 828), which fixes paidAt and the commission in jobSnapshot. If the repair shop added a line after the sewing shop's last job.update, the later update raises totalCents on the sewing side, but the job keeps paidAt. pickupJobs filters paidAt: null and completeSale rejects paid jobs, so the balance can't be rung up. Each side also froze commission from a different bill.
- **Scenario:** The repair shop adds a $30 service line to a READY machine at 10:00. At 10:00:30, before the sewing shop's next sync, the customer pays the old $120 at the sewing shop's counter. The repair ticket shows $30 still due, the job is 'paid' on both sides, and the commission differs (15% of $120 vs 15% of $150).
- **Fix:** On the repair side, set paidAt only when SHOP_COLLECTED plus direct payments cover the current total. Send the current due in the next job.update; on the sewing side, a due greater than 0 clears paidAt or lists the remainder in pickupJobs. Alternatively, have job.paid carry the totalCents it paid against, and have the repair shop answer with the difference.

### M62. Editing any cross-reference entry corrupts its 'where to buy' sources (comma prices/URLs, 3-decimal prices, currency, vendor names, notes)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/parts.ts:105` (hub-commerce; qa, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The xref form round-trips sources through plain text: sourcesText writes 'vendor, url, 12.50' and parseSources splits each line on every comma. It takes the first part as the vendor, the first http(s) part as the URL and the first part that looks like a number as the price, and it forces currency 'USD' (line 114). Any comma inside a vendor name, URL or price is therefore misread. Prices with 3 decimals are silently dropped. Currency and the note field (kept only when vendor and url match exactly) are lost. Because the form re-submits every source on every save, fixing an unrelated field such as specs or name rewrites the community entry for all shops. Supplier-scan and API sources that carry a currency or note are affected most.
- **Scenario:** (1) Where to buy = 'Big Supplier, https://big.example/foot, 1,000.50' gives $1.00. (2) 'Wawak, https://wawak.example/p?ids=12,34, 4.95' gives url '...?ids=12' and price $34.00. (3) 'Euro Parts, https://euro.example/f, 19.999' gives no price. (4) A hub source {vendor:'Canada Sew, Inc.', currency:'CAD', priceCents:1299, note:'ships from Toronto'}: a tech changes only Specs and saves, and the source becomes vendor 'Canada Sew', USD 12.99, no note, re-attributed to the editing shop.
- **Fix:** Stop round-tripping sources through comma text. Edit them as structured rows (vendor / url / price / currency / note inputs per source, with the source id in a hidden field). At minimum: parse the URL as the first 'https?://' token up to whitespace, parse the price with toCents on the last field only, keep `was.currency` and `was.note` when the id matches, and reject prices with more than 2 decimals instead of dropping them.

### M63. Reserved platform-admin emails can be claimed through /signup (the reservation only exists in addUser)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/shops.ts:44` (auth-accounts; security, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Round 1 added a reservation so nobody else can take an address in PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS (src/actions/settings.ts:105-106). It lives only in addUser. createShopWithOwner, used by /signup, has no such check, and neither does the signup action (src/app/signup/page.tsx:33-40). isPlatformAdmin() is decided purely by the account's email, so whoever registers a listed address first gets the Companies screen (every company's owner, email and counts, plus creating companies), install-admin rights over the hub connection (requireInstallAdmin), and the right to delete any company's shared notes (src/actions/shared.ts:54, src/actions/hub.ts:153).
- **Scenario:** The operator sets PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS=me@ops.com,partner@ops.com with ALLOW_SIGNUP=true, and partner@ops.com hasn't signed in or been created yet (or an address is listed ahead of time). An anonymous visitor signs up at /signup with email partner@ops.com. On their first page load the nav shows Companies, and they can join or leave the install's hub and add companies.
- **Fix:** Put the reservation in createShopWithOwner, taking the caller's platform-admin status (allowed only when a platform admin creates it through createCompany or setUpSewingShop). Or refuse any isPlatformAdmin(email) in the signup action. Better still, tie platform-admin status to a flag on the user row set by seeding or the operator, not to a bare email string.

### M64. A payment recorded as 'Invoice (on account)' counts as money received: the ticket shows paid, drops off Unpaid invoices and trade-account 'owed', and is added to 'Money taken'
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/ticket-math.ts:22` (gap; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (started in 5d0d336, finished and checked in round-3 part 2; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The ticket payment form offers every PaymentMethod, including INVOICE ('Invoice (on account)', constants.ts:54) and SHOP_COLLECTED. INVOICE appears nowhere else in the code: ticketTotals sums every payment into `paid` (ticket-math.ts:22), so a ticket billed to an account has balance 0. Several things follow. /invoices?show=unpaid and 'Still owed' leave it out. The trade account's '$X owed' on the customer page is 0 (customers/[id]/page.tsx:29). The printed invoice says 'Balance due $0.00'. The sales log adds it to 'Money taken' (sales/page.tsx:30), and so does the dashboard's 'taken today' aggregate. No receivable is tracked, and recording the account's real payment later counts the money twice.
- **Scenario:** The clerk bills a dealer's ticket on account by recording a $100 payment with method 'Invoice (on account)'. Seen live: the invoice reads 'Paid Sep 27, 2026 (Invoice (on account)) -$100.00 / Balance due $0.00', /invoices?show=unpaid shows 0 invoices and $0.00 still owed, and /sales shows $100.00 'Money taken'. Nothing anywhere records that the dealer still owes $100. When the dealer pays and the clerk records it, the ticket goes to -$100 and Money taken doubles.
- **Fix:** Treat INVOICE as a charge to an account rather than a payment: leave it out of `paid`, 'Money taken' and 'taken today', and show it as account receivable on the trade account with a way to record when the account settles. At minimum, stop offering it as a payment method. Consider leaving SHOP_COLLECTED out of 'Money taken' too, or showing it separately, because that money is still held by the partner until settlement.

### M65. README's hub-move promise has no StitchBench flow: re-joining at a new address creates a new install and loses the shop's listings, orders and notes
- **Where:** stitchhub `README.md:59` (hub-core; code, security; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The README says: 'After an import, members keep working with their existing keys once they point StitchBench at the new address.' transfer.mjs keeps install key hashes and seqs for that purpose. StitchBench has no way to change the hub URL while keeping the key. joinHub (stitchbench src/lib/hub.ts:245-279) always POSTs /v1/register at a new URL and stores the new installId/installKey, and it refuses outright when the shop has active partner links. desktop/join-hub.mjs does the same. After a move to a new address (e.g. a new *.up.railway.app), the shop's old install id still owns its listings, open orders (seller or buyer), notes ('mine') and partner links on the imported hub. The shop's new install can't see /v1/orders for them, can't edit or remove its listings, and can't remove its notes.
- **Scenario:** The admin moves the hub from app-old.up.railway.app to app-new.up.railway.app and imports the export. A seller re-joins at the new address as instructed. Its 3 active listings and 2 accepted-but-unshipped orders belong to the old install id, so it can no longer mark them paid or shipped. A shop with partner links is told to 'End those partnerships before moving to another hub', which the export was meant to preserve.
- **Fix:** Add a 'hub moved' action in StitchBench (Settings → Community hub → Change address). It should validate the new URL with GET /v1/me using the existing key, confirm that the installId matches, then update only hubState.url and keep all cursors. Until then, correct the README to say moves must keep the same address (custom domain) or members lose ownership of their marketplace items.

### M66. SSRF: the link checker (on by default) fetches member-supplied URLs on loopback, private and link-local addresses, follows redirects, and shows every member the internal HTTP status
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/checker.mjs:9` (hub-core; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** validUrl (server.mjs:57-64) only checks for http/https. The checker (enabled unless LINK_CHECKER=off; .env.example leaves it on) sends HEAD, then a ranged GET, to every link URL with `redirect: "follow"`. It does no DNS or IP filtering and no port restriction. The result is stored in links.http_status and status and returned to every member in /v1/changes (linkOut httpStatus), which gives a status-code oracle. A member can re-probe on demand: editing the URL resets checked_at, and POST /v1/links/:id/report sets it to NULL, and the checker picks NULLs first within 60 s. StitchBench itself refuses to download hub links from private networks (fetchPublic in src/actions/hub.ts), but the hub has no such guard.
- **Scenario:** A member adds links such as http://192.168.1.1/..., http://<service>.railway.internal/..., or http://127.0.0.1:<port>/..., or a public URL that redirects to one of them. Within a minute the hub sends HEAD/GET requests into its own network: the NAS, router, printers or the shop PC for a docker-compose hub, or other project services on Railway. The attacker reads the 200/404/403 results in /v1/changes to map internal services, and can trigger internal GET endpoints that have side effects.
- **Fix:** In probe(): resolve the host, reject loopback, RFC1918, link-local (169.254/16, fe80::/10), ULA, CGNAT, 0.0.0.0/8, multicast and *.internal. Connect to the vetted IP (a custom undici Agent `connect` lookup) so DNS rebinding cannot swap the address. Use `redirect: "manual"` and re-validate each hop, up to 5. Allow only ports 80 and 443. Consider applying the same check when a link is added so members get feedback. Reuse the logic of the bench's fetchPublic.

### M67. Hub process crashes whenever Postgres drops an idle connection (no pool 'error' listener); unawaited notify() can also crash it
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/db.mjs:6` (hub-core; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** `new pg.Pool(...)` has no `pool.on('error', ...)` handler. When an idle pooled client gets an error (Postgres restart, managed-DB failover or maintenance, a proxy idle timeout, pg_terminate_backend), pg-pool re-emits it on the pool. With no listener, Node throws an unhandled 'error' event and the whole hub exits. The 1-second positions() interval from 28842a1 means there is almost always an idle client in the pool, so any DB restart takes the hub down. Railway's ON_FAILURE policy restarts it, but migrate() fails while the DB is still down. That crash-loop can use up Railway's restart retries and leave the hub down. A related cause: notify() (server.mjs:67) is called without await or .catch() at 6 places. If getSetting() rejects during a DB blip after a write, the rejection is unhandled, and Node 22 exits on unhandled rejections.
- **Scenario:** Railway restarts the Postgres service for maintenance. Idle hub connections get FATAL 57P01, the pool emits 'error', and the hub process dies. Every in-flight upload and order action fails, and if the DB takes longer than the restart budget to come back, the hub stays down.
- **Fix:** Add `pool.on('error', (e) => console.error('db pool:', e.message))` in db.mjs; pg discards the broken client and the next query opens a new connection. Make notify() fully self-contained: wrap its body in try/catch, or call it as `notify(...).catch(() => {})`. Optionally add `process.on('unhandledRejection', ...)` logging as a backstop.

### M68. Marketplace ratings are trivially manipulated: seller unilaterally drives order to PAID and can defame buyers; sockpuppet 5-star inflation
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/market.mjs:449` (hub-commerce; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: buyer can rate once PAID and can always answer; standing counts the latest rating per rater and ignores $0 orders. Left: the seller can still mark an order PAID alone (owner kept the rating flow, d2a8c46)
- **Problem:** The order state machine lets the seller alone advance an order to PAID (POST /orders/:id/paid, step('seller',['ACCEPTED']), market.mjs:449) without any buyer action or real payment — the 'buyer pays seller directly' model has no verification. The seller can then rate the buyer (RATE_FROM.seller=['PAID','SHIPPED','RECEIVED'], market.mjs:490-506), and the buyer can never rate back because RATE_FROM.buyer requires SHIPPED/RECEIVED, which the seller can withhold (and the seller can then cancel from PAID, OPEN includes PAID). So a seller can attach a permanent 1-star public rating to any buyer it transacts with, unanswerable. Separately, because listings may be priced at 0 (priceCents>=0, market.mjs:189-192) and registration only needs the community join key (shared among all members) rate-limited per-IP at 10/hour (server.mjs:124), a member can spin up a second install and mint unlimited 5-star seller ratings against itself with zero money moving, inflating its `sellerReputation` shown on every listing and via GET /v1/reputation/:installId.
- **Scenario:** Defamation: buyer B places an order; seller S accepts, then S calls /paid (200) though B never paid, then S rates B 1 star ('Never paid, avoid') which is stored and shows in B's public asBuyer reputation; S cancels; B's attempt to rate S returns 409 ('you can rate the seller once the order has shipped'). Inflation: attacker registers a 2nd install, lists a $0 item, orders it from the 2nd install 5 times, accept+ship each, rate 5★ → asSeller {avg:5,count:5}.
- **Fix:** Gate buyer-rateable and seller-rateable states on mutual progress: only let the seller rate the buyer after the buyer has signalled payment (buyer-paid) or after SHIPPED, and let the buyer rate the seller whenever the seller could rate the buyer, so neither side can rate unanswerably. Consider ignoring ratings from orders where seller and buyer share contact/IP or where price is 0, and de-weighting reputation by distinct counterparties. At minimum, don't let a seller reach PAID + rate a buyer with no buyer participation.

### M69. Hub: a blocked seller's listings stay ACTIVE in every member's feed and still accept new orders
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/market.mjs:321` (hub-market; qa, security, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Blocking an install (POST /v1/admin/installs/:id/block) only sets installs.blocked. POST /v1/orders loads the seller with 'SELECT id, name FROM installs WHERE id = $1' and doesn't check blocked. The blocked install's listings are not paused or removed, so they keep going out in /v1/changes as ACTIVE. Buyers keep ordering from a shop the admin has banned, and that shop can no longer see the orders (every call returns 403).
- **Scenario:** The hub admin blocks a scam shop. Its 'cheap motor' listing stays on every shop's Browse page. Buyers keep sending orders, which sit in REQUESTED forever. Worse, orders the scam shop had already accepted still carry its payment instructions.
- **Fix:** In POST /orders, refuse with 409 when the seller is blocked. When an install is blocked, set its ACTIVE listings to PAUSED or REMOVED and bump seq so members pick up the change. Consider cancelling its open REQUESTED orders with a reason.

### M70. Multi-company install: every company's listings, sales, invoices, notes and star ratings are published under the one install's name
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/market.mjs:230` (hub-market; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** A hosted StitchBench with several companies has one hub install and one install name: the name of the company whose admin joined. The hub fills in the seller and buyer names from that install. Listings use `inst.name` as shop_name (market.mjs:229-230). Orders use `seller.name` / `me.name` as seller_shop / buyer_shop (market.mjs:334-335). Notes use `inst.name` (server.mjs:406-408). Ratings are keyed by install_id (order_ratings.rated_install_id). StitchBench never sends which company acted: createListing, placeOrder and pushPending send no seller/buyer company. The partner channel does handle this per company (shopRef/shopName in partners.mjs); the marketplace and notes do not. So company B's listing shows to every hub member as sold by company A. Company B's invoice (market/orders/[id]/invoice/page.tsx:30 prints `o.sellerShop`) carries company A's name in the header above company B's address and pay instructions. A company B note that showed its own name while pending (shared-posts.tsx uses p.shop.name) switches to company A's name after sync. The buyer's bad rating of a company B sale lowers company A's seller rating on every listing and on /market/shops/<installId>. That page also labels the other companies' listings 'Your listing', because ListingCard uses the raw `mine` flag. INSTALL.md documents this setup: one Railway copy holding several shops, and a repair shop hosting its sewing shops, which sell notions/fabric/thread, in the same install.
- **Scenario:** The shared test install at :3100 joined the hub as 'E2E Test Shop'. I renamed my own company there to 'QA HubMkt Shop A' and listed 'QA orphan test ...' from it. On the hub that listing has shop_name = 'E2E Test Shop', and the order a buyer placed on it has seller_shop = 'E2E Test Shop'. Every other shop sees another company's name, and ratings from that sale go to that company.
- **Fix:** Give marketplace and notes a per-company identity the way partners.mjs does. StitchBench sends shopRef (the company id) and shopName with every listing, order, note and rating. The hub stores seller_shop_ref/seller_shop_name on listings, orders and posts, and keys reputation by (install_id, shop_ref). StitchBench fills in sellerShop/buyerShop from these fields and computes ListingCard's 'Your listing' from shopId, not the raw `mine` flag. Until then, block the marketplace for companies other than the one that joined on multi-company installs.

### M71. Hub partner channel: any member can store unbounded 4 MB messages (self-link, and new installs minted via /partners/join)
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/partners.mjs:171` (partners; security, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: partner-only installs traced to the member whose codes brought them on (10/hour, 50 total); byte limits per link side. A self-link (two companies on one install) stays allowed for hosted installs
- **Problem:** The only limits on /partners/links/:id/messages are 4 MB per message and 3000 messages per hour per install. Messages are kept for 365 days. redeem() lets an install link two of its own made-up shopRefs (it only refuses the identical install+shopRef), so no victim is needed. A member can also turn its own invite codes into brand-new partner-scope installs through /partners/join. Each of those has fresh rate buckets and can create its own invites, which multiplies the per-install quota. At roughly 12 GB/hour per install, the hub's Postgres volume can be filled, taking the community hub and every partner channel down.
- **Scenario:** A member registers, creates an invite as shopRef 'a', redeems it as shopRef 'b', and posts incompressible 4 MB job.new messages at the rate limit. The partner_messages table grows about 4 MB per request. Additional installs minted via /partners/join each add another 3000/hour.
- **Fix:** Refuse links where repair_install_id = retail_install_id unless the shopRefs belong to one hosted install that has really set up the other company (or require the redeemer to be another install). Add per-install and per-link storage quotas (bytes per day, and outstanding unread bytes). Rate-limit /partners/join per inviting install as well as per IP, and cap the partner-scope installs one inviter can create. Drop messages once both sides' cursors have passed them, instead of keeping them for 365 days.

### M72. Unauthenticated partner-join (and register) limits are keyed on the client's own X-Forwarded-For, and the buckets map is never pruned
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/partners.mjs:142` (hub-commerce; code, security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by the merged fix-agent/hub-core work; re-checked in round-3 part 2)
- **Problem:** /v1/partners/join and /v1/register take the rate-limit key from the first X-Forwarded-For entry, which the client controls: behind a proxy that appends, the first entry is still the one the attacker sent. So the 10-per-hour limit is bypassed by changing the header. Each distinct key adds an entry to the in-memory `buckets` Map (server.mjs:74-85), and entries are never removed, so an unauthenticated client can grow hub memory without limit. Guessing codes stays impractical (about 59 bits), but the limit gives no protection.
- **Scenario:** A client sends millions of /v1/partners/join requests with random X-Forwarded-For values. None is throttled, and the buckets Map grows until the hub runs out of memory.
- **Fix:** Use the proxy-appended (right-most trusted) address or the platform's client-IP header, prune expired buckets (e.g. sweep entries older than an hour), and add a global cap for unauthenticated routes.

### M73. Any member can store about 12 GB/hour in partner messages kept for a year (a self-link needs no second shop)
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/partners.mjs:288` (hub-commerce; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** POST /v1/partners/links/:id/messages accepts messages of up to 4 MB, 3000 per install per hour, and messages are kept for 365 days. Any member can make an invite for shopRef 'a' and redeem it themselves for shopRef 'b' (allowed by design for multi-company installs), which gives a private link to fill. 3000 x 4 MB = 12 GB/hour of jsonb per install, and a join key can register more installs. That fills the hub's Postgres disk and takes down the community, and the sweep only removes messages after a year.
- **Scenario:** A member creates an invite (shopRef a) and redeems it (shopRef b). Five 4 MB messages are stored in about 0.7 s, so at the 3000/hour limit the member writes about 12 GB/hour.
- **Fix:** Add a byte budget per install per hour/day (e.g. 200 MB/day) in addition to the message count. Drop data a recipient has already fetched, or cap stored bytes per link. Alert the admin summary on unusual volume.

### M74. A removed cross-reference part can't be restored from StitchBench, and its numbers stay locked so nobody can re-add them
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/parts.mjs:137` (hub-commerce; code, security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by the merged fix-agent/hub-core work; re-checked in round-3 part 2)
- **Problem:** DELETE /v1/xparts/:id only sets deleted=true. The part's rows stay in xpart_numbers, and checkNumbers() does not exclude deleted parts, so every later POST/PUT that uses one of those numbers gets 409 'already on another part', with partId pointing at the deleted entry. On the StitchBench side, removeCrossRef (xref.ts:71-77) needs only requireUser, so any TECH on any member install can click 'Remove for everyone'. The next sync deletes the local copy on every install (hub.ts:457-459), and /xref/[id] then returns notFound. No StitchBench code calls /v1/xparts/:id/restore, and the history and roll-back UI is only on the detail page, which is gone. The local numberOwner check passes (the part is gone locally), so users get the hub's 409 about a part they cannot open, merge into ('The part to merge into was removed') or restore. The same applies to the part merged away in /merge. Manual links have the same gap: the success message says 'it can be restored from the hub', but no client restore exists.
- **Scenario:** A careless or hostile tech removes 'Needle plate screw' (Singer NPS-…). Every shop's copy disappears. Later a tech tries to add the part again from the parts list (addPartToCrossRef) or the xref form and gets 409 'That part number is already on another part. Open that part…', but no such part is visible anywhere. The number stays out of the cross-reference until someone calls the hub API by hand.
- **Fix:** On the hub, either free the numbers of deleted parts (move them to the revision snapshot and delete from xpart_numbers, putting them back on restore) or have checkNumbers() and import ignore deleted parts and reclaim their numbers. In StitchBench, keep deleted entries locally (a deleted flag) or add a 'Removed parts' view with Restore (/v1/xparts/:id/restore) and history. Consider limiting 'Remove for everyone' to owners/admins.

### M75. /v1/changes core sync feed can be wedged by oversized xpart entries (3000-fit cap, no feed byte cap)
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/parts.mjs:77` (hub-commerce; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** cleanPart allows up to 3,000 fits per part, each with brand(60)+model(80)+source(300) (parts.mjs:77-92), so one part serializes to ~1.7 MB in /v1/changes (server.mjs:207-251, `xparts: await partsByIds(ids)` at :246) and in /xparts/:id/history. The changes feed limits by row count only (up to 2000 rows, app uses 1000) with no aggregate byte cap. A member (writeLimit 300 writes/hour) can create a few hundred such parts; when they fall in one change page the response exceeds V8's ~512 MB string limit and c.json throws RangeError (500), the same failure mode proven for the partner feed. Because every member's background sync (stitchbench src/lib/hub.ts:636 doSync) re-requests from the same cursor and only advances on success, a wedged page permanently stalls the community sync (manual links, notes, parts, listings) for ALL members from that seq forward.
- **Scenario:** Member creates ~300 parts each with 3000 max-length fits over ~1 hour. Once ~300+ land in a single 1000-row /v1/changes page (~510 MB+), every member's sync GET /v1/changes hits RangeError → 500, and cursors stall; the whole community feed stops advancing.
- **Fix:** Lower the per-part fits cap to a sane maximum (e.g. 200-300) and/or cap the /v1/changes response by accumulated bytes, returning more:true with an advancing next cursor before the payload gets large, so no single feed page can exceed a safe size.

### M76. Editing or rolling back a large cross-reference entry silently truncates it to 50 numbers / 3000 fits / 30 sources
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/parts.mjs:66` (hub-commerce; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by the merged fix-agent/hub-core work; re-checked in round-3 part 2)
- **Problem:** cleanPart() caps numbers at 50 (line 66), fits at 3000 (line 77) and sources at 30 (line 94) with .slice(), with no error. Merge (UPDATE xpart_numbers SET xpart_id) and admin import (numbers and fits from many entries added to one part) have no such cap, so entries can legitimately grow past the limits, for example universal bobbins and needles that fit thousands of models. StitchBench's 'Fix this part' form then sends the whole entry back (numbersText/fitsText of every row). The PUT keeps only the first 50/3000/30, and writeEntry deletes the rest. Revert passes the snapshot through the same cleanPart, so a roll-back truncates too.
- **Scenario:** 'Class 15 bobbin' has 80 numbers after a merge. A tech fixes a typo in the name. The hub stores only 50 numbers, 30 are gone without warning, and they are now free for other parts to claim.
- **Fix:** Reject over-limit input with a 400 instead of slicing, and use the same caps in merge and import (or raise the caps and enforce them everywhere). For PUT, compare with the stored entry and refuse to drop rows the client didn't show.

### M77. Brute-force rate limits keyed on spoofable X-Forwarded-For (join-key guessing on /v1/register)
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/server.mjs:124` (hub-market; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The only brute-force protection on the community join key is `allow(register:${ip}, 10/hr)` where `ip = c.req.header("x-forwarded-for")?.split(",")[0]`. The hub trusts the first X-Forwarded-For token unconditionally (there is no TRUST_PROXY gate like the bench login limiter has). A client can send its own X-Forwarded-For header; on a hosted hub behind a proxy that prepends/passes the header, the attacker-chosen value is taken as the key, so rotating it gives unlimited attempts.
