The join key is a shared community secret (admin-settable, min 8 chars via /v1/admin/join-key), so an install-wide weak key becomes online-brute-forceable, granting full community membership (read all shared notes/links/marketplace, post spam, place orders). The public partner-join route has the same pattern (partners.mjs:142-143, pjoin:${ip}), though partner codes are high-entropy so less exposed.
- **Scenario:** An attacker repeatedly POSTs /v1/register with guessed join keys, sending a different X-Forwarded-For (e.g. 10.9.1.1, 10.9.2.1, …) on each request. The register limiter never trips, so the attacker can grind the join key at full network rate and, on success, gets a valid installKey.
- **Fix:** Only trust X-Forwarded-For when running behind a known proxy (gate on an env flag as the bench does with TRUST_PROXY), and take the correct hop, not the first client-supplied token; otherwise key the limiter on the socket remote address. Add a global cap on join-key failures independent of client IP.

### M78. Hub write budget shared across all companies on a multi-company install (one tech can deny all hub features to other tenants)
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/server.mjs:162` (hub-market; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** writeLimit() keys the 300/hr write budget on the installId. On a hosted multi-company StitchBench (PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS deployment) every company shares one hub connection and therefore one installId (there is a single HubState row, id=1, in the bench). So all companies draw from the same 300-writes/hour bucket, and any signed-in user of any company can exhaust it. "Sync now" (syncCommunityHub) calls reportUsageNextSync() then pushUsage(), which unconditionally sends 2 POST /v1/usage writes per sync; a low-privilege TECH can press it (or replay the server action) ~150 times to consume the whole install's budget, after which every other company's hub writes (creating/updating listings, accepting/paying/shipping orders, adding/fixing manual links, sharing notes) fail with 429 for up to an hour.
- **Scenario:** On a hosted install holding shops A and B, a TECH at shop B repeatedly triggers Sync now (each = 2 hub writes). After ~150 syncs the install hits write:${installId} 300/hr. Shop A's owner then cannot list a part or act on a marketplace order: every hub write returns 'Too many changes in the last hour.'
- **Fix:** Either give each company its own install/key on a multi-company hosted install, or track and rate-limit hub usage per company (shopId) on the bench before calling the hub, and/or raise/scope the hub budget per member. At minimum, stop 'Sync now' from spending write budget on usage reporting on every click (respect the hourly usage-push guard).

### M79. Hub figure store is first-writer-wins with no replace or delete: any member can permanently poison the figures every install shows
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/server.mjs:460` (files-library-desktop; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** PUT /v1/figures/:id accepts any 24-hex id from any member and inserts with `ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING`, then returns {ok:true} even when the upload was discarded. Figure ids are deterministic: sha1(sourcePath|page|box) over data that ships with every installer (seed-data/factory_settings.json) and is sent to every user in page props. So a malicious member can compute the ids for all ~14k settings and upload wrong or offensive images before the honest shops do. Nothing can replace or delete them: there is no hub endpoint or admin action for figures. Installs that don't have their own copy show the hub's (/api/figures/[id] falls back to hubPicture), and the bench caches that copy in HubFigure forever (src/lib/hub.ts:700-707 never re-fetches once data is set).
- **Scenario:** Mallory registers with the join key, computes figureId for every factory setting from the shipped JSON, and PUTs a misleading image for each. Every member install without the manual now shows Mallory's image under 'Show figure' for needle-bar height, timing and so on. When an honest bench later shares the real figure, the hub answers ok:true but keeps Mallory's, and there's no way to fix it short of editing the hub DB.
- **Fix:** Record the uploader (install_id is already stored) and let the original uploader or the hub admin replace or delete a figure, with an admin endpoint and admin.html control, and a changes-feed entry so benches drop their cached HubFigure.data. Return a distinct status (e.g. 409 or {kept:false}) when the upload is ignored. Consider letting the hub admin approve figures before they're served, or at least bounding per-member figure count and size.

### M80. Deleting a note (by its author or by the admin) doesn't remove it: /v1/changes keeps sending its full title, body and photo list to every member, and its photos stay downloadable
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/server.mjs:224` (hub-core; security, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** DELETE /v1/posts/:id (line 430) and the admin DELETE (line 543) only set deleted=true and bump seq. /v1/changes (lines 224-240) still returns title, body, authorName and photo metadata for deleted posts. GET /v1/photos/:id (line 439) serves photos without checking whether the post is deleted. StitchBench discards deleted posts (savePost deletes locally), so the content is useless to honest installs but available to anyone who reads the API, including shops that join later (since=0).
- **Scenario:** A tech shares a note that includes a customer's name, phone number or address, or a photo of a label. The owner deletes it (or the admin removes abusive or illegal content). Any member, or any shop that joins later, calls /v1/changes?since=0 and still gets the full text and photo ids, then downloads the photos. Moderation and retraction don't work.
- **Fix:** When a post is deleted, clear title/body/author_name (or return only {id, deleted:true} in /changes for deleted rows) and DELETE its photos rows in the same transaction. Make /v1/photos/:id join posts and return 404 when the post is deleted.

### M81. Photos of a removed note are still served to every member
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/server.mjs:440` (hub-core; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** DELETE /v1/posts/:id (and admin DELETE /posts/:id) only sets posts.deleted = true. GET /v1/photos/:id reads `SELECT mime, data FROM photos WHERE id = $1` and never checks the post. Every install that synced the note before removal already has the photo ids from /v1/changes, so it can keep downloading them. The usual reason to remove a note is that a photo shows something it shouldn't, such as a customer's name label or serial number, which the README promises are never shared. Removal does not take that photo back. Photos of removed notes are also kept in every export.
- **Scenario:** A tech shares a note with a photo that shows the customer's name tag, notices, and removes the note. Other installs drop the note locally, but any member who saw the photo id in an earlier sync can still GET /v1/photos/<id> and receive the image.
- **Fix:** Serve only photos of live posts: `SELECT ph.mime, ph.data FROM photos ph JOIN posts p ON p.id = ph.post_id WHERE ph.id = $1 AND NOT p.deleted`. Better still, delete (or null out the data of) the post's photo rows in the same statement that marks the post deleted.

### M82. The hub export is not a consistent snapshot: an export taken while members are active can contain rows whose parents are missing, and the all-or-nothing import then fails with a generic error
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/transfer.mjs:109` (hub-core; security, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** lines() pages each table with separate autocommit q() calls, streaming lazily as the client reads, and reads tables one after another in FK order. It never takes one snapshot. Rows written during the export can be missing from a parent table that was already exported but present in a child table exported later: posts->installs, photos->posts, link_revisions->links, order_events->orders, partner_messages->partner_links. importStream runs in a single transaction and aborts on the first FK violation. The admin only sees "Something went wrong on the hub." The README tells admins to "Download an export now and then and keep it somewhere safe", so those backups may not be restorable.
- **Scenario:** The admin downloads a backup while a new shop joins and shares a note, and keeps the file. Later the hub is lost. Importing the backup fails: the post references an install that isn't in the file. Nothing is restored, and the error doesn't say why.
- **Fix:** Run the whole export on one pooled client inside `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY` (read the meta sequences in the same transaction) and COMMIT when the generator finishes. Release the client on stream error or abort. Have the import report the table and line that failed (e.expose) and add an import round-trip test to test/smoke.mjs.

### M83. Restoring an older export (or importing into a live hub) leaves installs' cursors ahead of the restored sequences: they silently skip every new note, link, part, listing, order and partner message numbered at or below their cursor
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/transfer.mjs:175` (hub-core; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Round 3 (28842a1) keeps row seqs and sets change_seq and partner_messages_seq_seq to greatest(meta value, current value, max(seq)). That is correct only when the export was taken just before the import. Restoring a backup taken earlier sets the sequences to the backup's values, while installs have already synced past them (StitchBench keeps its cursor as long as the hub URL is unchanged). /v1/changes, /v1/orders and /v1/partners/messages never notice that `since` is beyond the current sequence value; they return nothing and `next=since`. Importing into a non-empty hub has the same effect: rows get older content with older seqs, and installs never see the rollback.
- **Scenario:** Backup taken at changeSeq 4373. Later edits move installs to cursor 4376. The hub is restored from the backup at the same address. The next changes (new notes, link fixes, new orders, and partner messages such as repair status, estimates and settlements, which use a separate sequence with the same logic) take numbers 4374..4376. Every existing install skips them for good.
- **Fix:** In /v1/changes, /v1/orders and /v1/partners/messages, return `reset: true` when `since` is greater than the sequence's current last_value, and have the bench restart that cursor from 0 (its upserts are idempotent). Alternatively, store a random hub `generation` in settings, change it on every import, return it from /v1/me, and have the bench reset all cursors when it changes. A large setval jump on import is a weaker stopgap.

### M84. Importing into a hub that is already in use keeps old change numbers (regression from 28842a1): restored rows never reach installs, duplicate seqs make paging skip rows, and one matching URL aborts the whole import
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/transfer.mjs:158` (hub-core; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Since 28842a1, a version-3 export row is inserted with its original seq (`kept`), and ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE also copies `seq = EXCLUDED.seq`. This is only correct when the target hub has never handed out change numbers. The admin page presents import as 'Rows with the same id are replaced', which covers restoring a backup into the live hub and loading an export into a hub that was already seeded or joined. In either case: (1) imported or replaced rows get seq values below the cursors installs already hold, so installs never receive them, and a row that was newer on the hub is reverted there while installs keep the newer copy (silent divergence). (2) Two rows can now share a seq across tables, and /v1/changes pages with `seq > since ... LIMIT n`, so a page boundary between equal seqs skips the second row. (3) links are upserted ON CONFLICT (id) only. If the target already has the same URL under another id (both hubs seeded from hub_links.json, or a member added it), the links_url_file unique index aborts the entire import. Before 28842a1, every imported row got nextval, so issues (1) and (2) did not exist.
- **Scenario:** Hub B is in use, and member M has synced to cursor 8. The admin imports an export from hub A (rows seq 3-5). The import reports links 1, posts 2, but M's GET /v1/changes?since=8 returns nothing, so M never sees them. A fresh install paging with limit=1 receives one of the two seq-3 links, next=3, and never receives the other. After a member adds a link to B whose URL also exists in A, the next import attempt fails completely with a 500.
- **Fix:** At the start of importStream, check whether the target is untouched (change_seq is_called = false and all WITH_SEQ tables are empty). Keep row.seq only in that case. Otherwise assign nextval('change_seq') to every imported or updated row (after setval to greatest(meta.changeSeq, current)), so every install receives the imported state once. Apply the same rule to partner_messages. For links, resolve url/file_name conflicts explicitly (skip, or merge into the existing id) instead of letting the unique index abort the transaction, and say in the admin UI that import is meant for an empty hub.

### M85. Community 'most-used parts' list breaks for all members: sum(qty)::int overflows int32
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/usage.mjs:82` (hub-commerce; security, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** GET /v1/usage/top aggregates community usage with `sum(qty)::int` and `sum(tickets)::int` (usage.mjs:82). Per-item qty is capped at 1,000,000 (line 22) and each stored row fits in int4, but the SUM across a member's rows and across months is not bounded and is cast back to int4. Any single member can POST /v1/usage with 2,000 items of qty=1,000,000 for two months (each POST is valid, 200), making sum(qty) = 4e9 which overflows int32. Every member's GET /v1/usage/top then returns 500 ('integer out of range'). The bad data persists, so the community 'Most used parts' page (stitchbench src/app/(app)/parts/most-used/page.tsx:62) is broken for everyone until an admin blocks the offending install (blocked installs are excluded by the query's `NOT i.blocked`, which is the only mitigation) or deletes the rows.
- **Scenario:** Member sends `POST /v1/usage {month:'2026-09', items:[2000 x {number:'EVIL-1', qty:1000000}]}` and the same for 2026-08. Both return 200 ({ok:true,items:1}). Any member then opens the Most-Used page → GET /v1/usage/top → 500 for all callers.
- **Fix:** Aggregate in bigint and clamp before returning: `sum(qty)::bigint` (and tickets), returning the value as a string/number the client can render, or `LEAST(sum(qty), 2147483647)`. Optionally cap a member's total monthly qty. The per-request Map accumulation in usage.mjs:44-49 should also guard against overflow before insert.

### M86. completeSale stores an unlimited number of tenders per sale
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/register.ts:213` (register-money; security; reproduced; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 part 1; verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Sale lines are capped at 300, but input.tenders is not capped. Each tender (with up to 100 characters of note) goes into the sale's JSON column and is later parsed and joined on every render of /register/sales and the receipt. The server-action body limit is 16 MB, so any signed-in user of a shop can store hundreds of thousands of tenders on one sale and slow down that shop's sales pages and exports.
- **Scenario:** A signed-in user replays completeSale with 5,000 one-cent CASH tenders for a $50 sale. It is accepted and stored, and the register-sales list and receipt render all 5,000 entries.
- **Fix:** Reject more than about 10 tenders (throw new UserError("Too many payments on one sale.")), and make sure note is a string before slicing (typeof t.note === "string").


## Low

### L1. Backups skip Listing, the only record of which company owns each marketplace listing, so a restore onto a new PC orphans listings and seller orders on multi-company installs
- **Where:** stitchbench `desktop/backup.mjs:14` (files-library-desktop; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** SKIP includes "Listing", treated as a hub cache. Listing.shopId is set only locally, when a company creates a listing (actions/market.ts:69). After a restore onto a new PC the table is refilled from the hub, and saveListing() assigns shopId only when the install has exactly one company (hub.ts:514, which says so). The restore FIXUP for Listing (backup.mjs:122) runs before any re-sync, while Listing is still empty, so it does nothing.
- **Scenario:** A Windows program with several companies (platformAdminEmails) loses its PC and is restored from backup. Every company's own listings come back with shopId NULL. They vanish from My listings, ownListing() refuses edits and removals ('That listing isn't one of yours.'), and new seller orders for them get shopId NULL (saveOrder -> listing.shopId null -> soleShopId() null), so no company can see those sales.
- **Fix:** Back up Listing, or at least (id, shopId, mine), and restore shopId onto re-synced rows. A cleaner option is to send a shopRef with each listing to the hub, as partner links already do.

### L2. Nightly/startup backup is queued again every 20 min while a long backup is still running
- **Where:** stitchbench `desktop/main.mjs:431` (files-library-desktop; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The 20-minute timer calls runBackup('nightly') whenever lastBackupTime() is older than 1 am. lastBackupTime() sees only completed folders (manifest.json present), and runBackup does not check `backingUp`. backup() then chains each extra call behind the running one. A backup longer than 20 minutes (large photo tables, NAS or USB target) therefore gets 1 to N full duplicate backups right after it. Each duplicate takes a slot of backupsToKeep, which shortens retention, and `backingUp` flips back to false while queued runs remain.
- **Scenario:** A shop with a few GB of machine photos backs up to a NAS in 50 minutes. The 1:20 and 1:40 ticks each queue another full backup, so three backups run back to back every night. The 30-slot retention covers 10 days instead of 30.
- **Fix:** In the interval (and the startup timeout), return early when `backingUp` is true, or keep a 'scheduled' flag. Alternatively record the start time of the running backup and compare against that.

### L3. `npm run lint` fails on every checkout: generated pdf.js files in public/ are not ignored (the other 8 errors are false positives), and lint runs nowhere in CI
- **Where:** stitchbench `eslint.config.mjs:9` (build-test-infra; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The postinstall script copies pdf.js into public/pdf.worker.min.mjs and public/pdfjs/** (both gitignored). globalIgnores only lists .next, out, build, next-env.d.ts and src/generated, so ESLint lints the minified vendor code. After `node desktop/prepare-app.mjs`, desktop/server/** is also linted: its .next folder is not matched by the root-relative `.next/**`, and node_modules is renamed to `_node_modules`, so the default node_modules ignore does not apply. The remaining 8 errors are rule false positives: `<a href="/api/export/...">` downloads of route handlers flagged by no-html-link-for-pages, and a ref object from useBarcodeCamera() passed to `ref=` flagged by react-hooks/refs. They are not product bugs, but they keep the lint gate permanently red, which is probably why no workflow runs it.
- **Scenario:** A developer runs `npm run lint` on a clean clone after `npm install` and gets 18 errors and about 1,750 warnings, most from vendor code. Real new lint errors are lost in the noise, and after a desktop build lint runs over the whole standalone server bundle.
- **Fix:** Add "public/pdf.worker.min.mjs", "public/pdfjs/**", "desktop/server/**", "desktop/dist*/**" to globalIgnores. Silence the two false-positive rules at those lines (eslint-disable-next-line with a reason) or destructure `const { video } = cam`. Add `npm run lint` and `tsc --noEmit` to a CI job.

### L4. INSTALL.md still promises a sign-in lockout that round 2 removed; the Windows program has no per-device limit at all
- **Where:** stitchbench `INSTALL.md:49` (files-library-desktop; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** INSTALL.md (Other devices) says: 'After 10 wrong passwords for one email (or 50 from one device) in 15 minutes, sign-in pauses for that email or device.' Round 2 (9ba25a4) replaced that with pacing after 5 failures per account (the right password still works), and a per-address limit only when TRUST_PROXY=true, which the Windows program never sets (main.mjs:269-284). So on the LAN program nothing limits one device guessing across many accounts, and the owner's risk judgement (shared customer Wi-Fi) rests on a limit that no longer exists. Round 2 changed the code but not this paragraph.
- **Scenario:** An owner reads INSTALL.md, relies on 'sign-in pauses for that device', and keeps customers on the same Wi-Fi. A customer device can run slow password guessing against every staff email in parallel with no per-device cap.
- **Fix:** Update INSTALL.md to describe the real behaviour (per-account pacing after 5 wrong passwords; no per-device limit on the shop PC). Consider a per-socket-address limit on the Windows program, where req.socket.remoteAddress is trustworthy because there is no proxy.

### L5. Server actions allow 16 MB, but the proxy only buffers 10 MB: 10-16 MB posts fail with a 500
- **Where:** stitchbench `next.config.ts:7` (auth-accounts; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** next.config.ts raises serverActions.bodySizeLimit to 16 MB for intake and marketplace photos. Every page path goes through proxy.ts, though, and Next 16 buffers at most proxyClientMaxBodySize (10 MB by default) there. Anything larger reaches the action truncated and fails with 'Failed to parse body as FormData', which is a 500 and the error page, not the friendly size message. Today's shrunk photos stay under 10 MB, but the configured limit can't be reached.
- **Scenario:** A marketplace listing with 8 large photos, or any action body over 10 MB, gets a 500 'This page couldn't load' instead of saving or a clear 'too large' message.
- **Fix:** Make the two limits agree: set experimental.proxyClientMaxBodySize to the same value (16mb), or lower bodySizeLimit below 10mb. Also check the size client-side before sending.

### L6. Round-1 migration assigns existing marketplace listings/orders to a company only on single-company installs; on hosted multi-company installs they become orphaned
- **Where:** stitchbench `prisma/migrations/20260926090000_review_fixes/migration.sql:43` (build-test-infra; qa; confirmed)
- **Status:** OPEN (low pass: the applied migration must not change; hub sends shopRef now, so rows it knows get their company at the next sync; older rows by hand (SQL in the low-pass notes) or an "unassigned" picker on Companies)
- **Problem:** The round-1 tenant-isolation fix added nullable Listing.shopId and MarketOrder.shopId, and backfills them only when exactly one Shop exists. On a hosted install with several companies, every listing created before the upgrade keeps shopId NULL. ownListing() then refuses edits and removal for every company ('That listing isn't one of yours'), /market/mine and the orders page filter by shopId and hide them, and saveOrder() inherits the listing's NULL shopId, so a new order from another shop on such a listing is invisible to the seller. saveListing() does the same for listings that come back from the hub on multi-company installs ('it stays unassigned'). No UI exists to assign an owner.
- **Scenario:** A hosted StitchBench with two companies had company A's listing live before upgrading. After the upgrade, shop B on the hub orders it. The order syncs with shopId NULL, company A never sees 'REQUESTED' in Market -> Orders, and the buyer waits indefinitely. Company A also cannot pause or remove the listing.
- **Fix:** For multi-company installs, backfill from the hub's own data where possible (e.g. the listing's creator), or show unassigned listings/orders to the install admin with a 'belongs to company' picker. At minimum, list them on the Companies page so a platform admin can assign them.

### L7. Email uniqueness across the whole install lets anyone probe which emails have an account at any shop, and lets one shop claim another shop's staff email
- **Where:** stitchbench `prisma/schema.prisma:82` (data-model; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** OPEN (low pass: per-company email uniqueness or invite/verification: schema and sign-in change, owner decision)
- **Problem:** User.email is @unique across all companies. /signup (page.tsx:36) and createShopWithOwner (shops.ts:44) say "That email already has an account." before anything else, which undoes the login's careful timing equalization (auth.ts DUMMY_HASH). Any shop admin gets the same answer from addUser (settings.ts:104) for people in other companies. A shop admin can also create logins for another shop's staff emails, and that shop then cannot add those people.
- **Scenario:** With ALLOW_SIGNUP=true, an anonymous visitor submits /signup with candidate emails and a valid password. 'taken' confirms an account at some shop on the install, including a tech at a different company. Separately, shop A's admin adds bob@shopB.example as a TECH; shop B can no longer add Bob.
- **Fix:** Give the same response whether or not the email exists (for example create-then-verify by email, or a generic 'couldn't create the account' with per-IP pacing like the login). In addUser, say only that the email can't be used. If people at several hosted shops are expected, consider @@unique([shopId, email]) with a shop picker at login.

### L8. Missing indexes on ticket/sale child tables and shop-scoped tables (every ticket page scans the whole shared table)
- **Where:** stitchbench `prisma/schema.prisma:335` (data-model; security, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** Postgres does not index foreign-key columns automatically, and the schema adds none for the hottest lookups. There is no index on TicketLine.ticketId (line 335), Payment.ticketId (358), SaleLine.saleId (783), StockMove.partId or shopId (459, used by the part page and the 'most used' report), ServiceItem.shopId (369), Ticket.machineId or Ticket.customerId, Machine.customerId, Payment.paidAt (sales report), or PartnerOutbox shopId/linkId. On the hosted multi-shop app these tables hold every company's rows, so each ticket or sale page and each report does a sequential scan whose cost grows with all tenants' data.
- **Scenario:** A hosted install with many shops builds up hundreds of thousands of ticket lines and payments. Every ticket page load (include: lines, payments), cascade delete and sales report scans them all, and one busy tenant slows every other tenant.
- **Fix:** Add @@index([ticketId]) to TicketLine and Payment, @@index([saleId]) to SaleLine, @@index([shopId, partId, createdAt]) to StockMove, @@index([shopId]) to ServiceItem, @@index([machineId]) and @@index([customerId]) to Ticket, @@index([customerId]) to Machine, and @@index([shopId, linkId, sentAt]) to PartnerOutbox, in a new migration (CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY where possible).

### L9. Unique key is case-sensitive while the app treats part numbers case-insensitively (check-then-create race)
- **Where:** stitchbench `prisma/schema.prisma:421` (parts-inventory; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** OPEN (low pass: a case-insensitive unique index would fail on installs that already hold case-variant duplicates; needs a cleanup step first)
- **Problem:** @@unique([shopId, partNumber]) is case-sensitive, but createPart, updatePart and the import enforce 'the same number in another case is the same part' with a read and then a separate create or update. Two concurrent creates of 'abc-1' and 'ABC-1' both pass the check and both insert. Two concurrent creates with the same case hit P2002, which surfaces as a generic 500 instead of the friendly message.
- **Scenario:** Two counter users add the same new item at the same moment, one typing 'xa1234' and the other 'XA1234'. The result is two separate inventory items; the scanner and tickets then pick one arbitrarily, and stock is split.
- **Fix:** Add a unique index on ("shopId", lower("partNumber")) in a migration (the '(own)' and '(Partner)' suffixes already keep consigned numbers distinct), and map P2002 to a UserError in createPart, updatePart and the import.

### L10. scripts/e2e-setup.ts stores E2E_EMAIL as typed, so a mixed-case email makes a login that can never sign in
- **Where:** stitchbench `scripts/e2e-setup.ts:11` (gap; qa, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (a5964f6)
- **Problem:** The e2e setup script looks up and creates the user with the email exactly as given, but sign-in lowercases the address before findUnique. Any E2E_EMAIL with a capital letter creates or resets a user nobody can sign in as, and every spec then fails at login with 'Email or password is wrong'. That is a confusing red suite, and it leaves a stray 'E2E Test Shop' company each time. The script also resets the password of any existing email, including a real owner, when pointed at a live DATABASE_URL.
- **Scenario:** Reproduced: `E2E_EMAIL=qa-crA2@test.local E2E_PASSWORD=qa-pass-12345 npx tsx scripts/e2e-setup.ts` prints 'E2E user ready' and stores email 'qa-crA2@test.local'. Signing in with that email and password at /login -> /login?error=1.
- **Fix:** Lowercase and trim E2E_EMAIL in the script, and refuse to reset a user that isn't in an 'E2E Test Shop' company.

### L11. Import holds row locks on every updated item for the whole (up to 180 s) transaction, stalling register sales
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/inventory-import.ts:198` (parts-inventory; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The round-2 all-or-nothing import runs every row's update inside one interactive transaction (timeout 180 s). Each tx.part.update locks that item until commit. A register sale of any already-updated item waits on the lock inside the register's own interactive transaction, which has Prisma's default 5 s timeout and has already locked the Shop row for nextSaleNumber. The sale fails and other register sales queue behind the Shop lock.
- **Scenario:** During opening hours the owner imports a 5,000-row price update (tens of seconds; 2000 rows took 16 s here). A customer at the register buys an item updated early in the import: the sale hangs, then fails with a transaction-expired error, and the next sales wait behind it.
- **Fix:** Read and plan outside the transaction, then apply writes in short chunked transactions keyed by an import id (which also gives the idempotency needed above). Alternatively do the update phase as a single set-based SQL statement so the locks are held only briefly.

### L12. A TECH can silently set shop-wide payment instructions and redirect marketplace payments when accepting an order
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/market.ts:199` (hub-market; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by the merged fix-agent/hub-core work; re-checked in round-3 part 2)
- **Problem:** orderAction only requires requireUser() + ownOrder(), so any TECH (not just owner/admin) can accept a marketplace sale. On accept, the payInstructions the tech types become the invoice the buyer pays, and if the shop has no payInstructions yet the action persists the tech's text as the shop-wide default (`if (!shop.payInstructions) await db.shop.update(... payInstructions: pay)`). saveShop, the normal way to set payInstructions, is gated by requireAdmin(); this path bypasses that gate. A tech can therefore (a) direct a buyer's payment to their own personal account on any order they accept, and (b) permanently set the shop's default payee without owner/admin review.
- **Scenario:** A TECH opens a REQUESTED sale and accepts it with payInstructions 'PayPal: tina.personal@evil.test'. The buyer's invoice and order page show that as how to pay the shop, and because the shop's payInstructions were empty they are now saved shop-wide for future invoices — all without any admin action.
- **Fix:** Require owner/admin (requireAdmin) to accept sales or to change payInstructions, or at least do not silently persist the shop-wide default from an order-accept; keep the accept-time value scoped to that order and surface any change of the shop default to an admin.

### L13. Listing price and quantity parsing: 19.999 silently becomes $20.00, 1.5 becomes 2, and large prices get an opaque hub 500
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/market.ts:32` (hub-market; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** createListing uses toCents, which rounds any number of decimals, and int(), which rounds with Math.round. Neither has an upper bound. The hub columns are int4, so a price of $21,474,836.48 or more makes Postgres throw and the user sees 'Something went wrong on the hub.' The same parsing applies to the accept form's shipping (market.ts:201): 'abc' falls back to the listing's flat rate without saying so.
- **Scenario:** A seller mistypes 19.999 and lists at $20.00 without being told. A quantity of 1.5 becomes 2 for sale. Typing 99999999 (an extra 9) gives a meaningless hub error.
- **Fix:** Reject more than 2 decimals and non-integer quantities with a UserError, cap prices and shipping (for example at $1,000,000), and validate the same bounds on the hub so it answers 400 instead of 500.

### L14. Consignment returns and counts accept fractional quantities for piece items, and the local return is committed separately from the message to the repair shop
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/partner-orders.ts:219` (partners; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** returnConsigned takes qty = Number(form) with no rounding (line 219), so '1.5' presser feet can go back. The repair side rounds with qtyOf to 3 decimals, so yard values like 0.33333 disagree slightly between the two books. countConsigned also stores fractional counts for piece items. consignReturned commits in its own transaction (line 222) and sendToPartner runs afterwards outside it (line 224). If the outbox insert fails, the sewing shop's shelf and stock change but the repair shop never hears about it.
- **Scenario:** A clerk types 0.5 to return half a pack. The sewing shop's consigned part goes to x.5 on hand and the repair shop's stock goes up by 0.5 of a piece item.
- **Fix:** Use the part's unit, as placeConsignment does: Math.round for pieces and Math.round(x*1000)/1000 for yards, and reject non-integers for pieces (also in countConsigned). Pass a tx into consignReturned and call sendToPartner(..., tx) in the same transaction.

### L15. 'Set up StitchShop for a sewing shop' isn't atomic: a hub failure leaves an orphan company and a retry is refused
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/partners.ts:142` (partners; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** setUpSewingShop creates the company and its owner login first, then calls the hub (createPartnerInvite, redeemPartnerCode). If the hub is offline or refuses, the company exists without a partnership. Running the form again fails with 'That email already has an account', and the page gives no hint about how to finish the connection.
- **Scenario:** The hub times out during set-up. A company with an owner login exists but isn't partnered, and the repair shop's retry is blocked by the duplicate email.
- **Fix:** Create the invite before the company (it is cheap), or catch hub errors after creation and return a clear message with a path to finish (e.g. make a code for the new company). Alternatively, make the form idempotent for an existing retail company with no links.

### L16. Negative cost, price and partner (wholesale) price are accepted for parts; a negative price becomes a hidden credit on tickets and makes a partner reject the whole catalog
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/parts.ts:41` (parts-inventory; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** partData uses toCents() for cost, price and wholesale, and toCents keeps the sign ('-5.00' becomes -500). importItems likewise accepts any finite costCents or priceCents (inventory-import.ts:63-64), and addPartSource accepts negative prices. The register refuses negative unit prices (register.ts:178), but scanCode ticket mode (scan.ts:65) and addLine price a ticket line at part.priceCents, so a negative part price quietly lowers the ticket total. catalogFor sends wholesaleCents and priceCents of offered items to partners. The receiving side rejects out-of-range values with int(..., 0, MAX_MONEY) (partners.ts:741-742), which fails the entire catalog message, so one bad item stops the partner seeing any catalog update.
- **Scenario:** A shop mistypes '-12.50' as a part's price or partner price, or a supplier sheet has a credit line. Scanning the part onto a ticket in 'Use on ticket' mode adds a -$12.50 line. If the part is offered to partners, the partner's copy of the catalog stops updating, with a quarantined 'partner price is out of range' message.
- **Fix:** In partData, importItems and addPartSource reject negative amounts (and amounts above a sane maximum such as partners' MAX_MONEY), e.g. a nonNegativeCents() helper that throws a UserError. Consider making the partner catalog sender skip, rather than send, items that fail the receiver's rules.

### L17. A TECH can get around the register's owner-only price change by editing or clearing the part's price
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/parts.ts:67` (parts-inventory; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** register.ts:191 enforces `if (user.role === "TECH" && part.priceCents != null && unit !== part.priceCents) throw 'Only an owner or admin can change the price'`. updatePart only calls requireUser(), so a TECH can set the part's price to anything, or blank it (priceCents = null, which turns the check off completely), ring the sale, and put the price back. updatePart also lets a TECH change the wholesale price and 'Offer to partner shops', which are commercial terms other shops see. (The register's line discounts appear to have no TECH limit either; that is outside this area.)
- **Scenario:** A TECH clears the Price on item X (Details, Save) and sells X at the register for $0.01. The check at register.ts:191 is skipped because part.priceCents is null.
- **Fix:** If price changes are meant to be owner/admin-only, have updatePart refuse changes to priceCents, costCents, wholesaleCents and offerToPartners when user.role === "TECH", and hide those inputs for techs. Otherwise drop the register check so the policy is consistent.

### L18. Stock and money inputs accept negative, fractional-piece and NaN values (shelf count -7, price -$12.50, 2.5 'each', NaN on hand)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/parts.ts:85` (parts-inventory; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** moveStock count mode stores whatever number is typed, negatives included (the scan-station count clamps to 0, the part page doesn't). qtyValue (line 15) rounds the opening quantity to thousandths whatever the unit, so an 'each' part can be created with 2.5 on hand, while moveStock and scanCode insist on whole pieces for the same item. toCents accepts negatives, so cost, price, partner price and supplier price can be stored below zero. A negative price flows straight onto tickets and register lines. saveCounts (scan.ts:86) and scanCode do Math.round/Math.max on client values without a finiteness check, so a crafted call with qty NaN writes NaN to Part.qtyOnHand, and from then on the inventory value shows '$NaN'.
- **Scenario:** 1) Add part NEG-PRICE-1 with Cost -5, Price -12.50, Quantity on hand 2.5 (kind Part, sold each). It is saved with costCents -500, priceCents -1250 and '2.50 on hand'. 2) On its page, Shelf count -7 > 'Count updated', and the page shows '-7 on hand'. 3) Replay saveCounts([{partId, qty: NaN}]) > {saved:1}. qtyOnHand is NaN, and /parts?q=QTY- shows '4 items · $NaN on the shelf at cost'.
- **Fix:** In moveStock, reject count < 0. In createPart, round qtyOnHand to whole pieces unless unit === 'yd'. Reject negative (and absurdly large) cost, price, wholesale and source prices in partData and addPartSource. In saveCounts and scanCode, skip or reject entries where !Number.isFinite(Number(c.qty)).

### L19. Add part with a number that already exists (qty 0) redirects to the old item and silently drops everything typed
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/parts.ts:54` (parts-inventory; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** When the number exists and no quantity is entered, createPart redirects to the existing item with no message. The new description, price, bin and other fields are thrown away, and the user lands on a page that looks like a successful save.
- **Scenario:** /parts > Add part: number 3148 (exists), description 'NEW DESCRIPTION typed by user', price 9.99, bin Z9 > Add part. Observed: lands on '3148 Clover Seam Ripper' with price 8.25, bin empty and no toast. Expected: a message such as '3148 is already in inventory: opened it, nothing changed' or an offer to update it.
- **Fix:** Throw a UserError naming the existing item, with a link to it, instead of redirecting silently, or redirect with a flag that shows 'Already in inventory: nothing was changed'.

### L20. The register trusts the browser's 'taxable' flag on every stocked-item line
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/register.ts:205` (gap; security; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** completeSale sets taxable from the client (`taxable: job ? false : Boolean(l.taxable)`) for every line, including lines tied to a stocked part. Part has no taxable setting on the server, and the UI always sends true for parts, so the server has no rule to check against. Anyone who can ring a sale, including a TECH, can mark taxable items as untaxed or refund tax on a return that was never charged. This is separate from the discount and refund controls already reported.
- **Scenario:** A tech rings up $200 of taxable notions with taxable:false on each line. The sale saves with $0 tax, and the shop under-collects sales tax that it still owes.
- **Fix:** For lines with a partId, decide taxable on the server (always true, or a Part.taxable column). Only accept the client flag for misc lines, and optionally only from owner/admin.

### L21. Yard quantities aren't normalized on the server; the partner rounds them to 3 decimals, so consigned sold counts drift
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/register.ts:190` (register-money; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** For unit 'yd' completeSale accepts any finite qty (e.g. 0.3333 or 1.0000001; the UI step is 0.125 but free typing is allowed). The value is stored as-is in SaleLine.qty, Part.qtyOnHand and ConsignItem.sold. The repair shop's handler parses the same message with qtyOf (Math.round(x*1000)/1000), so each side records a different sold count and gross. Later return guards (holder: sold >= -qty; owner: item.sold < -qty) can then disagree, and the partner dead-letters the message.
- **Scenario:** The cashier types 0.3333 yd of a consigned $100.00/yd fabric. The sewing shop records sold += 0.3333 and gross round(0.3333*10000) = 3333; the repair shop records sold += 0.333 and gross 3330, so statements differ by $0.03 and the counts no longer match.
- **Fix:** On the server, normalize yard quantities (e.g. `qty = Math.round(qty * 8) / 8` to match the 1/8-yd UI, or at least Math.round(qty*1000)/1000 to match the partner) before any maths, and reject a line whose qty rounds to 0.

### L22. Quantities stored as Float drift, so a legitimate consigned return is refused
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/register.ts:281` (data-model; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round3-tail: the consign guards in lib/partners.ts use inEighths + 1e-6 slack like the register; the Float column is left as is by design)
- **Problem:** Part.qtyOnHand, StockMove.qty and ConsignItem.placed/sold/returned are DOUBLE PRECISION and updated with SQL increments. Yardage is only rounded to 1/1000 on input, and the register's qty box accepts any typed value (step=0.125 is not enforced on a controlled input). So non-binary fractions accumulate error. The consignment guards compare the drifted value exactly: `sold: { gte: -l.qty }` in completeSale, and `if (item.sold < -qty) return bad(...)` on the repair side (src/lib/partners.ts:973).
- **Scenario:** A sewing shop sells 0.7 yd of a consigned fabric, and the customer returns 0.4 yd: sold = 0.7 + (-0.4) = 0.29999999999999993. The customer then returns the remaining 0.3 yd. The register refuses with 'More of <item> is being returned than was sold on consignment.', and the repair shop would quarantine such a message as bad data.
- **Fix:** Store quantities as integer thousandths (Int) or Decimal(12,3). Failing that, round after each increment (`ROUND((sold + x)::numeric, 3)`) and compare with a small tolerance (sold >= -qty - 1e-6).

### L23. Paying a repair at the register marks it collected without checking that it's back from the repair shop
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/register.ts:167` (register-money; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** completeSale checks only that a repair job is this shop's retail job and unpaid (lines 167-171). With pickedUp (the default), it then sets status COLLECTED and pickedUpAt and sends job.pickedup (lines 292-304), whatever the job's current status. markPickedUp in repair-jobs.ts:167 refuses unless the status is READY_IN_TRUCK, READY_FOR_PICKUP or RETURNED_UNREPAIRED, and the repair side ignores job.pickedup for any other status (partners.ts:833-839). A stale Repair pickup list, or a crafted call, therefore leaves the sewing shop showing 'Customer has it' while the repair shop still has the machine on the bench, and the two shops disagree from then on.
- **Scenario:** The pickup list is opened while tag 105 is READY_FOR_PICKUP. The repair shop moves it back to ON_BENCH (another fault found) and that syncs. The clerk still clicks 'Add $120' and completes the sale. The retail job becomes COLLECTED and pickedUpAt is set, while the repair ticket stays ON_BENCH.
- **Fix:** In completeSale, allow pickedUp only when job.status is one of the ready statuses (or throw 'It isn't back from the repair shop yet; untick taking the machine home'). Keep RETURNED_UNREPAIRED instead of overwriting it with COLLECTED, as the repair side does.

### L24. The register never checks stock on hand: concurrent or repeated sales take stock negative, consigned stock included
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/register.ts:257` (register-money; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The stock decrement is atomic, so concurrent sales lose no updates. But nothing compares the quantity to qtyOnHand, and the cart gives no warning. For the shop's own stock this may be deliberate. For consigned stock, which belongs to the partner, it means recording more sold than was placed (consignItem.sold > placed), and the repair side accepts it. Negative counts also feed the itemByCode ranking bug reported separately.
- **Scenario:** QALAST has qtyOnHand 0. Four registers sell it at once, all four succeed, and qtyOnHand becomes -4.
- **Fix:** Warn in the cart when a line's qty exceeds on-hand, since RegisterItem already carries qtyOnHand. For consigned parts, refuse server-side unless `sold + qty <= placed - returned`, using an updateMany with that condition as round 2 did for returns.

### L25. Sewing-shop intake (takeInMachine) creates the customer before validating the problem, machine and photos, so every corrected retry duplicates the customer
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/repair-jobs.ts:55` (bench-workflow; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** takeInMachine creates the customer at line 55 and only afterwards checks brand/model (61), problem (64), photo type/size (71), photo count (74) and total photo size (76). All of these throw UserError after the customer exists. The IntakeForm keeps what was typed by design, so the counter clerk fixes the one problem and submits again, and a second customer with the same name and phone is created. Whitespace-only text passes the browser's `required` check, so this is easy to trigger. So does 'The photos are too large together', which people will commonly hit with 6 phone photos.
- **Scenario:** At /repairs/new the clerk enters 'Jane', 555-0199, Janome MC9400 and a problem of '   ', then submits twice. 'Say what's wrong with it...' appears twice, no repair job is created, and there are now 2 customer records 'QA Intake Jane / 555-0199'. After fixing the problem and submitting, there are 3.
- **Fix:** Move every validation (brand, model, problem, photos) before any write, and create the customer inside the same db.$transaction as the machine and repair job.

### L26. Stock counts accept NaN, Infinity and negative values (saveCounts, Set count, import), storing NaN/Infinity quantities and a '$NaN' inventory value
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/scan.ts:86` (parts-inventory; security, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** saveCounts takes counts[].qty straight from the client. React's reply encoding delivers NaN and Infinity ('$NaN', '$Infinity'). `Math.max(0, Math.round(NaN))` is NaN and `p.qtyOnHand === n` is false, so NaN or Infinity is written to Part.qtyOnHand (Float) and to StockMove.qty. Postgres float8 stores NaN and Infinity. Once qtyOnHand is NaN, every later increment or decrement (receive, sale, ticket) leaves it NaN. moveStock count mode (parts.ts:90-96) accepts any finite number, including negatives from the real form and 1e300. Receive accepts 1e300. importItems (inventory-import.ts:77) accepts any positive finite qty.
- **Scenario:** A user (or a bug in the client) posts saveCounts([{partId, qty: NaN}]). The part shows 'NaN on hand' and the parts list header reads '$NaN on the shelf at cost' until someone does a manual count. Set count of '-5' stores -5 on hand. qty 1e300 makes the inventory value astronomically wrong. A stock level above 100,000 also makes a partner reject the whole catalog (qtyOf in partners.ts:64).
- **Fix:** Validate each quantity on the server: `const q = Number(c.qty); if (!Number.isFinite(q) || q < 0 || q > 100_000) skip/reject`. In moveStock count mode require 0 <= n <= a sane maximum. Put an upper bound on receive and import quantities. Optionally add a Postgres CHECK ("qtyOnHand" = "qtyOnHand") to reject NaN.

### L27. Scan-station 'Count shelf' (and the item page's Count) change a partner's consigned item behind the consignment's back
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/scan.ts:85` (gap; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** Consigned stock has its own count flow. countConsigned (partner-orders.ts:229) updates ConsignItem.countedQty and sends 'consign.count' to the repair shop. The generic counts skip all of that. scanCode lets consigned items through in 'lookup' mode, which the scan station uses for 'Count shelf', and saveCounts only skips yard goods (scan.ts:85). moveStock's count branch (parts.ts:92) doesn't check consignLinkId either. Counting the whole shelf at the scan station therefore overwrites a consigned item's qtyOnHand with an ADJUSTED move. The ConsignItem (placed - sold - returned), the consignment page and the repair shop are never told. This is a different path from the reported parts.ts:80 issue (receiving own stock into a consigned item).
- **Scenario:** A sewing shop scans its whole shelf in Count shelf mode, which includes 3 of the repair shop's consigned feet that the ConsignItem says should be 5. saveCounts sets qtyOnHand to 3. The register and parts list now say 3, the consignment page still says 5 on the shelf, and the repair shop's books still expect 5 back. The 2-unit shrink never reaches the partner statement.
- **Fix:** In saveCounts and moveStock's count mode, refuse consigned items with a message pointing to Partners → Consignment → Count, or route them through countConsigned so the ConsignItem and the partner are updated.

### L28. Owners and admins can set their own password through 'Reset password' without the current one
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/settings.ts:93` (auth-accounts; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** changePassword requires the current password. resetUserPassword allows u.id === admin.id for the OWNER and places no restriction on an ADMIN resetting themselves. The Settings page shows 'Reset password' on the signed-in owner's own row (settings/page.tsx:314). Anyone with a live owner or admin session (an unlocked counter PC, or a stolen cookie; see the LAN note) can set a new password without knowing the old one and keep the account for good. The sessionVersion bump also signs the real owner out everywhere.
- **Scenario:** The owner leaves the counter PC signed in. Someone opens Settings → People → the owner's row → Reset password and types a new one. The real owner is signed out everywhere and can't get back in, and the shop has no path to recover the owner account.
- **Fix:** In resetUserPassword, refuse userId === admin.id and send people to My password (changePassword). Or require the admin's own current password for any reset of an OWNER or ADMIN account.

### L29. saveShop stores unchecked tax, markup, day counts and ticket numbers (negative, huge or out of Int range)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/settings.ts:38` (auth-accounts; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** num() takes any finite Number: taxRatePct and partsMarkupPct can be negative or 1e300, and warrantyDays and abandonedAfterDays can be negative. nextTicketNumber is only floored at 1, so '1e12' goes past Postgres int4, Prisma throws a non-UserError, and the admin sees a generic server error instead of a message. An empty field becomes 0 (Number("") === 0), so clearing the tax box sets tax to 0 without saying so. toCents(laborRate or storageFee) accepts negatives too.
- **Scenario:** An admin types '-8.25' or '825' for tax. Every later invoice and register sale computes negative or 825% tax, and the wrong totals go into sales and exports. Or they type 99999999999 as the next ticket number and the save fails with a 500.
- **Fix:** Validate ranges and throw a UserError: tax 0-30 %, markup 0-1000 %, day counts 0-3650 as integers, nextTicketNumber 1 to 2_147_483_647, money at least 0. Keep the old value when a field is left empty.

### L30. saveShop turns a blank field into 0 (Number("") === 0): blank 'Next ticket number' resets numbering to 1; tax, markup and day counts are not range-checked
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/settings.ts:26` (auth-accounts; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** `num(k, d)` computes `Number(str(formData.get(k)) ?? "")`. str() returns null for a blank field, so this is Number("") = 0, which is finite, and the fallback d is never used for blank input. A cleared field saves 0: tax 0%, markup 0%, warranty 0 days, abandonedAfterDays 0, and nextTicketNumber max(1, 0) = 1. No range checks: negative tax or markup are accepted, and values beyond Int32 (e.g. 1e12 for warranty or ticket number) make Prisma throw a non-UserError, so the user gets a generic 500 instead of a message.
- **Scenario:** An admin clears 'Next ticket number' (expecting blank to mean 'leave it') and saves. The counter becomes 1. The next check-in (tickets.ts:104-106) walks past every used number, one UPDATE plus one SELECT per existing ticket, inside a single interactive $transaction with Prisma's default 5 s timeout. A shop with thousands of tickets may hit the timeout, which rolls the counter back to 1 so every later check-in fails the same way (this timeout part is not verified). Separately, entering -8 for tax gives negative tax on every new ticket and register sale.
- **Fix:** In num(), return d when str() is null: `const s = str(formData.get(k)); if (s == null) return d; const v = Number(s); ...`. Validate ranges with a UserError: tax 0-100, markup 0-1000, days 0-3650, and nextTicketNumber an integer from 1 to 2^31-1 that is not below the highest existing ticket number (or warn). Check Int limits for the cents fields too.

### L31. Global unique User.email lets any company's admin probe or squat email addresses belonging to other companies
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/settings.ts:104` (data-model; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** OPEN (low pass: same as L7 (owner decision))
- **Problem:** User.email is @unique across the whole install (schema.prisma:82), because login is by email. addUser (any company's OWNER/ADMIN) and /signup answer 'That email already has an account.' for an address used in ANY company. Neither verifies that the address belongs to the person being added. On a hosted multi-company install, an admin of company A can confirm whether a given person has a login anywhere on the install. They can also pre-register another company's staff or owner addresses as disabled techs in their own shop, which blocks that company from ever adding them or signing them up.
- **Scenario:** The owner of shop A adds 'tech@competitor.com' with a throwaway password. The competitor, on the same hosted install, can no longer add that tech ('That email already has an account.') and has no way to reclaim the address. Shop A's admin can likewise test arbitrary addresses and learn which are StitchBench users.
- **Fix:** Bind an address to a login only after the person accepts an emailed invite or verification link. Until then, keep pending invites per company, so one company can't block or enumerate another's addresses. At least return a neutral message and rate-limit the lookups.

### L32. Add person, sign-up and Companies accept emails that sign-in rejects, making logins that can never be used
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/settings.ts:100` (auth-accounts; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (a5964f6)
- **Problem:** addUser only lowercases and trims the email; createShopWithOwner (sign-up, Companies, setUpSewingShop) does the same. Sign-in validates with zod `z.string().email()` (auth.ts:9), which rejects these addresses, so the account can never sign in. The email column is unique and there is no delete, so the address stays taken. Browsers block these through type=email, but the server doesn't: replayed or scripted posts, or a browser that doesn't enforce it, get through.
- **Scenario:** An admin adds 'qa-auth-bob' (no domain) or 'qa-äuth-ü@test.local' and gets 'Account created. Give them the temporary password.' The person can never sign in; the admin can't correct the email and can't re-add it.
- **Fix:** Validate emails server-side with the same zod schema login uses, in addUser and in createShopWithOwner, and throw UserError("Enter a valid email.").

### L33. Deleting a note while it is uploading still publishes it, and then its author can't remove it
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/shared.ts:55` (hub-market; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** sharedNotes offers a Remove button on local notes still marked 'Sending to the community hub'. deleteSharedPost deletes the SharedPost with no check that pushPending is uploading it. pushPending has already picked the note and posts it, then `db.sharedPost.update({ where: { id: p.id }, data: { hubId: id } })` throws P2025. That exception stops pushing the rest of the batch and is only logged. The next feed brings the note back as a hub post marked mine. removeHubPost then finds no local copy (`own` null). On a multi-company install only the platform admin can remove it, and on a single-company install a TECH author cannot.
- **Scenario:** A tech shares a note with photos, realizes it shows a customer's label, and clicks Remove within the few seconds of the upload. The note is published to every shop anyway, and the tech cannot take it down.
- **Fix:** Instead of deleting, mark the note 'withdrawn'. pushPending skips withdrawn notes, and if one was uploaded anyway, it sends DELETE /v1/posts/<id> and then removes the local row. Also use updateMany in pushPending, so a missing row doesn't stop the loop.

### L34. Shared note bodies over 5000 characters are cut off silently, with a success toast
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/shared.ts:38` (hub-market; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** sharePost stores body.slice(0, 5000) (the hub repeats the cut), and the share-form textarea has no maxLength (src/components/share-form.tsx:43). Listings behave the same way: the hub truncates the title to 160 and the description to 4000 characters with no warning, and the ListingForm inputs have no maxLength.
- **Scenario:** A tech writes a detailed 6,000-character fix procedure and shares it. They see 'Shared with every shop', but the last steps are gone for every shop.
- **Fix:** Add maxLength={5000} (and 160 or 4000 on the listing fields) and a character counter, or raise a UserError instead of slicing.

### L35. Typing paper ticket number 2147483646 permanently breaks auto-numbered check-in (500 on every new ticket)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/tickets.ts:97` (bench-workflow; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The typed 'Paper ticket number' is only checked as a positive integer (`Number.isInteger(typedNumber) && typedNumber > 0`). The column is Postgres int4. 2147483646 is accepted, and line 129 moves nextTicketNumber to 2147483647. Every later check-in with the number left blank runs `nextTicketNumber: { increment: 1 }` (line 104) and fails with 'integer out of range', which surfaces as an HTTP 500. The only fix is typing numbers by hand or editing the database. Incoming partner jobs use the same counter increment (newJobFromPartner, src/lib/partners.ts:1062), so they fail too and are set aside after 3 tries. Any role can do this, and values of 2147483647 or more produce a raw 500 instead of a friendly error.
- **Scenario:** A TECH enters 2147483646 as the paper ticket number (maliciously or by pasting a long number). From then on 'Check in a machine' returns a generic server error for everyone in the shop, and partner shops' machines no longer arrive on the bench.
- **Fix:** Put an upper bound on typed numbers (e.g. `typed <= 999999`, matching the /search 1-6 digit rule) and throw a UserError otherwise. Optionally refuse a typed number far ahead of the counter (e.g. more than 10000 ahead) unless an admin confirms.

### L36. A failed check-in leaves behind the customer and machine it just created, so each retry adds duplicates
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/tickets.ts:39` (bench-workflow; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** createTicket creates the new Customer (line 39) and new Machine (line 68) with plain db calls before the ticket transaction (line 96). When the transaction then throws, for example the typed-number clash UserError at line 101 or any Prisma error, those rows stay committed. The form keeps what was typed and still has no customerId/machineId, so the retry creates a second customer and machine.
- **Scenario:** A clerk types paper ticket 51, which already exists, for new customer 'QA Dup Person' with a Bernina B790. The error says 'Ticket 51 already exists'. The customer and the machine were created anyway with no ticket, and fixing the number and resubmitting creates them again.
- **Fix:** Create the customer and machine inside the same db.$transaction as the ticket (use tx.customer.create / tx.machine.create). Also check the typed-number clash before creating anything.

### L37. toggleChecklist stores arbitrary client-chosen keys (up to ~1 MB each) in Ticket.checklist
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/tickets.ts:187` (data-model; security, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** toggleChecklist(ticketId, item, done) writes list[item] for any string the client sends. Nothing checks it against Shop.checklistItems, and length and count are unlimited (the server-action body limit is 16 MB). Round 2 hardened saveMeasurements by keeping only keys that are real factory settings; the checklist JSON, same kind of field, was left open. The whole JSON is then sent to the browser on every ticket page (tickets/[id]/page.tsx:229, state={checklist}).
- **Scenario:** A TECH (or a script with their cookie) repeatedly replays the toggle action with fresh ~1 MB keys. The ticket row and each page load grow without limit, and on the hosted app that is shared Postgres storage.
- **Fix:** Accept `item` only if it is in the shop's checklist (t.shop.checklistItems, or DEFAULT_CHECKLIST when that is empty), which the ticket page already uses. Also cap string lengths in str() callers for free-text ticket fields.

### L38. The bench blocks cross-reference entries that the hub would accept: its duplicate-number check ignores brand and matches 1-2 character numbers
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/xref.ts:22` (parts-inventory; security, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** numberOwner() refuses a save when any local XPart has the same numberKey, whatever the brand, and it does not skip short keys (`.filter(Boolean)`). The hub's own check (stitchhub parts.mjs checkNumbers) matches brand_key AND number_key. So on the bench, 'Janome, 12' is refused because some 'Brother, 12' entry exists, and the error tells the user to merge the two unrelated parts. Following that advice corrupts the shared cross-reference for every shop.
- **Scenario:** The user adds 'Singer, 102' for a Singer part. The bench says 'One of those numbers is already on "Bobbin case (Brother)". Open that part and merge the two instead.' The user merges them, and every shop now sees two different parts as one.
- **Fix:** Compare brand and number the way the hub does, using the numbers JSON on XPart (or just let the hub's 409 with partId decide), and ignore keys shorter than 3 characters.

### L39. '(own)' and consigned-suffix part numbers leak into community data (cross-reference numbers and usage reports)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/xref.ts:207` (parts-inventory; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** Round 2's import gives own stock beside a consigned item the number '<number> (own)'. addPartToCrossRef sends `{ brand, number: p.partNumber }`, so the community entry gets the number 'ABC-1 (own)' (hub key ABC1OWN). pushUsage (src/lib/hub.ts:567) groups by p."partNumber", so usage of that item is reported as 'ABC-1 (own)', which the hub keys as a separate part that matches no xref entry.
- **Scenario:** A sewing shop's own 'CL-3148 (own)' is added to the cross-reference. Every shop now sees a part number 'CL-3148 (own)'. The community most-used list splits CL-3148 usage between CL3148 and CL3148OWN.
- **Fix:** Strip a trailing ' (own)' or ' (<partner>)' suffix before sending (or keep the original number in altNumbers[0] and send that). In pushUsage, report the underlying number, e.g. altNumbers[0] when the partNumber carries a suffix.

### L40. Unchecked integers and enum values from the client cause generic 500s (invoice search, paper ticket number, payment method, ticket status)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/invoices/page.tsx:20` (bench-workflow; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** Several inputs reach Prisma without range or enum checks, so a Prisma error comes back instead of a UserError. /invoices?q=<11+ digits> passes Number(term) as the Int ticket number (int4 overflow), giving a 500. createTicket accepts any positive typed paper number (tickets.ts:93-97): a number over 2,147,483,647 fails, and a large valid one (e.g. a scanned barcode) permanently moves the shop's counter there ('never back'). addPayment casts `method` and createTicket casts `status` unchecked (tickets.ts:361, 115). createTicket also lets a crafted request create a ticket already COLLECTED with no closedAt, which then never appears in the Sales log.
- **Scenario:** Searching invoices for a phone number such as 5551234567 returns HTTP 500 instead of 'No invoices match'.
- **Fix:** Accept a number only when it is 1 <= n <= 2_147_483_647 (and reasonable, e.g. <= 9_999_999, for typed ticket numbers). Validate method against PAYMENT_LABEL keys and status against STATUS_ORDER, and set closedAt when status is closed at creation. Throw UserError for anything outside these.

### L41. Invoices search on a long all-digit term (e.g. a 10-digit serial) returns 500; repeated query params crash several bench pages
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/invoices/page.tsx:20` (bench-workflow; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** `const num = /^\d+$/.test(term) ? Number(term) : null` is then used as an Int filter `{ number: num }`. Any digit string of 2147483648 or more (the placeholder invites serial searches, and many serials are 10-digit numbers) makes Prisma throw, so the page returns 500. Separately, the bench pages call `.trim()` or pass `sp.x` straight into Prisma on searchParams, which can be string[] when a parameter is repeated. /search, /customers, /machines, /invoices, /specs and /tickets/new?machineId=a&machineId=b all return 500.
- **Scenario:** On /invoices, searching for serial 5041234567 gives HTTP 500 instead of the matching invoice.
- **Fix:** Only treat the term as a ticket number when it has at most 9 digits (like /search's \d{1,6}); otherwise search it as text. Normalise searchParams with a helper that takes the first value when an array is given.

### L42. Sign out doesn't end the session server-side, and the Windows program sends the 30-day JWT over plain HTTP
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/layout.tsx:57` (auth-accounts; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** OPEN (low pass: session revocation policy (per-session table or shorter sessions): owner decision; LAN HTTPS is handoff item 11)
- **Problem:** Sessions are stateless JWTs (auth.ts:71, default 30-day maxAge). The only server-side revocation is sessionVersion, which goes up on password change, reset and deactivation but not on sign-out. The sidebar Sign out only clears the cookie in that browser. The Windows program serves on 0.0.0.0 over http (desktop/main.mjs:273, HOSTNAME 0.0.0.0), so auth.js issues the non-Secure `authjs.session-token` cookie. Anyone on the shop Wi-Fi who captures it keeps a working session for up to 30 days, even after the victim signs out.
- **Scenario:** A tech signs in on a tablet on shop Wi-Fi that customers share, and the cookie is captured in transit. The tech signs out at the end of the day. The captured cookie still passes sessionUser() (same sessionVersion) until the owner changes that tech's password or removes access.
- **Fix:** Bump sessionVersion on sign-out (or keep a per-session id with a server-side revoked list) so signing out ends the token everywhere it was copied. Shorten session maxAge on the LAN program (for example 12 h with updateAge). Longer term, serve HTTPS on the LAN (Tailscale certs, or a self-signed cert pinned by the Electron window).

### L43. Market invoice and order history show dates in the server's time zone, not the shop's
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/market/orders/[id]/invoice/page.tsx:38` (hub-market; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** The invoice 'Date', 'Paid' and 'Shipped' lines use fmtDate() with no timeZone, and the order page's event list uses new Date(e.at).toLocaleString() (orders/[id]/page.tsx:97). Both run on the server, which is UTC on a hosted install and in the container. Shop.timeZone exists and is already used by sales and partner pages, but not here.
- **Scenario:** A New York shop accepts an order at 9:30 pm on Sep 26. The hosted invoice is dated Sep 27, 2026, and the history shows 1:30:00 AM.
- **Fix:** Load shop.timeZone and pass it to the date formatting on the market order, invoice and orders-list pages.

### L44. Bench dashboard loads (and renders) every part of the shop to find low stock
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/page.tsx:27` (data-model; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The dashboard fetches `db.part.findMany({ where: { shopId } })` with no limit and filters qtyOnHand <= reorderAt in JavaScript. It then renders every match in the 'low stock' list. reorderAt defaults to 0, so every sold-out or never-stocked item matches. The query also runs for retail-only shops before they are redirected to /register (it is inside the Promise.all ahead of the doesRepair check). A shop that imported its notions catalogue (import allows 5,000 rows per run) moves thousands of Part rows, each with altNumbers, numberKeys and notes, on every Bench view.
- **Scenario:** A 'both' shop with 15,000 SKUs, 3,000 of them at 0. Every open of the Bench pulls 15,000 rows from Postgres and renders a 3,000-item low-stock list.
- **Fix:** Filter in SQL with a field reference (`qtyOnHand: { lte: db.part.fields.reorderAt }`, and optionally reorderAt > 0), select only the displayed columns, `take: 50` with a 'see all' link, and skip the query for shops that don't do repairs.

### L45. 'Invoice (on account)' and 'Shop collected' payments count as money taken and zero the balance, so what trade accounts owe never shows as owed
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/page.tsx:30` (bench-workflow; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** PAYMENT_LABEL has INVOICE = 'Invoice (on account)' and SHOP_COLLECTED = 'Shop collected' (the quilt shop took the customer's money). Both mean the repair shop has not received the money yet, but they are stored as ordinary payments. The dashboard's 'taken today' sums every method, and the sales log's 'Money taken' does the same. ticketTotals subtracts them from the balance, so /invoices puts those tickets under 'Paid' and 'Still owed', and the trade customer's 'owed' excludes them. README promises an Invoices area showing 'what's owed'.
- **Scenario:** A trade-account ticket for $139.64 is settled with 'Invoice (on account)'. The dashboard reports $139.64 taken today, and /invoices lists it as Paid with $0 still owed, although no money has arrived from the account.
- **Fix:** Treat INVOICE and SHOP_COLLECTED as receivables: leave them out of 'taken', show them as 'on account' in the balance and invoice totals, and give /invoices and the customer page an 'on account' figure (or settle them through Settlement / a statement).
