### L46. Partner statement month title and paid-at dates use the server locale/zone, not the shop's time zone
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/partners/[id]/statement/page.tsx:23` (gap; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** monthPeriod() correctly computes [from,to) in the shop's timeZone, but the page then derives the heading from p.from.toLocaleDateString(...) with no timeZone argument, and every row/settlement date uses fmtDate() which also omits timeZone. On a server whose TZ is not the shop's, p.from (the instant of local midnight, e.g. 04:00Z for New York) formats to the correct month in most zones but a job paid at, say, 9:30pm on the last day of the month renders on the next day/month, so a repair shown as 'paid' in the period can display a date outside the printed month. This is the same class as the reported invoice/usage time-zone finding but a distinct, unfixed surface (the partner statement heading and every fmtDate on that page).
- **Scenario:** Shop time zone America/New_York, server TZ UTC. A repair paid 2026-09-30 21:30 local (2026-10-01 01:30Z) is included in September's statement (monthPeriod is zone-correct) but fmtDate renders it 'Oct 1, 2026'. For a shop east of UTC, zonedMidnight for from can even fall in the previous calendar month, so a boundary title could read the wrong month.
- **Fix:** Pass { timeZone: shop.timeZone } to the heading's toLocaleDateString and to fmtDate for every date on this page (thread the zone through, as the invoice fix did).

### L47. Partner sync status is install-wide but shown to every company: one company's partner errors and stuck-message count appear on every other company's Partners page
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/partners/page.tsx:51` (partners; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** HubState (id=1) holds one partnerError for the whole install. doPartnerSync writes into it either the thrown message ('partner message <type> from <side>: <error>', which for Prisma errors can include query details of another company's data) or a count of stuck outbox rows across all companies (partners.ts:605-608). Every company's Partners page renders 'Last partner sync failed: {hub.partnerError}'. A shop with no partners at all sees 'N messages to partners couldn't be sent (see below)' with nothing below. Also, a retryable failure for one company's message throws, so 'Sync now' returns a generic server error for every company until it is set aside.
- **Scenario:** On a hosted install, company A's partner sends a message that fails to apply. Company B's staff see A's error text and message counts on B's Partners page.
- **Fix:** Show only per-shop information: compute stuck and failures by shopId (already done for the lists below) and keep install-level errors (hub unreachable) generic. Don't render raw exception text; keep the details in server logs or in a per-shop PartnerFailure row.

### L48. Most-used periods and monthly usage sent to the hub use the server's clock (UTC) instead of the shop's time zone
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/parts/most-used/page.tsx:24` (parts-inventory; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** 'Last N months' starts at new Date(y, m-N+1, 1) in the server's local zone, and the running server is UTC. pushUsage (src/lib/hub.ts:561-562) buckets usage into months the same way. The Shop row has a timeZone (default America/New_York, added in round 1 and used by monthPeriod() for partner statements), but these two ignore it. Parts used on the evening of the last day of a month (US time) count toward the next month on the hub, and the shop view's start boundary is 4-5 hours off. Static review only.
- **Scenario:** A New York shop uses a part at 9 pm on Aug 31 (01:00 UTC Sep 1). It is reported to the hub as a September use, and 'Last 3 months' viewed in November includes some July-31-evening uses.
- **Fix:** Use zonedMidnight or monthPeriod from src/lib/partners.ts with the shop's timeZone for the most-used boundary. For pushUsage (install-wide), bucket per shop using that shop's time zone.

### L49. The sewing shop's repair page tells the clerk the customer owes the full bill, ignoring a deposit paid to the repair shop; the register charges less
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/repairs/[id]/page.tsx:93` (bench-workflow; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** 'Handing it back' shows `The customer owes {money(j.totalCents)}` whenever paidAt is null. The register's pickup list uses repairDue = total - repairPaidCents - paidCents (src/actions/register.ts:66-67), so deposits paid straight to the repair shop are subtracted there but not on this page. The clerk quotes one amount and the register rings up another.
- **Scenario:** $150 repair, customer paid a $50 deposit to the repair shop (repairPaidCents=5000, job not fully paid, so paidAt is null). /repairs/<id> says 'The customer owes $150.00'; the register's Repair pickup line is $100.00.
- **Fix:** Export repairDue (or move it to a shared lib) and use it on the repair page and in the repairs list; show 'Paid to repair shop: $X' when repairPaidCents > 0.

### L50. Repairs page 'commission earned this month' uses the server's time zone, not the shop's
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/repairs/page.tsx:27` (bench-workflow; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** Round 1 made the dashboard use the shop time zone (startOfToday(tz)). The repairs page still computes the month start as `new Date(new Date().getFullYear(), new Date().getMonth(), 1)`, which is midnight in the server's zone (UTC on Railway).
- **Scenario:** On a hosted install (UTC), a US Eastern shop's repair pickup paid at 9 pm on Sept 30 local time (01:00 UTC Oct 1) counts toward October's commission, and the September figure is short.
- **Fix:** Load the shop's timeZone and use monthRange(undefined, undefined, tz).start (src/lib/sales.ts) for the month boundary.

### L51. Claim-tag and receipt barcodes are printed but nothing reads them, and the StitchShop search box promises 'tag #' but never searches repairs
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/search/page.tsx:15` (gap; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round3-tail: the register scan box understands T<tag> / plain-digit claim tags and S<number> receipts, same parsing as /search (src/lib/scan-codes.ts); a receipt loads its lines as a return with an exact refund (src/lib/sale-return.ts, unit-tested))
- **Problem:** The two-part claim tag prints a barcode `T<tag>` and the register receipt prints `S<sale number>`. No code parses either: scanning a claim tag at the register looks it up as an inventory item ('Nothing matches T101. Add it to inventory...'), or rings up a part whose numberKey happens to be T101. The sidebar search placeholder for a sewing shop reads 'Customer, item, tag #', but /search never queries RepairJob. A tag number is taken as a bench ticket number (and on a 'both' shop jumps to bench ticket #101), then searched as a machine, customer or part. To find a machine the customer came to collect, the clerk has to go through the Repair pickup list or /repairs.
- **Scenario:** Reproduced on my own retail shop with a READY_FOR_PICKUP repair job tag 101: the search placeholder reads 'Customer, item, tag #'. /search?q=101 and /search?q=T101 both show 'Machines (0) ... Customers (0)'. At /register, scanning or typing T101 gives 'Nothing matches T101. Add it to inventory ...'. Expected: the repair job (and at the register, its balance as a pickup line).
- **Fix:** In /search, match RepairJob.tag (and strip a leading T). In the register lookup, map T<tag> to the pickup job line and S<number> to the sale being returned.

### L52. Customer search by phone only matches the exact formatting it was typed with
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/search/page.tsx:34` (gap; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The global search and the Customers page match `phone: { contains: q }` on the raw stored text. A customer saved as '(555) 123-4567' isn't found by '5551234567' or '555-123-4567', which is how a tech types a caller's number. The register's customer picker already normalizes to the last 4 digits; the bench searches don't. When the customer isn't found, techs create a new customer, adding to the duplicate problem (the reported check-in duplicates, plus no merge).
- **Scenario:** Reproduced: create customer 'Phone Search Person' with phone '(555) 123-4567'. /search?q=5551234567 -> Customers (0); /search?q=555-123-4567 -> Customers (0); the /customers?q= page gives 0 rows for both. '(555) 123-4567' and '123-4567' find it.
- **Fix:** Store a digits-only phone key (or compare with regexp_replace(phone, '\D', '', 'g')) and search on the digits of the query when it has 4 or more digits.

### L53. People: no way to change a role, and a removed person's email can never be reused
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/settings/page.tsx:308` (auth-accounts; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** People offers only Remove/Restore access and Reset password. No action changes a user's role, name or email, or deletes one: grep finds user.update only for passwordHash/sessionVersion and active. addUser refuses any email that already has a row, including deactivated ones. Round 1's note says sessions end when a role changes, but no role change exists. The People text says admins 'can also change prices and people', yet a tech added by mistake can't be promoted.
- **Scenario:** The owner adds a new hire as Tech, then wants them as Admin. The only option is Remove access and add them again, which fails with 'That email already has an account.' They must use a different email.
- **Fix:** Add a role select (ADMIN/TECH) per person that bumps sessionVersion, and let 'Add person' with a removed person's email reactivate that row with the new role and temporary password.

### L54. Hub times in Settings and 'Offline since' use the server's clock zone (UTC on hosted), not the shop's time zone
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/settings/page.tsx:37` (auth-accounts; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** Server components format times with toLocaleString(), toLocaleDateString() and toLocaleTimeString() without a timeZone: settings/page.tsx:34 (joinedAt) and :37 (lastSyncAt), and (app)/layout.tsx:47 ('Offline since'). On Railway the server runs in UTC, so a shop set to America/Chicago sees times five hours off. Round 1 added the shop time zone for exactly this reason.
- **Scenario:** A Chicago shop on the hosted app sees 'last sync 5:11 PM' at 12:11 PM local, and 'Offline since 9:02 PM' for an outage that started at 4:02 PM.
- **Fix:** Pass the shop's timeZone to the formatter: `toLocaleString("en-US", { timeZone: shop.timeZone })`. The layout already loads the shop, so add timeZone to its select.

### L55. Printed invoice shows refunds as '--$20.00'
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/tickets/[id]/invoice/page.tsx:101` (gap; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** addPayment accepts negative amounts (it rejects only 0), which is how a refund is recorded on a ticket. The invoice prints each payment as `-{money(p.amountCents)}`, and money() already adds '-' for negatives, so a refund prints as '--$20.00' on the customer's invoice. The balance line is right, but the payment lines are confusing.
- **Scenario:** A $20 overpayment is refunded as a -$20 payment. The printed invoice lists 'Paid ... (Cash) --$20.00'.
- **Fix:** Print negative payments as 'Refunded {money(-amount)}', or format as money(-p.amountCents).

### L56. Register-sales export drops the whole-sale discount (and change and void time), so exported sales don't add up
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/api/export/[what]/route.ts:43` (register-money; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** The register-sales export is documented as 'what a shop takes with it if it moves' but has no column for Sale.discountCents, changeCents or voidedAt. When a whole-sale discount was given, sum(line_total) + sale_tax does not equal sale_total, and nothing in the file explains the difference.
- **Scenario:** Sale #1004: 10 yd at $12.99 with a 100% whole-sale discount exports as line_total 129.90, sale_tax 0.00, sale_total 0.00, with no discount column.
- **Fix:** Add sale_subtotal, sale_discount (dollars(s.discountCents)), change (dollars(s.changeCents)) and voided_at (s.voidedAt) columns.

### L57. Saving a scan into _inbox: two saves with the same name race to a 500, and an empty base name gives ' (2).pdf'
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/api/library/file/route.ts:55` (files-library-desktop; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The free name is picked with existsSync and then written with flag 'wx'. Two concurrent saves of the same name both pick it, and the loser's EEXIST is unhandled, so the request gets a 500. The collision loop also uses `base` where the first name used `base || "scan"`, so a filename of '.pdf' gives 'scan.pdf' and then ' (2).pdf'.
- **Scenario:** Two techs save scans of the same model at the same moment: one gets an error toast and has to save again. A second unnamed scan lands as ' (2).pdf' in the inbox.
- **Fix:** Loop on `writeFile(..., { flag: 'wx' })`, catch EEXIST and try the next suffix. Compute `const stem = base || 'scan'` once and use it in both places. The same pattern is in actions/hub.ts inboxName() plus writeFile.

### L58. CSV downloads have no UTF-8 BOM: Excel on Windows garbles accented names and fractions
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/api/sales/route.ts:34` (register-money; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** /api/sales and /api/export/[what] send UTF-8 without a byte-order mark. Excel on Windows (the Windows program's users) opens a BOM-less CSV as the ANSI code page, so customer names like 'José' or 'Müller', and descriptions with '½' or '—', show as mojibake.
- **Scenario:** Customer 'José Núñez' is exported, and the file is opened by double-click in Excel on Windows: it shows 'JosÃ© NÃºÃ±ez'.
- **Fix:** Prefix the body with "﻿" in both routes (optionally in a shared csvResponse helper in src/lib/csv.ts, which could also use CRLF line endings).

### L59. GET /api/session-end signs the user out with no CSRF protection
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/api/session-end/route.ts:4` (gap; security, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The route is a plain GET that calls signOut(). Any link or top-level navigation from another site signs the victim out, because SameSite=Lax cookies still go on top-level GETs. This matters most on a shared counter tablet.
- **Scenario:** A page the tech opens on the counter tablet navigates to https://<install>/api/session-end, and the tablet is signed out in the middle of a sale.
- **Fix:** Only sign out when the session really is invalid (call sessionUser() first and return a redirect to /login otherwise), or make sign-out a POST with next-auth's CSRF token.

### L60. Labels page renders a barcode per copy with no overall cap (up to 300 ids x 30 copies = 9,000 bwip-js renders), and silently drops ticks past 300
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/labels/page.tsx:95` (parts-inventory; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Label calls barcodeSvg(p.partNumber) once per copy instead of once per part. Measured on the production build: 100 ids x 5 copies (500 labels) took 1.24 s and 1.1 MB. The allowed maximum is 18x that (about 20 s of server CPU and about 20 MB per request), which any signed-in user can repeat (DoS needing auth). The Parts list lets you tick up to 1000 rows, but the page keeps only the first 300 ids (line 15) without saying so.
- **Scenario:** GET /labels?id=<100 ids>&format=thermal&copies=5 -> 1236 ms, 1.1 MB. With 300 ids and copies=30 the cost scales roughly 18x. Ticking 400 parts on /parts and pressing Print labels prints 300 with no notice.
- **Fix:** Compute the SVG once per part (a Map of id to svg) and reuse it for copies. Cap total labels (e.g. 600) with a visible 'first N shown' message, and show a note when more than 300 ids were sent.

### L61. Sign-in ignores callbackUrl: every deep link lands on the dashboard after signing in
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/login/page.tsx:17` (auth-accounts; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The proxy sends signed-out users to /login?callbackUrl=<page>, but the login action always calls signIn with redirectTo: "/". Links to a ticket, a labels print sheet or /tickets/new are lost after a session ends (for example after 'Please sign in again: your password or your access changed').
- **Scenario:** A tech opens a texted link to a ticket while signed out, signs in, and ends up on the dashboard instead of the ticket.
- **Fix:** Read callbackUrl from searchParams, keep it only if it is a same-origin path (starts with '/' and not '//' or '/\'), and pass it as redirectTo. Otherwise use '/'.

### L62. Internal error text reaches the new user through the sign-up redirect and the Companies error
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/signup/page.tsx:54` (auth-accounts; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The sign-up action puts `e.message` from any error redeemPartnerCode throws into the /partners?error= redirect, including errors that aren't UserErrors (Prisma errors from saveLinkFromHub or tradeAccountFor, fetch or JSON errors). createCompany does the same in its UserError text (src/actions/companies.ts:30). This goes around safe()'s rule that only UserError text reaches the UI, so Prisma messages, which quote the failing query or constraint, can show up in the browser for an anonymous sign-up.
- **Scenario:** The hub returns a link whose partner name collides with an existing constraint, or a DB error happens during saveLinkFromHub. The new owner lands on /partners?error=<Prisma message naming tables and columns>.
- **Fix:** Show e.message only when isUserError(e). Otherwise log the error on the server and use the generic message.

### L63. StitchBench and StitchShop on one PC share one session cookie (cookies aren't scoped by port)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/auth.config.ts:7` (files-library-desktop; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** INSTALL.md says both programs can run on one PC (ports 3100 and 3110). Both use next-auth's default cookie names (authjs.session-token, authjs.csrf-token) on the same host, and browsers don't separate cookies by port. Signing in to one overwrites the other's session cookie; with different AUTH_SECRETs the other program can't read it, so that user is signed out. Every browser on the LAN that uses both addresses keeps getting signed out. Each server (and any other web service on that host) also receives the other program's 30-day session token.
- **Scenario:** The counter tablet uses http://shop-pc:3110 (StitchShop register) and http://shop-pc:3100 (StitchBench tickets). Every switch between them forces a new sign-in. And if some other HTTP service on the shop PC is compromised or untrusted, it collects StitchBench session cookies.
- **Fix:** Give each flavor its own cookie names in the next-auth config, e.g. `cookies: { sessionToken: { name: `${appName().toLowerCase()}.session-token` }, csrfToken: {...}, callbackUrl: {...} }`, or include the port in the name on the Windows program.

### L64. Spreadsheet import reads a CSV file as UTF-8 only; Excel's default 'CSV (Comma delimited)' (Windows-1252) turns accents and fractions into �
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/import-items.tsx:125` (gap; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The file picker uses `await f.text()`, which always decodes UTF-8. On Windows, Excel's plain 'CSV (Comma delimited)' saves in the ANSI code page (cp1252), and so do many supplier line sheets. Characters such as é, ½, °, ™ and curly quotes are not valid UTF-8 there, so they become U+FFFD and are saved into descriptions and brands. They then show up on labels, receipts and the register. Pasting cells from Excel works; only the file path is affected.
- **Scenario:** A quilt shop opens a supplier's .xlsx on its Windows PC, saves it as 'CSV (Comma delimited)' and imports it. 'Crème Brûlée ½ yd' is stored as 'Cr�me Br�l�e � yd' on 300 items.
- **Fix:** Read the file as an ArrayBuffer and try TextDecoder('utf-8', { fatal: true }). If that fails, fall back to TextDecoder('windows-1252'), or offer an encoding choice.

### L65. Rows whose quantity can't be read are imported with 0 stock and counted as 'added' (the round-1 parseQty comment says they are skipped)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/import-items.tsx:55` (parts-inventory; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** parseQty returns NaN for quantities such as '12 pk', '.5' or '1e3', and its comment says 'the row shows it and is skipped'. The server turns a non-finite qty into 0 (inventory-import.ts:81) and still creates or updates the item. The result message lumps these rows under 'added', and the preview only flags the first 8 rows with '?'. On a long line sheet the user never learns that those items came in with no stock.
- **Scenario:** Paste rows with Qty '12 pk', '.5', '24', '1e3' > preview shows '?', '?', '24', '?' > Import > 'Imported: 4 added, 0 updated, 0 skipped'. In the DB, QTY-A, QTY-B and QTY-D have qtyOnHand 0 and no stock move. Expected: rows with an unreadable quantity are skipped and reported (e.g. '3 skipped: quantity not understood'), or the Import button stays disabled until they are fixed or unmapped.
- **Fix:** When a mapped qty cell is non-empty and NaN, and addQty is on, count the row as skipped on the server, return the row numbers, and show a count of unreadable quantities in the preview for all rows (not just the first 8). Also accept '.5' by allowing a leading dot.

### L66. Listing a fabric, notion, thread or pattern item from its part page silently lists it as a Part, with yardage rounded
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/listing-form.tsx:56` (hub-market; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The part page passes kind: p.kind and qty: Math.max(1, p.qtyOnHand) to ListingForm (src/app/(app)/parts/[id]/page.tsx:244-246). The kind select only offers PART, ACCESSORY and SUPPLY, so a default of FABRIC, NOTION, THREAD or PATTERN falls back to the first option and posts PART. qtyOnHand is Float (yards), so 2.5 is rounded by int() to 3 units. The hub and createListing both accept all 7 kinds, but the /market filter can't find the other four either.
- **Scenario:** A sewing shop lists 'Kona cotton, white' (FABRIC, 2.5 yd on hand). The listing shows Type: Part and 3 available.
- **Fix:** Render every KIND_LABEL option in the kind select, add those kinds to the /market filter, and for FABRIC either list by the yard (a unit field) or refuse fractional quantities with a message.

### L67. 'Open p. N' for tension specs never uses the shop PC's library, although every spec has sourcePath
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/local-manuals.tsx:84` (files-library-desktop; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** OpenSpecSource matches only by file name in this browser's own index (findByName). It never falls back to manualPath(), which LibraryManuals and ManualFigure use to open manuals from the Windows program's LIBRARY_DIR. It receives only sourceFile, not sourcePath.
- **Scenario:** In the Windows program, phones, tablets and other PCs on the shop network never see 'Open p. 51' next to a tension spec, although INSTALL.md says 'Every device opens them through StitchBench' and the shop PC serves the file.
- **Fix:** Pass sourcePath and resolve it with `manualPath(index, sourcePath ?? file)`, as ManualFigure does, then openLocal(path, page).

### L68. Figure drawing fails on browsers without native Map.prototype.getOrInsertComputed (modern pdf.js 6 build is imported, legacy build has the polyfill)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/manual-figure.tsx:25` (files-library-desktop; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** loadDoc() runs `await import("pdfjs-dist")`, which is the modern build of pdfjs-dist 6.3.289. That build calls `Map.prototype.getOrInsertComputed` (17 uses in build/pdf.mjs) without a polyfill. Only legacy/build/pdf.mjs ships the core-js polyfill (lines 5263-6014). In any browser that lacks the method, 'Show figure' fails straight away with a toast and nothing is drawn or saved. That includes Chromium 141, which is the browser this repo's Playwright config pins, and older Chrome/Edge/Safari on shop tablets and phones. The figures feature (local folder or shop PC library) is therefore broken there, and tests/figure.spec.ts fails on the pinned browser.
- **Scenario:** A tech on a browser without the method opens a machine or model page and clicks 'Show figure'. Expected: the timing figure is drawn from the manual (and saved online). Observed: the toast 'this[#ne].getOrInsertComputed is not a function', the canvas stays hidden, and no figure is stored for phones.
- **Fix:** Import `pdfjs-dist/legacy/build/pdf.mjs`, and have scripts/copy-pdf-worker.mjs copy `legacy/build/pdf.worker.min.mjs`. Or install a small polyfill for Map/WeakMap.prototype.getOrInsert(Computed) before loading pdf.js. Run figure.spec in CI.

### L69. A tech can't price an item that has no price, so it goes on the sale at $0
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/register.tsx:353` (register-money; security, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** addItem warns "<part> has no price. Type the price on the line.", but the price box is `disabled={!canSell || ...}`, and canSell is false for TECH. The line stays at $0.00 and a tech can't fix it. The server (register.ts:191) does let a tech set any price when part.priceCents is null, so the UI is stricter than the server here and looser elsewhere (see the TECH controls finding).
- **Scenario:** A tech scans a new notion that has no price yet: the line shows $0.00, the price box is greyed out, and the sale either goes through at $0 or waits for an owner.
- **Fix:** Leave the price box enabled when the part has no price (pass `priced` on RegisterItem), or tell a tech that an owner has to price the item first, and block $0 part lines for TECH on the server.

### L70. Register shows 'Change' and enables Complete for a card/check overpayment that the server then rejects
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/register.tsx:499` (register-money; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** saleTotals computes change = paid - total regardless of method, and the button is enabled whenever due == 0. The server rejects any non-cash amount above the total. When a cashier types a larger card amount, or lowers a price after adding a card tender, the register shows 'Change $X' in green and then fails on submit.
- **Scenario:** $10.00 item, the cashier types 15 and clicks Card. The register shows 'Change $5.00' and Complete sale is enabled; the server answers 'Card, check and gift amounts can't be more than the total. Only cash gives change.'
- **Fix:** On the client, compute the non-cash sum and when non-cash > total show 'Only cash gives change' instead of Change and disable Complete (or cap the Card/Check tender at the remaining due in addTender).

### L71. The refund 'blank' amount goes the wrong way after an over-refund, and nothing says the refund doesn't match
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/register.tsx:113` (register-money; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** For refunds, addTender computes `amount = t.total - t.paid` and then forces it negative (`-Math.abs(amount)`). After an over-refund, total - paid is positive, and forcing it negative adds more refund instead of correcting it. For refunds the page never shows how much is over or under ('Still due' only renders when total >= 0), so the only sign is a disabled button. The server rejects the mismatch, so no bad data is saved, but the clerk has no guidance.
- **Scenario:** Return one $5.32 item, type 10 and click Cash (tender -$10.00). The label reads 'blank = $4.68'. Clicking Cash again adds another -$4.68 (refunds now total -$14.68). Complete refund stays disabled with no explanation.
- **Fix:** For refunds use `amount = typed != null ? -Math.abs(typed) : t.total - t.paid` without forcing the sign, so a positive result means cash back to the drawer, or refuse it. Show 'Refund still owed / over-refunded by $X' when t.total < 0 && t.paid !== t.total.

### L72. Camera scanning repeats the same barcode every 1.5 s while it stays in view (duplicate ticket lines and receipts)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/scan-station.tsx:73` (parts-inventory; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** The camera callback drops a repeat of the same code only within 1500 ms of the last accepted scan, then accepts it again. BarcodeDetector polls every 250 ms and ZXing decodes continuously (about every 500 ms). A barcode that stays in the frame is scanned again every 1.5 s. The comment's intent is 'count it once'. In Receive mode each repeat adds stock; in 'Use on ticket' mode each repeat adds another billable line to the customer's ticket and decrements stock.
- **Scenario:** A tech in 'Use on ticket' mode holds the phone over a bobbin case label for about 3 s after the beep. Two or three identical lines are added to the ticket, the customer is billed two or three times, and stock drops by the same amount.
- **Fix:** Track when the code was last seen, not last accepted: update `last.current.at` on every detection (including dropped ones), and accept the same code again only after it has been absent from the frame for about 1 s. In receive and ticket modes, consider refusing an identical consecutive camera scan until a different code is seen or the user taps 'again'.

### L73. Scan station qty box (type=number, min=1, default step) blocks fractional yards, so fabric scans at 2.5 yd never submit
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/scan-station.tsx:125` (parts-inventory; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The server supports yards to the eighth in receive and ticket modes (scan.ts:48). The 'Qty each' input sits in the same <form> as the scan box and is `type="number" min={1}` with the default step of 1. Any fractional value fails constraint validation, so pressing Enter or the scanner's Enter does not submit, and the scan is not processed.
- **Scenario:** Cutting 2.5 yd of fabric onto a ticket: the tech sets Qty 2.5 and scans the bolt. The browser shows a validation bubble and nothing is added. They must use 1 or 3.
- **Fix:** Use `step="any"` (or 0.125) and `min="0.125"`, or add noValidate to the form; the server already clamps by unit.

### L74. Scan-station 'Count shelf': clicking a log link or leaving the page throws away the unsaved count without warning
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/scan-station.tsx:27` (parts-inventory; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** Counts live only in React state (useState Map), with no beforeunload prompt and no localStorage copy. Each scan-log entry has an in-page 'open' link (a Next Link, same tab), and the page has the site nav. Clicking either during a long shelf count, or a reload, discards every scan. Going back shows 'Counted so far (0 parts)'.
- **Scenario:** /parts/scan > Count shelf > scan 3148, QTY-C, CL-3149 ('Counted so far (3 parts)') > click 'open' on a log line > browser Back > Count shelf. Observed: 'Counted so far (0 parts)', and no confirmation was shown (0 dialogs).
- **Fix:** Keep the in-progress count in sessionStorage or localStorage keyed by shop, restore it on mount, add a beforeunload guard while counts.size > 0, and open the log's part links in a new tab while in count mode.

### L75. Checklist 'done at' timestamp breaks hydration (React #418) whenever the browser's time zone or locale differs from the server's
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/ticket-controls.tsx:87` (bench-workflow; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** Checklist is a client component rendered on the server first. `new Date(s.at).toLocaleString()` produces the server's zone and locale text during SSR and the browser's during hydration. On any hosted install (server UTC) every ticket with a ticked item throws a hydration mismatch, and React discards the server HTML and re-renders on the client.
- **Scenario:** Server in UTC, tech's browser in America/New_York (or any other zone or locale). Opening a ticket with a ticked checklist item logs 'Minified React error #418 (text)' and the page is re-rendered on the client.
- **Fix:** Format the timestamp on the server with the shop's time zone and pass the string in, or render it after mount (useEffect) or with suppressHydrationWarning on that span.

### L76. An invalid PARTNER_SYNC_SECONDS makes the partner sync timer run about every millisecond
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/instrumentation.ts:14` (auth-accounts; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** `setInterval(partners, Number(process.env.PARTNER_SYNC_SECONDS ?? 60) * 1000)`. A non-numeric value ('1m', '') gives NaN, and 0 gives 0. Node treats both as a 1 ms interval, so syncPartners is started continuously and hammers the DB and the hub.
- **Scenario:** An operator sets PARTNER_SYNC_SECONDS=1m or leaves it empty on Railway. The server pegs a CPU and floods the hub with partner sync requests.
- **Fix:** Parse and clamp the value: `const s = Number(process.env.PARTNER_SYNC_SECONDS); const secs = Number.isFinite(s) && s >= 10 ? s : 60;`.

### L77. No per-company limit on the online barcode lookup: one tenant can use up the hosted install's shared daily quota or run up its paid key
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/barcode-lookup.ts:15` (parts-inventory; security; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** barcodeDetails (inventory-import.ts:14) only calls requireUser(). The free UPCitemdb plan allows 100 lookups a day per server IP, and a paid UPCITEMDB_KEY is billed per call. On a hosted multi-company install every company shares the one server IP or key, with no per-company or per-user throttle. The input check (8 or 12-14 digits) rules out SSRF and the key goes only to api.upcitemdb.com, so the problem is only quota and cost.
- **Scenario:** One tenant, or a script it runs, calls barcodeDetails 100 times. Every other shop gets 'Today's free barcode lookups are used up' for the rest of the day, or, with a paid key, the operator pays for the calls.
- **Fix:** Add a per-company daily counter (in memory or in the DB) and cache results by UPC in a table, so repeat lookups cost nothing and one tenant cannot use up the install's quota.

### L78. Production builds open a separate Prisma pool per bundle copy
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/db.ts:22` (auth-accounts; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The client is cached on globalThis only when NODE_ENV !== 'production'. As action.ts and hub.ts note, Next's production build loads separate copies of the code for pages, route handlers, actions and timers. Each copy creates its own PrismaClient with its own pg pool (10 connections max by default), which multiplies the app's connection count against Postgres limits.
- **Scenario:** On a small hosted Postgres or a busy install, several pools of 10 exhaust max_connections under load, and requests fail with connection errors.
- **Fix:** Always store the client on globalThis (drop the NODE_ENV condition), and optionally set an explicit pool max through the adapter.

### L79. Money amounts have no bounds or sign rules: over $21.4M crashes the page, negative service/fee prices and discounts larger than the bill give negative invoices, and a custom service with no price becomes $0
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/format.ts:10` (bench-workflow; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: toCents refuses ambiguous and out-of-range amounts. Left: 3+ decimals are still rounded (supplier costs may need them)
- **Problem:** toCents accepts any finite number, including negatives and exponents. It also silently rounds 19.999 to $20.00 and turns a European-formatted '1.000,50' into $1.00. Actions store the result directly into Int columns. Amounts of 2^31 cents or more throw an unhandled Prisma 'Value out of range' error that replaces the whole ticket page with 'This page couldn't load', for example when a check or card number is typed into Amount. addLine lets SERVICE/FEE/PART take negative prices and lets a DISCOUNT exceed the bill, which produces a negative total and balance. For partner tickets, the sewing shop's job.update validation (`totalCents: optInt(..., 0, MAX_MONEY)`) then rejects those updates. A custom SERVICE line with a description but no price is added at $0 with no warning (tickets.ts:275), whereas PART requires a price.
- **Scenario:** Typing 25,000,000 in Record payment gives 'This page couldn't load | A server error occurred'. A service line priced 1.000,50 is stored as 100 cents. A $200 discount on a $159 ticket gives Total -$41.00.
- **Fix:** Add a parseMoney(v, {min, max}) helper that rejects negatives (except for the DISCOUNT kind), caps at a MAX_MONEY such as $1,000,000, rejects more than 2 decimals and ambiguous separators, and throws UserError. In addLine, require a price for custom services and cap a discount at the current bill.

### L80. Pictures fetched from the hub for /api/figures/[id] are read with no size cap and cached forever
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/hub.ts:705` (files-library-desktop; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** hubPicture reads the hub's response with `r.arrayBuffer()` (figure, photo and listing branches) with no limit, and stores it in HubFigure/HubPhoto/ListingPhoto permanently. A malicious or compromised hub can send a huge body for a figure id it advertised in the changes feed, and every install runs out of memory the first time anyone opens that figure. The bloated row then ends up in memory each time it's served (it's cached, so it won't be re-fetched). safe-fetch has byte limits for member links, but this path doesn't use them.
- **Scenario:** A compromised hub adds a figure id to the feed and answers GET /v1/figures/<id> with 3 GB. A tech on any member install opens that setting, and the shop PC's server dies (or stores 3 GB in its DB and backups).
- **Fix:** Read hub pictures through a capped reader (e.g. 3 MB, matching the hub's own 2 MB figure and photo limits): check Content-Length and count bytes while streaming, abort past the cap, and don't cache anything over the cap.

### L81. Most-used periods and usage months use server-local time; the hub validates months in UTC
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/hub.ts:561` (parts-inventory; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** pushUsage computes month boundaries and the 'YYYY-MM' label with the server's local calendar (new Date(y, m, 1)), and so does the most-used page (most-used/page.tsx:24). The shop's own timeZone setting is ignored. The hub rejects months after its UTC current month (stitchhub src/usage.mjs:29). A Windows install east of UTC (Europe, Australia) sends next month's label for the first hours of each month and gets 400 until UTC catches up; last month's final counts wait with it. On the UTC-hosted app, US shops' windows start at UTC midnight rather than local midnight.
- **Scenario:** A UTC+10 install at 05:00 local on Oct 1 posts month '2026-10'. The hub, still on '2026-09' in UTC, answers 400, and pushUsage logs and aborts before sending September's totals; this repeats every sync for about 10 hours.
- **Fix:** Compute month ranges in the shop's timeZone (the zonedMidnight or monthRange helpers in src/lib/partners.ts and src/lib/sales.ts). Either label usage months in UTC to match the hub, or have the hub allow +1 month of skew. Push each month independently so one rejection doesn't block the other.

### L82. One part number with no A-Z/0-9 characters makes the hub reject the shop's whole usage report, silently and every sync
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/hub.ts:570` (gap; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** pushUsage sends every part used on tickets as { number: partNumber }. The hub (usage.mjs) computes key(number) (upper-case A-Z0-9 only) and throws 400 'Each item needs a part number.' for the whole request if any key is empty. The bench filters only on qty > 0, not on numberKey(number) being non-empty. The loop throws on the current month, so last month is never sent either. doSync swallows the error with console.error, and lastUsagePush is not updated, so the rejected POST repeats on every sync. Part numbers like '½ – ¼', '—', '#', or Chinese labels (the library already holds Chinese Dahao manuals) are enough to trigger it.
- **Scenario:** A tech uses an item numbered '螺丝' (or '½ – ¼') on one ticket this month. From then on every hub sync posts /v1/usage and gets 400. That shop's usage never reaches the community most-used list for this month or last, and nobody is told.
- **Fix:** In pushUsage, drop rows whose numberKey(number) is empty (and cap number length at 60), and/or have the hub skip such items instead of failing the batch. Send each month independently so one failure doesn't block the other.

### L83. Usage report month is the server's local month but the hub checks UTC: installs east of UTC are rejected for the first hours of every month
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/hub.ts:572` (gap; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** pushUsage builds months with new Date(now.getFullYear(), now.getMonth() - back, 1), in server local time. The hub rejects any month later than monthAgo(0), which is UTC (usage.mjs:28). On a desktop install in a zone ahead of UTC (e.g. Australia or New Zealand), just after local midnight on the 1st the local month is a month ahead of UTC. The hub answers 400, pushUsage throws before setting lastUsagePush, and the error is only logged. The report is retried and refused on every sync until UTC catches up, up to 13 hours. The previous month's final numbers are also not re-sent in that window.
- **Scenario:** Shop PC in Pacific/Auckland, local 2026-10-01 08:00 (2026-09-30 19:00 UTC). pushUsage sends month '2026-10', hub responds 400 'Send a month as YYYY-MM: this month or up to 24 months back.' and the loop aborts on every sync until 2026-10-01 00:00 UTC.
- **Fix:** Compute the usage months in UTC on the bench, or let the hub allow monthAgo(-1) as a small clock-zone tolerance. Also keep a failed month from stopping the other month's report.

### L84. Replacing a wrong figure doesn't reach devices that already showed it (1-year immutable cache) or the hub, and after a reload the UI has no way to save a redraw
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/image-response.ts:12` (files-library-desktop; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: figures cached an hour. Left: a "Redraw from manual" button and a hub-side replace
- **Problem:** Round 1 added 'an owner or admin drawing it again from the manual replaces a wrong one' (figures/route.ts:36). But /api/figures/<id> is served with `Cache-Control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable` at an unchanged URL, so any browser that already loaded the wrong image keeps it for a year. shareFigure() goes to the hub, which does `ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING`, so the hub and every other member keep the wrong one. Also, once a page renders with stored=true, the only drawing button is 'Whole page' (show(true)), which never calls saveOnline. The replace path is therefore reachable only in the same page visit before a reload.
- **Scenario:** A wrong figure is saved. The owner redraws it on the shop PC before reloading, and the DB row is updated. The bench tablet and phones that already viewed it keep showing the wrong image, and so does every shop on the hub.
- **Fix:** Version the figure URL (e.g. /api/figures/<id>?v=<updatedAt>) or drop `immutable` and use a short max-age with an ETag. Add an explicit 'Redraw from manual' action for install admins that forces saveOnline, and a hub-side replace for the original author or a hub admin.

### L85. A figure drawn from a different manual with the same file name is saved as the canonical figure and shared to the hub
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/local-manuals.ts:310` (files-library-desktop; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** findByPath() falls back to the first indexed file with the same name when the exact path is not in the connected folder. ManualFigure uses the result via manualPath(), draws page N from it, and saveOnline() stores it under the id of the setting's real sourcePath, then pushes it to the hub. The library has same-named files in different folders, including figure sources.
- **Scenario:** A tech connected only 'Industrial-Sewing\Singer\_teamworksales-instructions' (it contains 20U.pdf, the instructions). A setting cites '_teamworksales-service\20U.pdf' page 30. The figure is drawn from the instructions manual, saved for every company and sent to the hub (first copy wins).
- **Fix:** Have manualPath/findByPath report whether the match was by path or only by name, and call saveOnline only for path matches. Name-only matches can still be shown locally.

### L86. The note typed when ending a partnership ('Note for <partner>') is sent through the hub but thrown away by the receiving app
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/partners.ts:675` (partners; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The partner page offers 'Note for {partnerName} (optional)' (partners/[id]/page.tsx:182). endPartnership passes it to the hub (line 231), and the hub puts {endedBy, note} into the link.ended message (stitchhub/src/partners.mjs:232-236). The app's link.ended handler only sets status and endedAt (line 675), so the note and who ended it are never stored or shown.
- **Scenario:** The repair shop ends the partnership with the note 'Please return the consigned cutters by Friday'. The sewing shop only sees 'partnership ended'; the note is lost.
- **Fix:** Store text(d.note, 500) and d.endedBy on the link (e.g. endNote and endedBy columns, or an events list) and show them on the partner page and in PartnerTodo.

### L87. Partner order accept and cancel racing leaves the two shops permanently disagreeing on the order (one side cancelled, the other accepted and later delivered)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/partners.ts:880` (gap; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** Each shop moves its own copy of a partner order first and tells the other side afterwards. If the sewing shop cancels a PLACED order while the repair shop accepts it, each side sees the other's update as illegal from its own state and only adds a 'sort it out with them' event. Nothing converges. The repair shop can go on to deliver, which takes its stock and bills the order in its statement and running balance. The sewing shop's copy stays CANCELLED, so it can never mark it received and its statement never includes it. This is a different root cause from the reported stale-catalog divergence.
- **Scenario:** Order PO-12 PLACED. Sewing shop clicks Cancel (CANCELLED locally, order.update sent) while the repair shop clicks Accept (ACCEPTED locally, order.update sent). Each receives the other's message and only records a note. The repair shop then delivers, so its balance includes PO-12 and the sewing shop's does not.
- **Fix:** Make the seller authoritative: a buyer cancel becomes a cancel request that the seller confirms (or that is auto-applied if the seller's copy is still PLACED), and the seller's resulting state is echoed back and applied on the buyer side even when it differs from the buyer's local state.

### L88. The same barcode in 12-, 13- or 14-digit form (UPC-A / EAN-13 / GTIN-14) doesn't match, and Excel's scientific-notation UPCs are imported silently
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/parts.ts:27` (parts-inventory; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** numberKey only upper-cases and strips punctuation, so '0045544319491' (EAN-13, the form the import page's own placeholder uses: '3148\t0442717314803'), '045544319491' (UPC-A, what most USB scanners send for a UPC-A symbol by default) and '00045544319491' (GTIN-14 from distributor files) are three different keys. scanCode (scan.ts:16) and the register's itemByCode (register.ts:49) both use that key, so an item imported from a line sheet in one form reads as 'isn't in your parts' when a scanner sends another form. The import also takes Excel's display form of a numeric UPC column ('7.44272E+11', which is what gets copied when a 12-digit number sits in a General-format cell) as a barcode, with no warning. Several different products then get the same lossy alt number (numberKey '744272E11').
- **Scenario:** Import CL-3149 with UPC 0045544319491. Scanning 0045544319491 finds it. Scanning 045544319491 (12 digits), 45544319491 or 00045544319491 gives '... isn't in your parts.'. Separately, paste 'SKU\tUPC\tDescription...' rows whose UPC cells read 7.44272E+11. The preview shows the value without a warning, the rows import as 'added', and both items store altNumbers {7.44272E+11}.
- **Fix:** For all-digit codes of length 8, 12, 13 or 14 (and ideally with a valid check digit), normalise to one canonical key, e.g. GTIN-14 left-padded with zeros, and store it in numberKeys next to the raw key. Normalise scanned codes the same way in scanCode, itemByCode and addLine. In the import preview, flag barcodes matching /\d(\.\d+)?E\+\d+/i (and UPC cells that have lost leading zeros) and skip those rows.

### L89. Rounding differs for returns: a half-cent refunds one cent less than it was charged
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/register-math.ts:10` (register-money; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** lineNet and the tax line use Math.round, which rounds .5 toward +infinity (so -649.5 becomes -649), and taxRatePct is a float (6.35 is stored as 6.3499999...). A return is the negative of a sale, but its rounded cents are not the negative of the sale's rounded cents. Half-yard fabric cuts and some tax amounts hit exact half-cents, so refunds come out one cent short of the original charge. Client and server share the function, so they agree with each other; they just don't mirror the sale.
- **Scenario:** Sell 0.5 yd at $12.99/yd with 6.35% tax: line 650, tax 41, total $6.91. Return the same 0.5 yd: line -649, tax -41, refund $6.90. At 7.25%, a $10.00 item is taxed 73 but its return refunds 72.
- **Fix:** Round symmetrically: const r = (x) => Math.sign(x) * Math.round(Math.abs(x)); use it in lineNet, discountOf and tax. Compute tax in integer basis points (store the rate as an integer or use Math.round(rate*1000)) to avoid float error, e.g. r(taxable * Math.round(rate * 1000) / 100000).

### L90. New companies always get time zone America/New_York; the Windows program never sets the PC's own zone
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/shops.ts:57` (gap; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round3-tail: the Companies form has a time-zone field (src/components/company-timezone-field.tsx) defaulting to the admin's own shop zone, then the browser's; src/actions/companies.ts validates it like Settings)
- **Problem:** Shop.timeZone defaults to 'America/New_York' (schema.prisma:55), and createShopWithOwner (used by sign-up, Companies and the Windows first run) never sets it. The Windows program knows the PC's zone (Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone) but doesn't pass it. Every report that correctly uses the shop zone therefore cuts days at Eastern midnight for shops elsewhere until someone finds the Settings field. That covers the register's 'today', the sales log month, the dashboard 'taken today' and partner statements.
- **Scenario:** A California shop on the Windows program rings up sales until 9:30 pm. Everything after 9:00 pm PT (midnight ET) lands on the next day's register report and next day's 'taken today', so the end-of-day drawer report never matches the drawer.
- **Fix:** Set timeZone at creation: the server's resolved zone on the Windows program, and the browser's zone (sent from the sign-up form) on hosted installs. Show it prominently in first-run setup.

### L91. README test instructions produce a red suite: default base URL is 3100 while `npm run dev` serves 3000, and figure.spec has no skip guard
- **Where:** stitchbench `tests/figure.spec.ts:21` (build-test-infra; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** README 'Run it for development' ends with `npm run dev` (`next dev`, port 3000). 'Tests' then says to run `npm run e2e` with only E2E_EMAIL/E2E_PASSWORD. playwright.config.ts:9 defaults baseURL to http://localhost:3100 and has no webServer, so every spec gets connection refused. E2E_BASE_URL is not documented. Among the non-opt-in specs, figure.spec.ts reads `process.env.MANUAL_DIR!` with no `test.skip`, so the documented command always fails on it even against the right port. README lists it neither as opt-in nor with its MANUAL_DIR/MANUAL_BRAND/MANUAL_MODEL needs. Without PW_CHANNEL=chrome a Playwright browser must also be installed (`npx playwright install chromium`), which is not mentioned.
- **Scenario:** A new developer follows README step by step: docker compose, .env, npm install, db:migrate, db:seed, npm run dev, e2e:setup, npm run e2e. All specs fail to connect to :3100. With E2E_BASE_URL fixed, figure.spec still fails with 'Cannot read properties of undefined (reading \'buffer\')'.
- **Fix:** Add `test.skip(!process.env.MANUAL_DIR, "set MANUAL_DIR ...")` to figure.spec. Either run dev on 3100 (`next dev -p 3100`) or document `E2E_BASE_URL=http://localhost:3000`, or add a `webServer` entry to playwright.config.ts. List figure.spec and offline.spec under opt-in suites with their variables.

### L92. Root cause of hub.spec.ts:47: the spec assumes the test hub was loaded with prisma/seed-data/hub_links.json, and that step (plus four other prerequisites) is not documented
- **Where:** stitchbench `tests/hub.spec.ts:47` (build-test-infra; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED in round-3 part 2 (verified 30 Sep: build, all migrations, drift check, Playwright specs, hub smoke test)
- **Problem:** Line 47 expects a heading 'Manual links (N)' with N >= 2 for Janome MC6700P. A fresh StitchHub has no links: the shared hub on :8080 has one link in total (the smoke test's deleted one). The two MC6700P links come only from prisma/seed-data/hub_links.json (3,025 links produced by scripts/hub/parse_sources.py). They have to be loaded through the hub admin API `POST /v1/admin/links/import` (admin page -> 'Add links'). Neither README.md ('tests/hub.spec.ts needs a test StitchHub (HUB_URL, HUB_JOIN_KEY, PDF_URL)'), the spec header, nor the hub README (which documents only the xparts import endpoints) mentions it. The spec also silently needs: LIBRARY_DIR set on the app server (downloadManualLink refuses without it, line 59); FETCH_ALLOW_HOSTS including the PDF_URL host (the fetch guard blocks localhost; only documented in a safe-fetch.ts comment); a Janome MC6700P machine already in the E2E shop (line 86-87, created by smoke.spec, which runs after hub.spec alphabetically); the E2E user being an install admin; and no ACTIVE partnership in any company on the install (leaveHub counts install-wide), otherwise it fails at line 35.
- **Scenario:** A developer points HUB_URL at a freshly started hub as README says. hub.spec fails at :47 (the baseline failure). If any company on the install has an active partnership (true right now on the shared app: 3 ACTIVE PartnerLink rows), it fails earlier at :35 because 'Leave the hub' is refused.
- **Fix:** Make the spec seed what it needs: with HUB_ADMIN_TOKEN, POST the two MC6700P rows from hub_links.json to /v1/admin/links/import (it skips duplicates), or change line 47 to assert on the link the test adds itself. Document LIBRARY_DIR, FETCH_ALLOW_HOSTS and HUB_ADMIN_TOKEN in the README Tests section. Create the MC6700P machine inside the spec instead of depending on smoke.spec. End or skip active partnerships before 'Leave the hub', or join without leaving.

### L93. Hub `engines: node >=20` is wrong (server.mjs needs import.meta.dirname, Node 20.11+), and the 'Without Docker' steps never load .env
- **Where:** stitchhub `package.json:15` (build-test-infra; qa; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** server.mjs:17 computes `path.resolve(import.meta.dirname, "..")`. `import.meta.dirname` was added in Node 21.2 / 20.11. On Node 20.0-20.10 it is undefined and path.resolve throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE at startup, even though package.json and the README say 'Node 20+'. Separately, README 'Without Docker' says `npm ci`, 'set the variables from .env.example', `npm start`. The hub has no dotenv or --env-file, so copying .env.example to .env (as the Docker section instructs) makes a bare `npm start` fail with 'Set DATABASE_URL'.
- **Scenario:** An admin on Node 20.9 (e.g. an older LTS package) runs `npm start` and the hub crashes with 'The "paths[0]" argument must be of type string. Received undefined'. An admin who created .env as in the Docker section gets 'Set DATABASE_URL (a PostgreSQL database).'
- **Fix:** Set engines to `>=20.11` (or use `fileURLToPath(new URL('..', import.meta.url))`), and make start `node --env-file-if-exists=.env src/server.mjs` (Node 22+) or say explicitly in the README to export the variables.

### L94. Link checker overwrites a member's link fix: the new, working URL is marked dead for a week
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/checker.mjs:50` (hub-core; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** startChecker selects a batch of up to 12 links (id, url, status, fail_count), then probes each one in turn. Each probe can take up to 40 s (a 20 s HEAD, then a 20 s GET), plus a 3 s wait per host. checkLink then runs `UPDATE links SET status=$2, http_status=$3, fail_count=$4, checked_at=... WHERE id = $1` using the URL and fail_count it read at the start. Nothing checks that the row still has that URL. If a member fixes the link during this window (PUT /v1/links/:id or a revert), applyEdit resets status to 'unchecked' and fail_count to 0 for the new URL. The checker's late UPDATE then stamps the old URL's result onto the fixed link: status 'dead', http_status 404, and checked_at = now(), so the link is not probed again for 7 days. Fixing dead links is the core community flow, and those links are exactly the ones in the checker's 'retry in 6 hours' queue.
- **Scenario:** Link L has URL A, fail_count 1 after one earlier 404. The checker selects L and starts probing A. A tech fixes L to URL B, which works. The hub answers status 'unchecked'. The probe of A returns 404, so fail_count becomes 2 and the result is 'dead'. The checker's UPDATE sets L (now URL B) to dead/404/fail_count 2/checked_at now. Every install syncs L as a dead link, and it stays that way for a week.
- **Fix:** Make the write conditional on the URL that was probed: `... WHERE id = $1 AND url = $6 AND NOT deleted` (pass link.url). If rowCount is 0, drop the result, because the link changed and applyEdit already queued it for a fresh check.

### L95. Feed safety (holdFeeds/feedLimit) only holds while exactly one hub process writes; nothing stops a second one
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/db.mjs:41` (hub-core; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: one replica in railway.json, README and db.mjs say one hub process. Left: a runtime single-writer lock (would 503 during a deploy overlap: owner decision)
- **Problem:** The `open` set of in-flight writes lives in process memory. A process only knows about its own writes, so a second process serving /v1/changes, /v1/orders or partner messages can hand out a seq above a number another process still holds uncommitted, which is the exact skip 28842a1 set out to fix. The assumption is stated only in a code comment. The README and Railway config do not stop an admin from setting replicas > 1 in Railway or running `docker compose up --scale hub=2`, and deploy overlap briefly runs two processes.
- **Scenario:** The admin scales the Railway service to 2 replicas for reliability. Replica 1 commits an order update with seq 101 while replica 2 has a link insert with seq 100 still in flight. A shop served by replica 1 advances its cursor to 101, and link 100 never syncs.
- **Fix:** Enforce single-writer at startup with `pg_try_advisory_lock(<const>)` and exit or refuse writes if the lock is held. Alternatively, derive the limit from the database: keep track of in-flight writes in a table, or use `pg_snapshot_xmin(pg_current_snapshot())` against a txid column on seq'd rows. Document 'do not scale beyond one replica' in the README.

### L96. Seller ratings can be inflated at no cost with a second install and a $0 listing; repeat ratings from one rater and ratings by blocked installs all count
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/market.mjs:46` (hub-commerce; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** reputations() averages every order_ratings row. It does not weight by distinct rater, exclude blocked raters, or require a non-zero amount. Listings may cost 0 cents (line 191 allows v >= 0). Every member has the shared join key, and /v1/register's limit is keyed on a spoofable X-Forwarded-For. So a seller can register a sock-puppet install and run order, accept, ship (fake tracking), rate 5 stars as many times as it likes (4 calls per rating). Admin blocking of the sock puppet doesn't remove its ratings.
- **Scenario:** Seller S lists 'anything' at $0 with qty 1000. A sock install orders 5 times, S accepts and ships each with tracking '1', and the sock rates 5 stars each time. S's listings now show 5.0 (5) to every member.
- **Fix:** Count at most one rating per (rater, rated) pair, ignore orders under a minimum total, and exclude raters who are blocked (join installs NOT blocked), as /usage/top already does. Consider requiring RECEIVED for the buyer's positive ratings.

### L97. Out-of-range integers and malformed feed parameters give 500 instead of 400; orders?limit=0 answers more:true
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/market.mjs:191` (hub-commerce; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** Money and quantity fields are checked with Number.isInteger and >= 0 but have no upper bound, while the columns are int4. A price, quantity or shipping value above 2,147,483,647 (for example a typo of $30,000,000.00) makes Postgres throw 'integer out of range', and the hub answers 500 'Something went wrong on the hub.' The same applies to xpart source priceCents (parts.mjs:99-100) and to /v1/usage when a number sent many times is summed past int4 (usage.mjs:44-47). GET /v1/orders does BigInt(since) on raw input (500 on 'abc'); limit=0 returns more:true with next unchanged, and a negative or NaN limit gives 500.
- **Scenario:** A seller types 30000000 in the price box, which StitchBench's toCents turns into 3,000,000,000 cents, and gets a generic hub error instead of 'price too high'. /v1/usage with 3000 rows of the same number at qty 1e6 returns 500.
- **Fix:** Bound money to a sane maximum (e.g. <= 100_000_000 cents, like MAX_MONEY in stitchbench partners.ts) and qty to <= 1_000_000 everywhere. Validate since with /^\d+$/ and clamp limit to [1, 1000], as /partners/messages already does. Cap summed usage per number.

### L98. Listing titles go into the Discord webhook unescaped, so any member can ping @everyone
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/market.mjs:242` (hub-commerce; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** notify() posts `{ content }` to the community Discord webhook without `allowed_mentions`, so Discord parses mentions in the content. The listing title and brand are member-controlled, and anything like '@everyone' or '<@&role>' in them pings the whole community server. Shared notes (server.mjs:426) have the same problem.
- **Scenario:** A member lists a part titled '@everyone FREE Featherweights' and every member of the community Discord gets a notification. This can be repeated up to the write limit.
- **Fix:** Send `allowed_mentions: { parse: [] }` in every webhook payload, and escape Discord markdown in member-supplied text.

### L99. Partner-join (and register) rate limits key on client-supplied X-Forwarded-For and are trivially bypassed
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/partners.mjs:258` (hub-commerce; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** /v1/partners/join limits 10 tries/hour per `x-forwarded-for.split(',')[0]`. That is the left-most, client-controlled value: a proxy like Railway's appends the real address on the right. Sending a different first entry each time gives unlimited guesses. The partner code space (31^12) keeps guessing impractical, but /v1/register (server.mjs:124) uses the same pattern to protect the admin-chosen join key (minimum 8 characters), so join-key brute force is not limited at all.
- **Scenario:** 12 join attempts with the same XFF: the last two get 429. 12 attempts with XFF '198.51.100.<i>, 203.0.113.9': all are processed (404), with no 429.
- **Fix:** Use the connection's remote address, or the right-most XFF entry added by the trusted proxy count (as StitchBench's TRUST_PROXY does). Also add a global limit per code prefix / total failed joins per hour.

### L100. xpart 'where to buy' source keeps the original shop's attribution after another member changes its URL/vendor/price
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/parts.mjs:187` (hub-authz-sweep; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** The parts cross-reference is an open wiki (any member can PUT /v1/xparts/:id). In writeEntry, when a submitted source carries the id of an existing source on that part, its by_shop (attribution) is preserved from the old row regardless of whether the content changed: `byShop: was ? was.by_shop : shop` (parts.mjs:187). So member C can take a source that trusted member B added, change its url/vendor/priceCents, and the row keeps by_shop = B. StitchBench then renders the attacker-controlled buying link with B's name: '· shared by <B>' (stitchbench src/app/(app)/parts/[id]/page.tsx:149) and '· from <B>' (src/app/(app)/xref/[id]/page.tsx:140), lending a malicious 'where to buy' link false credibility from a trusted shop. Only updatedAt is refreshed on a content change; by_shop is not.
- **Scenario:** Trusted shop B adds source {vendor: 'Real Supplier', url: 'https://real-supplier.example/p/1'} to a popular part. Attacker C PUTs the part with the same source id but url 'https://attacker.example/checkout'. Other shops see the attacker link displayed as 'shared by <B's shop name>' and click it as if B vouched for it.
- **Fix:** When a source's content (vendor/url/price/note) changes, set by_shop to the editing install (who(c).byShop) and updatedAt to now; only preserve by_shop for a byte-for-byte unchanged source. Consider tracking last-editor separately from original contributor and showing 'edited by X'.

### L101. Admin parts import merges new numbers and fits into removed parts and reports them as 'merged'
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/parts.mjs:373` (hub-commerce; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** POST /v1/admin/xparts/import looks up which part owns each number without filtering deleted parts. The sources import (line 505) does filter them. An import entry whose number belongs to a removed or merged-away part therefore adds its new numbers and fits to that invisible deleted part. It also bumps its seq, writes an 'edited' revision and counts it as merged. The data never appears on any install, because the deleted flag makes every client delete the entry.
- **Scenario:** The admin seeds a parts list containing Singer NPS-… after a member removed the old entry. The import answers {added:0, merged:1}, but the new Generic number GEN-… and fit HD3000 go onto the deleted part. No shop ever sees them, and GEN-… is now locked as well (see the removed-part finding).
- **Fix:** Join xparts and treat numbers owned by deleted parts as free: make a new part and move the number off the deleted one, or restore the deleted part with a revision. Report such rows separately (e.g. `onRemoved`) instead of counting them as merged.

### L102. Rolling back a merged part (the History button) silently drops every number, fit and source that came from the merged-away part
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/parts.mjs:334` (hub-commerce; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (earlier; the low pass also fixed a same-millisecond merge/roll-back case in parts.mjs)
- **Problem:** /merge moves the from-part's numbers and sources onto intoId (UPDATE … SET xpart_id), copies its fits and marks from deleted. If someone then uses 'Roll back to this' on the target to undo a wrong merge, revert runs writeEntry(). That deletes all of the target's numbers, fits and sources and re-inserts only the pre-merge snapshot. The merged-in data is not returned to the from-part, which stays deleted with nothing on it, so it disappears from the cross-reference. The only copy left is in the from-part's revisions, which StitchBench cannot reach (the part is deleted locally).
- **Scenario:** A tech merges 'Bobbin case dup' (Babylock BL-…, fits BL137, plus where-to-buy links) into 'Bobbin case' by mistake, then clicks 'Roll back to this' on the 'added' revision of 'Bobbin case'. Afterwards no live part holds BL-…, the BL137 fit or those links, and the merged-away entry cannot be opened in the app.
- **Fix:** Make merge undoable as one step: record the from-id in the merge revision, and when reverting past a 'merged' revision, restore the from-part from its pre-merge snapshot (deleted=false, its numbers, fits and sources) in the same transaction. At minimum, refuse the revert (409) and explain how to unmerge.

### L103. Stale-edit lost update on the community cross-reference: a where-to-buy edit silently drops fits/numbers another shop added
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/parts.mjs:240` (gap; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (already, by earlier round-3 work; re-checked in the low pass)
- **Problem:** PUT /v1/xparts/:id (and StitchBench saveCrossRef, which posts the whole form state) replaces the entry's numbers, fits and sources wholesale from the submitting shop's local copy. There is no version/If-Match check. Because the app's xref edit form is seeded from the locally-synced db.xPart (which can be minutes to a full sync-interval stale), a shop that only meant to add a where-to-buy source re-submits its stale fits/numbers and erases fits or numbers that another shop added since its last sync. This is a different root cause from the reported parts.ts:105 'where to buy' comma-parsing bug and the parts.mjs:342 merge-undo issue: here correctly-formed edits destroy other members' contributions with no conflict shown.
- **Scenario:** Shop A adds a second fit (HD3000) to an entry. Shop B, whose synced copy still shows only MC6700P, opens the entry and adds a supplier link and saves. The hub replaces fits with B's list, so HD3000 is gone for everyone; only history preserves it and only a manual revert restores it.
- **Fix:** Send and check an updatedAt/version on PUT and reject or merge on mismatch (409 like number clashes), or merge numbers/fits additively server-side and only replace sources; at minimum surface a 'this entry changed since you loaded it' conflict in saveCrossRef.

### L104. Discord notifications carry member-controlled text with no allowed_mentions: shop names and note titles can ping @everyone and post masked phishing links
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/server.mjs:70` (hub-core; security, code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** notify() posts `{ content }` to the admin's Discord webhook. The content includes the shop name from /v1/register (anyone with the join key), and note titles and bodies, link titles and brands from any member. Discord parses mentions in webhook content by default when allowed_mentions is absent, so @everyone, @here and role mentions fire. Masked markdown links such as [Janome service manual](https://evil) are rendered.
- **Scenario:** A member registers as "@everyone URGENT: new hub address [stitchhub.example](https://phish)" or posts a note with that title. The hub's trusted webhook pings the whole community Discord with a phishing link that appears to come from the hub.
- **Fix:** Send `allowed_mentions: { parse: [] }` with every webhook payload. Escape Discord markdown and link syntax in interpolated member text (\[, \], (, ), *, _, ~, `, @, <).

### L105. Link URL collisions on edit, revert or concurrent add return 500 'Something went wrong' instead of a 409 the member can act on
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/server.mjs:305` (hub-core; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** POST /links checks for an existing URL and then inserts (check-then-act), so two concurrent adds of the same URL hit the links_url_file unique index and one gets a 500. applyEdit (PUT /links/:id and revert) never checks whether the new URL/file_name already belongs to another link, so the UPDATE violates the unique index and onError returns the generic 500. StitchBench treats 5xx as a transient 'busy or broken moment', so the tech sees a meaningless error and no pointer to the existing link.
- **Scenario:** A tech 'fixes' link X to the URL that link Y already uses, or reverts X to a URL that has since been added as Y. The hub answers 500 'Something went wrong on the hub.' every time.
- **Fix:** In applyEdit and POST /links, catch pg error 23505 on links_url_file (or pre-check inside the transaction) and throw fail(409, 'That link is already on the hub.') with e.linkId set to the existing row's id, as POST already does on its pre-check.

### L106. POST /v1/links/:id/report has no rate limit or existence check: any member can keep the checker busy re-probing and re-publishing every link
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/server.mjs:374` (hub-core; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
