- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The report route runs `UPDATE links SET checked_at = NULL WHERE id = $1` for any id, with no writeLimit (every other member write has one) and no 404. The checker selects `checked_at IS NULL` first, and each check bumps seq. A member looping over all link ids therefore makes the checker re-probe the same links continuously (12 per minute instead of weekly). Manufacturer sites see a steady stream of requests from the hub's user agent and may block it, and every install re-downloads each re-checked link.
- **Scenario:** A member calls /report on all ~3,000 link ids every few minutes. The checker never gets to genuinely stale links, manufacturer sites start answering 403/429 (links flip to 'blocked'), and every shop's sync carries hundreds of link changes per hour.
- **Fix:** Call writeLimit(c) (or a tighter allow(`report:${id}`, 30)), return 404 when rowCount is 0, and ignore reports for links checked within the last hour.

### L107. Admin join key is stored untrimmed while StitchBench trims what members type, so a pasted trailing space locks everyone out
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/server.mjs:515` (hub-core; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** admin.put('/join-key') stores `String(joinKey)` as sent, and admin.html sends `$('join').value` without trimming. StitchBench's joinHub sends `joinKey.trim()` to /v1/register and /v1/upgrade. If the key saved on the hub has leading or trailing whitespace (e.g. pasted from an email), no StitchBench install can ever match it, even from the admin's own invite code, which embeds the untrimmed key.
- **Scenario:** The admin pastes 'join-4f8k2m9x ' with a trailing space and saves. Every new shop gets 403 'That join key isn't right' even though it pasted the exact invite.
- **Fix:** Trim (and reject internal whitespace) in admin.put('/join-key') and when seeding from JOIN_KEY. Compare trimmed values in register and upgrade.

### L108. Link write validation gaps: unbounded URL and edit-onto-existing-URL both return 500 instead of a clean error
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/server.mjs:262` (hub-core; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** In linkFields (server.mjs:255-269) brand/models/title/etc. are length-capped with .slice() but `url` is not (line 262: `url: String(body.url ?? '').trim()`). The links table has a UNIQUE btree index on (url, coalesce(file_name,'')) (migrations/001_init.sql), whose row limit is ~8191 bytes, so any URL longer than that fails the INSERT with a raw 'index row requires N bytes' error surfaced as 500 'Something went wrong on the hub.'. Separately, applyEdit (server.mjs:302-317) does not check for an existing link at the target URL the way the create path does (server.mjs:284-289 returns a friendly 409 with linkId); editing link X to a URL already used by link Y hits the same unique index and returns 500 instead of the 409 the user should get.
- **Scenario:** A tech pastes a very long signed manual URL (>8KB, common for pre-signed cloud links) -> 500. Or a tech fixes link X to point at the same file another link already references -> 500 with no linkId, instead of being told 'that link is already on the hub'.
- **Fix:** Cap url length in linkFields (e.g. .slice(0, 2000)) and validate it. In applyEdit, look up an existing non-self link with the same (url, file_name) and throw the same 409+linkId as the create path.

### L109. Unvalidated query/body params return 500 (and limit=0 loops the sync client) instead of 400
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/server.mjs:208` (hub-core; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** Several core routes turn malformed input into 500s and one into a client loop. /v1/changes: `since = BigInt(query('since')||'0')` throws on non-numeric since, and `limit = Math.min(2000, Number(query('limit')||1000))` yields NaN for non-numeric limit (SQL 'LIMIT NaN' -> 500) and yields 0 for limit=0, which makes `more = rows.length === limit` = (0===0) true with `next` unchanged, so a client paging with limit=0 loops forever. Core JSON routes (register:126, links POST:281, links PUT:321, admin join-key/discord/block/import) call `await c.req.json()` with no .catch, so a malformed or empty/null body is a 500 (TypeError/SyntaxError) rather than a 400 — unlike the partner routes which use `.catch(()=>({}))`.
- **Scenario:** GET /v1/changes?since=abc or ?limit=abc -> 500; ?limit=0 -> {more:true,next:'0'} (a client honoring more/next spins). POST /v1/register with body 'null' -> 500 'Cannot destructure property joinKey of null'; POST /v1/links with '{oops' -> 500.
- **Fix:** Validate/clamp: reject non-numeric since/limit with 400, floor limit at 1. Wrap body reads in try/catch (or `.catch(()=>({}))`) and return 400 for malformed JSON, matching the partner routes.

### L110. Admin import: a 570 KB crafted .gz crashes the whole hub process (uncaught readline RangeError), and every imported row is trusted
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/transfer.mjs:137` (hub-core; security; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** importStream pipes the upload through createGunzip with no size or ratio limit into readline. A single newline-free line longer than about 536M characters makes readline throw `RangeError: Invalid string length` inside a stream event handler. That error is not caught by the for-await or the transaction, and it kills the process. Imported rows are also not validated: settings (a discord_webhook pointing anywhere bypasses the discord.com check, and the join_key is replaced), installs with key hashes the file's author knows (backdoor member keys), photo and figure mime types, and link URLs (validUrl is bypassed, so javascript: or data: URLs reach every install). This needs an admin action, but the typical untrusted input is an export file someone else hands the admin.
- **Scenario:** The admin imports a .gz file they were given, for example to merge another community's hub. The process exits with an uncaught exception and is down until it restarts. Alternatively, the file silently adds installs whose keys the sender holds and points the Discord webhook at the sender's server, which then receives every new note's text.
- **Fix:** Wrap the gunzip stream in a byte counter (for example 2 GB total) and reject lines over a few MB before JSON.parse, using a custom line splitter or `input.on('error')` plus a length check. Handle stream errors so they reject the import instead of crashing. Validate each row with the same rules as the live routes: settings keys on an allowlist with the Discord URL regex, mime in IMAGE, validUrl for links. Show a summary of settings and install changes before COMMIT, or require a dry run.

### L111. An error mid-export (DB hiccup, timeout) crashes the whole hub: pipe() leaves the source stream's 'error' unhandled
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/transfer.mjs:123` (hub-core; code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** `Readable.from(lines()).pipe(zlib.createGzip())` does not forward errors from the source. If a page query inside the lines() generator throws, Readable.from destroys itself and emits 'error' with no listener. That is an uncaught exception, and the process exits. The admin's download also ends mid-stream.
- **Scenario:** On a big hub, the admin clicks 'Download export'. A statement timeout, a connection reset or a concurrent migration makes one page query fail. The hub process dies for all shops, and the admin gets a truncated file or a network error.
- **Fix:** Use `stream.pipeline(Readable.from(lines()), zlib.createGzip(), cb)` (or compose) so errors destroy the chain, and log them. Returning a web stream that errors makes the client see a failed download instead of the process crashing.

### L112. posts.client_id (added in 28842a1) is not exported, so retry-safe notes stop being retry-safe after a hub move
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/transfer.mjs:22` (hub-core; code, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (earlier; the low pass added installs.invited_by and partner_messages.bytes to the export)
- **Problem:** Migration 006 added posts.client_id and the dedupe in POST /v1/posts relies on it, but the export column list for posts was not updated. After export and import, every note loses its client_id. The (install_id, client_id) index then no longer matches retries, so a note the old hub stored just before the move (answer lost, hubId still null in StitchBench) is created a second time when StitchBench retries against the moved hub.
- **Scenario:** A shop shares a note, the connection drops before the answer arrives, and the admin moves the hub to the same address. StitchBench's next sync sends the note again with the same clientId, and the imported hub creates a duplicate.
- **Fix:** Add "client_id" to the posts cols in TABLES. Add a check to the smoke test that compares exported columns against information_schema so new columns can't be missed again.

### L113. The community 'most used parts' list gives one shop's exact monthly part usage to any member (no minimum group size)
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/usage.mjs:66` (gap; security, qa, code; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** GET /v1/usage/top returns, for each part number, the summed qty and tickets, how many shops (`shops`) contributed, and the exact spelling and brand reported. It also reports the top-level shop count for the window and brand filter. There is no minimum-contributors rule. A brand, a one-month window, or any part number only one shop uses reveals that shop's exact consumption and job counts. For a niche brand, the whole list is one identifiable dealer's parts usage for up to 36 months. The feature is presented as community totals ('counts only'). This is a different issue from the reported sum(qty)::int overflow on the same query.
- **Scenario:** A member queries /v1/usage/top?brand=barudan&months=1. The answer says shops:1 and lists every Barudan part number that shop used this month, with exact quantities and ticket counts, e.g. the only Barudan dealer in the community. Stepping `months` from 1 to 36 gives the history month by month.
- **Fix:** Leave out rows (and brand or window combinations) with fewer than about 3 contributing shops, and don't return exact qty and tickets for small groups (round them or bucket them). Don't return the reported spelling or brand when only one shop reported it.

### L114. Installer build uses npm install (not the lock file) with mutable action tags, persisted checkout credentials and no permissions block
- **Where:** stitchbench `.github/workflows/installers.yml:33` (files-library-desktop; security, qa; reproduced; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The job that produces the unsigned Windows installers runs `npm install` for the app. The lock is known to be out of sync (the baseline notes npm ci fails over missing @emnapi entries), so whatever the lock doesn't pin is resolved from the registry at build time and shipped. Lifecycle scripts of every dependency run with actions/checkout's persisted GITHUB_TOKEN in .git/config, and there is no `permissions:` block, so the token has whatever the repo default is. Actions are pinned to moving tags (@v4).
- **Scenario:** A newly published malicious minor version of a transitive dependency that the lock doesn't pin gets pulled into a tagged release build and ends up inside StitchBench Setup.exe. That installer then runs on every shop PC with access to the shop's DB password and data, and there's no code signature to break.
- **Fix:** Regenerate the root lock file so `npm ci` works (the missing @emnapi entries) and use `npm ci` in CI. Add `permissions: { contents: read }` and `persist-credentials: false`. Pin actions to commit SHAs. Sign the installers (INSTALL.md already notes this).

### L115. Backups hold the hub install key, password hashes and all customer data unencrypted, and the docs advise copying them around with no warning
- **Where:** stitchbench `desktop/backup.mjs:14` (files-library-desktop; qa; plausible)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: INSTALL.md warns backups hold logins and the hub key; import temp dir removed. Left: backup encryption (feature)
- **Problem:** Backups skip only the hub cache tables. They include HubState.installKey, the bearer credential push-figures.mjs and hubFetch use to act as the shop on the hub (notes, marketplace, partner messages), plus User.passwordHash and all customer PII. They are written unencrypted to Documents\StitchBench Backups, a folder Windows often syncs to OneDrive. INSTALL.md then tells owners to copy the folder to another drive or a NAS and says only that a backup 'holds the shop's data'. import-from-dev.mjs also writes a full dump to os.tmpdir() and deletes it only on success (line 28 isn't reached if restore throws).
- **Scenario:** A backup folder copied to a shared NAS or synced cloud drive leaks. Whoever has it can impersonate the shop to the community hub and its partner shops (installKey), and can crack staff passwords offline.
- **Fix:** - Leave HubState.installKey out of backups, or encrypt it with a key kept in secrets.json.
- Offer optional backup encryption.
- In INSTALL.md, say that backups contain logins and the hub key and should be stored like the shop PC itself.
- Clean the import temp dir in a finally block.

### L116. import-from-dev.mjs wipes all of the program's data with no safety backup, while its server is running
- **Where:** stitchbench `desktop/import-from-dev.mjs:21` (files-library-desktop; qa; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** INSTALL.md describes the tool as 'copy shop data from another StitchBench database into the program', and the script says 'the program must be running'. It calls restore(), which TRUNCATEs every shop table and loads the other DB's rows. There is no prompt and no 'before restore' safety backup (the tray restore makes one). The live server keeps running against the truncated data during the import.
- **Scenario:** A maintainer runs `node import-from-dev.mjs <url>` against an install that already has real tickets, expecting a merge. Everything entered since the last nightly backup is gone.
- **Fix:** Take `backup(prog, cfg.backupDir, { prune: false })` first and print its path. Refuse when the program's DB already has shops unless --replace is passed. Tell the user to quit the program (or stop the server) first. Reword INSTALL.md as 'replace'.

### L117. join-hub.mjs skips the hub-address check, so a plain-http hub gets the install key and all partner traffic
- **Where:** stitchbench `desktop/join-hub.mjs:21` (files-library-desktop; security; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round3-tail: hubFetch and hubReachable refuse a stored hub address that isn't https or this computer, the same rule as checkHubUrl / join-hub.mjs)
- **Problem:** Settings -> Community hub goes through checkHubUrl (https only, http only for localhost, no credentials, query or hash). The CLI join only strips trailing slashes, POSTs the join key to whatever URL it's given, and stores that URL in HubState. After that, every hubFetch sends `Authorization: Bearer <installKey>` and partner, marketplace and note traffic to that address, including over http:// to a remote host.
- **Scenario:** The owner runs `node join-hub.mjs http://hub.example.com <key>` (from an old note, or typed without the s). The join key and, from then on, the install key and all partner messages cross the internet in clear text, and anyone on the path can take over the shop's hub identity.
- **Fix:** Apply the same rules as checkHubUrl in join-hub.mjs (copy the small function, since the script can't import TS), and have hubFetch refuse a stored non-https, non-localhost URL.

### L118. The Windows program's web server connects to Postgres as the cluster superuser
- **Where:** stitchbench `desktop/main.mjs:222` (files-library-desktop; security; plausible)
- **Status:** OPEN (low pass: a separate app database role on the Windows program can't be tested here)
- **Problem:** embedded-postgres runs initdb with --username=stitchbench, which makes that the bootstrap superuser, and the Next server gets DATABASE_URL for the same user. No SQL injection was found (all raw SQL uses tagged templates). But the server faces the whole shop LAN, and any future injection or Prisma misuse would give COPY ... TO PROGRAM or pg_read_server_files, i.e. code execution as the Windows user who runs the program. Only backup and restore need superuser (session_replication_role), and those run in the Electron main process.
- **Scenario:** Defence in depth: a future injection bug in any server action becomes remote code execution on the shop PC instead of data access.
- **Fix:** After initialise, create an app role (NOSUPERUSER, owning the stitchbench database and its tables) and pass that role to the Next server. Keep the superuser only in main.mjs for migrate, backup and restore.

### L119. E2E specs share install-wide hub state but run in parallel by default; hub.spec leaves/re-joins the hub while market/inventory/partners/offline need it
- **Where:** stitchbench `playwright.config.ts:3` (build-test-infra; qa; reproduced; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** playwright.config.ts sets no `workers` and no `fullyParallel: false`, so `npm run e2e` spreads spec files over half the CPU cores. The hub connection (HubState id=1) and the 'Leave the hub' guard (partnerLink.count ACTIVE across all companies) are install-wide. hub.spec clicks 'Leave the hub' and re-joins. At the same time market.spec and inventory.spec (with HUB_URL) expect 'Connected to', and partners.spec creates ACTIVE partnerships that make hub.spec's leave fail. inventory.spec's community step assumes an earlier spec already joined. partners.spec and offline.spec only clean up if they reach their last steps, so one failed run leaves an ACTIVE partnership that breaks hub.spec on every later run.
- **Scenario:** `HUB_URL=... HUB_JOIN_KEY=... PDF_URL=... npm run e2e` on a 4+ core machine. partners.spec creates a partnership while hub.spec runs, so hub.spec fails at :35. Or hub.spec leaves the hub while market.spec is syncing, so market fails with 'Join the hub first'. Results depend on scheduling.
- **Fix:** Set `workers: 1` (or `fullyParallel: false` plus a project with `dependencies`), and add a global setup that joins the hub once. Give hub-dependent specs their own install/DB, or stop hub.spec from leaving the hub. Wrap partnership teardown in `test.afterAll` so it runs on failure.

### L120. StitchBench railway.json has no healthcheck, so a release whose migration fails on start takes the hosted site down instead of keeping the old deployment
- **Where:** stitchbench `railway.json:3` (build-test-infra; qa; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The hosted start command runs `prisma migrate deploy && tsx prisma/seed.ts --if-empty` before `next start` (INSTALL says the first start 'takes a few minutes to answer'). Without `deploy.healthcheckPath`, Railway treats the new deployment as live once its container starts and retires the previous one. If a migration fails on a shop's real data, or the first seed takes minutes, the service crash-loops (restartPolicyMaxRetries 5) or does not answer, and the old working version is already gone. The hub's railway.json does set `healthcheckPath: "/health"`.
- **Scenario:** A shop redeploys a version whose migration hits a constraint on its data. The new container exits five times and stays down, the previous deployment was already removed, and the shop has no hosted StitchBench until someone rolls back by hand.
- **Fix:** Add a lightweight unauthenticated route (e.g. /api/health that does `SELECT 1`) and set `deploy.healthcheckPath` and `healthcheckTimeout` (long enough for a first-start seed) in railway.json.

### L121. Internal error text reaches the user: raw exception messages go into a UserError and into a /partners?error= link
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/companies.ts:30` (auth-accounts; code; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round3-tail: /partners?error= carries a fixed code mapped to a fixed message in src/app/(app)/partners/page.tsx; the two redirect sites in src/app/signup/page.tsx send a code, not free text)
- **Problem:** createCompany wraps any error from redeemPartnerCode, including Prisma and network errors, as `UserError(`... ${e.message} ...`)`, so safe() returns internal text (query or constraint details, hub URLs, fetch failure causes) to the browser. Next.js normally hides those in production. Signup does the same through a redirect: `/partners?error=${encodeURIComponent(e.message)}` (signup/page.tsx:54). The partners page renders whatever ?error= contains, so any link can also put arbitrary text inside the app's own error box.
- **Scenario:** The hub is unreachable or a DB constraint fails during partner-code redemption, and the new owner sees a raw Prisma or fetch error in a toast or banner. Separately, someone sends a signed-in user /partners?error=Your%20account%20is%20suspended%2C%20call%20555-..., which appears in StitchBench's own alert styling.
- **Fix:** Put e.message in the text only when isUserError(e) (or HubError); otherwise log it and show a generic message. Have the partners page map a short error code to a fixed message instead of echoing free text from the URL.

### L122. One import holds a pooled DB connection for up to 180 s; a few parallel imports by any company admin can stall the hosted app for every tenant
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/inventory-import.ts:130` (parts-inventory; security; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** The round-2 fix made the import all-or-nothing by running up to 5,000 rows (2-3 sequential queries each) in one interactive transaction with timeout 180_000. The transaction keeps one of the pg pool's connections (default max 10, @prisma/adapter-pg/pg-pool) for its whole duration. On a hosted install with ALLOW_SIGNUP, anyone can be the admin of their own company and send about 10 parallel 5,000-row imports (the 16 MB serverActions body limit allows it). That holds every pooled connection for tens of seconds, and all tenants' pages and actions queue behind it.
- **Scenario:** An attacker signs up, captures the importItems action id, and fires 10 concurrent 5,000-row imports, repeating as each finishes. Other shops' requests wait on the exhausted pool.
- **Fix:** Allow one import at a time per company (e.g. a pg advisory lock on the shopId) and reject concurrent ones. Batch the lookups: one findMany of all existing numbers, then createMany/updateMany in chunks, so the transaction takes seconds rather than minutes. Lower the transaction timeout, or give imports a separate small pool.

### L123. UPC lookup has no caching or per-shop limit, so one company can use up the whole install's shared UPCitemdb quota
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/inventory-import.ts:14` (parts-inventory; qa; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** barcodeDetails is an exported server action gated only by requireUser. lookupBarcode calls UPCitemdb every time. On the free trial endpoint the quota is 100 lookups a day per server IP; with UPCITEMDB_KEY it is a paid plan. On a hosted multi-company install every company shares that IP or key, and nothing caches results or limits calls per shop, so one user or script can use it up (or run up cost) for everyone. Static review only (I did not call the external API). The key is sent only in a header to a fixed https host with digits-only input, and I found no leak or SSRF; the 8 s timeout is fine.
- **Scenario:** Any signed-in user of company A calls barcodeDetails 100 times (e.g. by pressing 'Look up barcode online' or replaying the action). Every company on the install then gets 'Today's free barcode lookups are used up (100 a day)' until the next day.
- **Fix:** Cache results (including 'not found') in a small table keyed by GTIN, and add a per-shop daily limit (e.g. a counter row per shop per day) before calling UPCitemdb.

### L124. 'Mark received' / 'Mark shipped' stock changes are lost for good if anything fails after the hub step
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/market.ts:237` (hub-market; code; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** orderAction moves the order on the hub first, and only then adds or removes stock locally (receiveIntoStock / takeFromStock). If the hub's reply is lost, the user sees the OFFLINE error even though the hub is already RECEIVED or SHIPPED. If the local write throws, e.g. P2002 on @@unique([shopId, partNumber]) when receiveIntoStock creates `${partNumber} (own)` for a number shorter than 3 characters that is both consigned and already has an '(own)' copy, the stock change never happens. The page stops offering the step (the hub now says RECEIVED/SHIPPED), so the checkbox can't be used again and nothing records that the stock was never adjusted. The hub step also has no idempotency key.
- **Scenario:** A buyer clicks 'Mark received' with 'Add 2 to my parts stock' ticked. The hub commits RECEIVED, but the response times out (or the part create fails). The stock stays unchanged, and the order page now just says 'Done'.
- **Fix:** Record that stock is owed before calling the hub (e.g. a pending flag on MarketOrder), apply it idempotently keyed by the order id (skip if a StockMove note for this order exists), and retry from sync when the order is RECEIVED/SHIPPED but the flag is still set. Also offer 'Add to stock' on RECEIVED orders that were never added.

### L125. Shelf-count adjustments write a stock-move delta from a stale read (ledger drifts from qtyOnHand)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/parts.ts:91` (parts-inventory; code; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** moveStock mode=count and saveCounts (scan.ts:83-91) read p.qtyOnHand, then in a separate batch transaction set qtyOnHand to the absolute count and record `qty: n - p.qtyOnHand`. A register sale or ticket line that lands between the read and the write changes the real before-value, so the ADJUSTED move no longer matches the change and the move history stops adding up to on-hand.
- **Scenario:** On hand is 5. The tech submits a count of 3 while a register sale of 1 commits between the read and the update. qtyOnHand becomes 3, but the moves are SOLD -1 and ADJUSTED -2 (a net -3 against an actual change of -2), so the history is off by 1 for good.
- **Fix:** Do the count in an interactive transaction that locks the row (`SELECT "qtyOnHand" FROM "Part" WHERE id=$1 FOR UPDATE`), or use `UPDATE ... SET "qtyOnHand"=$n ... RETURNING` with the old value taken from a CTE, and compute the delta from the locked value.

### L126. Customers, machines, tickets and parts can never be deleted or merged, so duplicates and mistakes are permanent
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/records.ts:95` (gap; qa; plausible)
- **Status:** OPEN (low pass: merging and deleting customers/machines is a feature that needs UI and an owner decision)
- **Problem:** No action anywhere deletes or merges a customer, machine, ticket, part or service item. The only deletes are ticket lines, payments, photos, notes, part sources and hub items. Duplicate customers and machines from failed check-ins (reported separately), from the unsearchable check-in customer <select>, or from the phone-search gap stay forever and split a customer's history. A mistyped part number stays reserved under the shop's @@unique([shopId, partNumber]). A ticket opened by mistake keeps its number and counts on the dashboard until someone closes it with a fake status. A customer's request to remove their details can't be honoured without database access.
- **Scenario:** Static: grep for .delete(/deleteMany( in src/actions and src/lib finds no customer/machine/ticket/part deletion. The UI offers no delete or merge on /customers/[id], /machines/[id], /tickets/[id] or /parts/[id].
- **Fix:** Add 'Merge into...' for customers and machines (move tickets, machines, photos, repair jobs, sales), owner/admin delete for records with no references, and a void/cancel state for tickets opened by mistake. For parts, deactivate rather than delete to keep stock history.

### L127. Voiding an earlier day's sale (or removing a ticket payment) rewrites that day's totals and records nothing on the day the money went back
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/register.ts:320` (gap; code, qa; reproduced; plausible)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: a voided sale stays on its own day and comes off the day of the void. Left: removePayment still deletes a ticket payment (needs a reversing-payment decision)
- **Problem:** voidSale has no date limit. It flips the sale to VOIDED and puts stock back. The register sales report selects sales by createdAt in the day range and totals only status COMPLETED (register/sales/page.tsx:19-23), so the voided sale disappears from its original day: tender totals, 'Sales tax collected' and consigned totals all shrink after that day was counted and closed. The day the void actually happens gets no negative entry, because voidedAt is never used in reports. removePayment does the same for ticket payments: it hard-deletes the Payment row (tickets.ts:375), so past sales-log months change with no record. There is no return-by-receipt flow, so opening last week's receipt and pressing 'Void sale' is the easiest way to refund a returned item.
- **Scenario:** Monday's drawer and card batch are reconciled and Monday's tax is recorded. On Friday a customer returns Monday's $80 purchase, and the admin opens the Monday receipt and voids it, handing back $80 cash. Monday's report now shows $80 less cash and less tax than was counted. Friday's report shows nothing for the $80 that left the drawer, so Friday's drawer is $80 short with no trace.
- **Fix:** Allow a void only on the sale's own shop day. Otherwise require a return sale, which records the negative amounts on the day they happen. Alternatively, have reports count a void as a negative entry on voidedAt's day. For ticket payments, record a reversing (negative) payment instead of deleting the row.

### L128. The repair tech can set COLLECTED on a partner shop's ticket, which marks the sewing shop's job as picked up so it can no longer be charged at the register
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/tickets.ts:140` (bench-workflow; qa; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** setStatus accepts any status for any ticket, including one from a partner sewing shop. For those tickets 'Collected' should come from the sewing shop (job.pickedup / register sale), not from the bench. If the tech sets COLLECTED, pushJobUpdates sends status COLLECTED (and sets pickedUpAt on the repair copy, partners.ts:412). The sewing shop's job.update handler then sets pickedUpAt (partners.ts:787). The sewing shop's job page only offers 'Handing it back' for READY_*/RETURNED statuses (repairs/[id]/page.tsx:25), and the register's pickupJobs only lists READY_IN_TRUCK/READY_FOR_PICKUP/RETURNED_UNREPAIRED (register.ts:96). The unpaid machine that is still on the sewing shop's shelf can no longer be rung up and shows as 'Customer has it'.
- **Scenario:** A bench tech treats a partner ticket like a trade-account ticket and moves it straight from 'Ready - in truck' to 'Collected by customer' when dropping it at the quilt shop. On the next sync the sewing shop's /repairs moves the job to 'Finished'. When the customer comes to pay, 'Repair pickup' on the register doesn't list it.
- **Fix:** In setStatus, if a RepairJob with side 'repair' exists for the ticket, refuse COLLECTED (and possibly ABANDONED) with a UserError that says the sewing shop marks it collected when the customer picks it up. Alternatively, leave COLLECTED out of the StatusSelect options for partner tickets.

### L129. Local cross-reference clash check ignores brand, unlike the hub, and pushes users to merge different parts
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/xref.ts:118` (parts-inventory; code; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** numberOwner blocks a save when any number key matches any existing entry's numberKeys, regardless of brand. It also keeps keys shorter than 3 characters. The hub's checkNumbers (stitchhub src/parts.mjs:137-147) treats a number as taken only for the same brand_key and number_key. The UI refuses entries the hub would accept, and the message tells the user to 'merge the two', which on the hub combines two unrelated parts' fits and numbers for every shop.
- **Scenario:** The xref has 'Singer, 12345' (a presser foot). A tech adds 'Janome, 12345' (a different screw). The app answers 'One of those numbers is already on "Presser foot". Open that part and merge the two instead.' If they follow it, the hub merges the screw into the foot entry for everyone.
- **Fix:** Mirror the hub: compare (brandKey, numberKey) pairs against XPart.numbers, or drop the local pre-check and surface the hub's 409 (which carries partId) as the error with a link to that entry.

### L130. Every ticket page loads (and ships to the browser) the shop's entire parts list for a datalist; check-in loads every customer
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/(app)/tickets/[id]/page.tsx:73` (gap; code; reproduced; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round3-tail: the check-in customer box is a live search (src/components/customer-picker.tsx, reuses findCustomers) instead of loading every retail customer; createTicket's customerId field unchanged)
- **Problem:** The ticket page, the most-used page on the bench, runs an unbounded db.part.findMany({ where: { shopId } }) and renders one <option> per part into the 'parts-list' datalist, which goes in both the HTML and the RSC payload. A 'both' shop that imports a supplier sheet (up to 5,000 rows per import, repeatable) sends hundreds of KB to MBs on every ticket view, and every revalidatePath after each line or payment re-renders it. The list also includes partners' consigned items, which addLine refuses ('isn't in your parts'). /tickets/new loads every RETAIL customer into a <select> the same way (tickets/new/page.tsx:14). This is separate from the reported dashboard low-stock query.
- **Scenario:** A shop with 15,000 SKUs opens a ticket on a tablet over shop Wi-Fi. Each ticket render transfers several MB and builds a 15,000-option datalist, and adding each part or payment triggers the same full re-render.
- **Fix:** Replace the datalist with a debounced server search (findItems-style, take 25, own stock only), and do the same for the customer picker on check-in.

### L131. CSV downloads have no UTF-8 byte order mark, so Excel garbles accented names
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/api/export/[what]/route.ts:89` (register-money; security; reproduced; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** Both CSV routes send `text/csv; charset=utf-8` without a BOM. When a .csv is opened by double-click, Excel for Windows ignores the HTTP charset and reads it in the ANSI code page unless the file starts with EF BB BF. Customer names, descriptions and addresses with accents come out garbled in the spreadsheet the shop is told to keep "or take to another system".
- **Scenario:** The customer "José Núñez" is exported and opened in Excel on the shop's Windows PC; it shows "JosÃ© NÃºÃ±ez".
- **Fix:** Put "﻿" in front of the body in both routes (and use \r\n line endings for Excel), e.g. new Response("﻿" + body, ...).

### L132. Drawing one figure from the shop PC's library downloads the whole manual and keeps every opened PDF in memory
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/components/manual-figure.tsx:27` (files-library-desktop; code; reproduced; plausible)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: only the last 3 opened PDFs kept. Left: Range requests for /api/library/file and pdf.js url loading
- **Problem:** When a device has no local copy, manualPath() points at the shop PC. loadDoc() then fetches the entire PDF through /api/library/file (fileAt reads r.blob(), wraps it in a File, then arrayBuffer: several copies) and passes `data` to pdf.js. /api/library/file does not support Range requests, so pdf.js cannot load only the page it needs. Parsed documents are cached in a module-level Map that is never evicted or destroy()ed.
- **Scenario:** A tech on the road (Tailscale over cellular) taps Show figure on a machine page. The phone downloads a 60-150 MB service manual to draw one crop. Opening figures from several manuals in one visit keeps all of them in the pdf.js worker until the tab runs out of memory. This hits a fresh Windows install hardest, since FigureImage starts empty unless upload-media.ts was run.
- **Fix:** Support Range in /api/library/file (Accept-Ranges, 206) and call pdfjs.getDocument({ url, disableAutoFetch: true, disableStream: false }) for server paths. Or have the desktop server render the crop. Cap or evict the docs cache and call destroy() on eviction.

### L133. Partner sync lease has no owner and no renewal; non-idempotent handlers depend on a partnerSeen row written after the apply
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/partners.ts:580` (partners; security, code; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round3-tail: applyOnce writes the partnerSeen row and applies the message in one transaction (seen insert first, skipDuplicates); consign.place/return/count are idempotent by their data id through PartnerSeen keys; the hub link fetch happens before the transaction)
- **Problem:** withSyncLease takes a fixed 5-minute lease and, in finally, clears partnerLockUntil unconditionally. A sync longer than 5 minutes (many links with a slow hub: up to 300 sends per pushOutbox, each with a 30 s timeout, plus up to 50 pages of pulls) lets a second server process of a hosted install take the lease. When the first finishes, it clears the second's lease as well. Duplicate application is guarded only by a check-then-act on partnerSeen (lines 540-545), and the seen row is written after applyMessage, outside its transaction. Several handlers are not idempotent by themselves: job.paid (817-831) reads paidWhere and then creates a SHOP_COLLECTED payment; consign.place (941-945, receiveConsigned) increments stock and consignItem.placed; job.reply and job.cancel append notes; job.new creates the Machine outside its transaction (1059). The ids that consign.place, consign.return and consign.count carry (`id: crypto.randomUUID()`) are never checked. Two overlapping pulls, or a crash between the apply and the seen row, can apply the same message twice: a double payment on the ticket, or doubled consigned stock at the sewing shop.
- **Scenario:** On a hosted install with 2+ server processes, one sync runs past 5 minutes, another process acquires the lease and pulls the same page, and both apply a consign.place. The sewing shop's inventory and placed count show twice the stock. A crash or redeploy between applyMessage and partnerSeen.create has the same effect for that one message.
- **Fix:** Store a random owner token with the lease, extend it while running (for example before each page and each outbox batch), and release it only `WHERE partnerLockOwner = $token`; abort the run when renewal fails. Apply each message and insert its partnerSeen row in one transaction, with the seen insert first so a unique conflict aborts a duplicate. Make handlers idempotent on their own: key consign.place, return and count by message id; make job.paid a conditional update (`updateMany where paidWhere not in ('shop','split')`) before creating the payment; create the Machine inside the job.new transaction.

### L134. The hub (or whoever a pasted long partner code points at) can create partnerships on any company of an install and forge or replay any partner message: link.created and the missed-link lookup are trusted without a local invite and nothing is signed end to end
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/partners.ts:670` (partners; code; plausible)
- **Status:** OPEN (low pass: needs a local pending-invite record and end-to-end signing (design + hub change))
- **Problem:** applyMessage accepts link.created for whatever toShopRef and toSide the hub names (lines 670-673). saveLinkFromHub creates the PartnerLink and, on the repair side, a trade account, without checking that this shop ever created a matching invite or redeemed a code. hubLinkOf doesn't check that the link's shopRef for that side equals the local shopId. The missed-link path does the same using the hub's /v1/partners/links answer (lines 680-686). Partner messages are plain JSON relayed by the hub with no MAC, so the hub can inject job.new (tickets and photos), consign.place (inventory), settle.paid (up to $100,000 per message) or replay any message under a new id. It can also read all content: customer first name and last initial, serials, photos, prices, settlements. That contradicts the relay's own comment 'The hub doesn't read them'. On a hosted multi-company StitchBench, the community hub's operator is a third party to every tenant. An install that isn't on a hub joins whatever URL a pasted 'stitchpartner:' code carries (redeemPartnerCode join path).
- **Scenario:** A compromised or malicious hub sends link.created with toShopRef set to company B on a hosted install. B never made or entered a code, yet B gets a 'partner'. The hub then sends settle.paid with netCents 10,000,000 and consign.place rows, and B's statements and inventory show them as coming from a partner.
- **Fix:** Keep a local record of pending invites (shopId, code hash, expiry) and redeemed codes. Accept link.created, and the missed-link recovery, only for a shop that has one, and check that l[side].shopRef === shopId. For authenticity, derive a per-link key from the partner code that is handed over in person (e.g. HKDF(code, 'stitchpartner-v1')), which the hub never learns, and HMAC every message (type, id, data) with it. Reject messages without a valid MAC, and use the MAC-protected id to dedupe replays. Optionally encrypt the data too.

### L135. Catalog snapshots pile up (never coalesced) and sent outbox rows are never pruned: full catalogs and intake photos accumulate in the database and in every desktop backup, and a hub page can be 200 x 4 MB
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/partners.ts:453` (partners; code; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** pushCatalogs queues a full catalog (up to 5000 items, roughly 1 MB of JSON) whenever the stock or price hash changes and 10 minutes have passed. The hash is marked sent at enqueue time, not delivery, so while the hub or the partner is offline one full snapshot per link per 10 minutes piles up. All of them are later sent and applied, although only the last one matters. No code deletes sent PartnerOutbox rows (grep finds no partnerOutbox.delete*), so every catalog and every job.new with base64 photos (up to ~3.5 MB) stays in the DB forever. desktop/backup.mjs copies every table into each of the 30 kept backups. On the hub, /v1/partners/messages returns up to 200 messages per page with no byte budget. A partner catching up after a day or more offline can make the hub load and serialize hundreds of MB in one response, and the app then r.json()'s it.
- **Scenario:** A repair shop offers 5000 parts and sells steadily, so the stock hash changes more than every 10 minutes. Its sewing-shop partner's PC is off over a weekend, and ~288 catalog messages (~1 MB each) queue on the hub. When the sewing shop opens StitchShop, its first pull asks the hub for 200 of them in one response (~200 MB+), which can exhaust a small hub container. Meanwhile each shop's database and each of its 30 backups keeps growing by every snapshot and photo it ever sent.
- **Fix:** When queuing a catalog, delete (or mark superseded) any unsent catalog row for the same link. Prune sent outbox rows after a short retention (or null their data once sentAt is set). On the hub, cap each page by bytes (e.g. stop at ~8 MB) and drop catalog messages superseded by a newer catalog on the same link.

### L136. An incoming message that fails for any reason 3 syncs in a row (~3 minutes) is set aside permanently with no way to re-apply it, and later messages about the same record are then silently ignored
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/partners.ts:550` (partners; code; plausible)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: job.new transaction timeout 30 s. Left: the 3-syncs set-aside and a re-apply by seq (hub call + column)
- **Problem:** skip = e instanceof PartnerDataError || tries >= 3. With PARTNER_SYNC_SECONDS=60, a transient failure lasting a few minutes permanently drops a message: a Prisma interactive-transaction timeout (default 5 s) while newJobFromPartner writes several MB of photos, a lock wait, or the hub lookup in the missed-link path. PartnerFailure.data is storable(m.data), which strips photos and truncates to a 2,000-character preview, so even manual recovery is impossible. There is a 'Send again' for outgoing messages but nothing for incoming ones. After a set-aside job.new, every job.update, job.paid and job.cancel for that job hits 'if (!job) return' and is marked seen.
- **Scenario:** On a busy shop PC, a job.new with six photos exceeds the 5 s transaction timeout three syncs in a row. The machine never gets a ticket at the repair shop, and the later job.paid is ignored too.
- **Fix:** Auto-skip only PartnerDataError. For other errors, keep retrying with back-off without blocking the other companies' messages: keep a per-message pending queue, or a per-shop cursor. Add a 'Try again' button that re-fetches the message from the hub by seq, since the hub keeps it for 365 days, and re-applies it.

### L137. Hub is only safe as a single process: two instances starting together crash in migrate(), and the round-2 feed-position fix is per-process
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/db.mjs:83` (build-test-infra; qa; reproduced; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** migrate() reads hub_migrations and applies pending files without an advisory lock. Two processes starting on the same database (two replicas, an overlapping deploy, or a manual `npm start` beside the container) both try the same file, and the loser dies on a duplicate pg_class entry. Railway's ON_FAILURE restart usually recovers. The feed safety added in 28842a1 (holdFeeds/feedLimit) keeps its open-write marks in process memory; the comment says 'One hub process writes the database (Railway runs one)'. With a second process, a feed served by process B can hand out a cursor past a seq that process A has taken but not yet committed, so installs permanently skip that row. That is the bug the fix was meant to close. SIGTERM calls pool.end() and exits without draining, which widens the window during a deploy switchover. Nothing in the README, railway.json or docker-compose says the hub must never run more than one process.
- **Scenario:** Two hub containers start on a fresh or upgraded database. One logs `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "pg_class_relname_nsp_index" ... (change_seq, 2200) already exists` and exits. With two replicas serving, a link fix committed by one process can be skipped by installs syncing through the other.
- **Fix:** Wrap migrate() in `SELECT pg_advisory_lock(<const>)` / unlock on a dedicated client. Document (README and railway.json `numReplicas: 1`) that the hub must run as exactly one process, or move the feed limit to a DB-level mechanism, e.g. `pg_snapshot_xmin`-based visibility or a commit-ordered outbox, so it holds across processes.

### L138. Re-rating an order re-sends all of the seller's listings to every member each time (sync amplification)
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/market.mjs:519` (hub-commerce; qa; reproduced; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** Each POST /orders/:id/rate by a buyer bumps seq on every non-REMOVED listing of the seller, and 'Rating again replaces the earlier one'. There is no limit on re-rating beyond the general 300 writes/hour. A buyer can re-rate one order 300 times an hour. Each time, every member's /v1/changes re-downloads all of that seller's listings (with photos metadata and reputations), and each re-rate also appends an order event. For a seller with thousands of listings this multiplies sync traffic and hub load for the whole community.
- **Scenario:** Buyer re-rates one order 10 times: the next /v1/changes page for an unrelated member contains all 20 seller listings again (would be 5000 x 300/hour for a large seller). The order carries 14 events.
- **Fix:** Bump listings only when the seller's rounded avg or count actually changes. Rate-limit re-rating per order (e.g. once per hour, or allow changes only for 30 days). Alternatively, serve seller reputation separately from listings so ratings don't touch listing seq.

### L139. xpart history is unpaginated; a vandal can make it too large for the app to load (blocking rollback)
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/parts.mjs:319` (hub-commerce; qa; reproduced; plausible)
- **Status:** FIXED (round3-tail: xref/[id]/history.tsx loads pages of 50 with a "Load older" button (before=<oldest id>, X-History-More); the old src/components/xref-history.tsx is removed)
- **Problem:** GET /v1/xparts/:id/history returns every revision with its full snapshot (up to 3000 fits each), with no LIMIT. StitchBench's History button fetches it all through a server action into the browser. Each member PUT adds a full snapshot (about 135 KB with 3000 fits). A vandal at the 300 writes/hour limit adds about 40 MB/hour to one part's history, and the History panel, the app's only rollback tool, becomes slow or fails to load for that part.
- **Scenario:** 20 edits alternating 0 / 3000 fits leave a 2.7 MB history response. 300 edits make it about 40 MB.
- **Fix:** Paginate history (limit, before) and return snapshot summaries, with the full snapshot fetched per revision for rollback. Collapse repeated edits by the same install within minutes.


## Info

### I1. Test coverage: the Windows program (backup/restore/reference/migrate/config/Electron guards) has no automated tests
- **Where:** stitchbench `desktop/backup.mjs:127` (files-library-desktop; qa; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** No test exercises the following:
- backup() or restore(): snapshot consistency, a truncated or corrupt .gz rolling back, 'newer version' refusal, fix-ups, cursor reset, reference-table handling, pruning.
- loadReference() or migrate().
- loadConfig(), secrets.json and the .bak recovery.
- The main.mjs SETUP_TOKEN, restore flow and Electron navigation/permission guards.

The issues above (stale reference data after restore, prune with keep=0/null) would have been caught by a small node test against a throwaway Postgres. tests/*.spec.ts cover only the web app, and shop-library.spec.ts is opt-in.
- **Scenario:** A regression in restore() or migrate() ships in an installer and is found only when a shop restores after a disk failure.
- **Fix:** Add node:test suites that run backup, then mutate, then restore against a scratch database (superuser role for replica mode), plus loadReference/migrate idempotency and pruning edge cases. Run them in the installers workflow before packaging.

### I2. Untested critical path: the Windows program (desktop migrate/reference/backup/restore/config) has no automated test, and CI ships its installer untested
- **Where:** stitchbench `desktop/migrate.mjs:7` (build-test-infra; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** desktop/migrate.mjs (its own migration runner writing _prisma_migrations), reference.mjs (hash-based reference reload), backup.mjs (nightly backup, restore with safety backup, data fix-ups), config/secrets handling and the start-up sequence in main.mjs are exercised by nothing. shop-library.spec needs a running Windows program and is opt-in. The installers workflow builds and uploads the .exe without starting it. I checked the runner by hand: on an empty DB it applies 19 migrations, a second run applies 0, prisma migrate status/diff report no drift, and `npm run release` afterwards finds nothing pending.
- **Scenario:** A migration that works under prisma migrate deploy but not inside migrate.mjs's explicit BEGIN/COMMIT (e.g. using an enum value added by ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE in the same file), or a restore that drops data, reaches shops in an installer with nothing catching it.
- **Fix:** Add node:test tests that run migrate.mjs and reference.mjs against a throwaway Postgres (both fresh and re-run), and backup/restore round-trips via pg_dump/psql (backup.mjs logic without Electron). Run them in the installers workflow before electron-builder.

### I3. Untested critical path: sewing-shop repair job edge flows (decline, second estimate, cancel at counter, hand back without payment, intake validation)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/repair-jobs.ts:147` (bench-workflow; qa; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: partners.spec checks a blank problem is refused and no duplicate customer. Left: decline, second estimate, cancel and hand-back specs
- **Problem:** partners.spec.ts covers one intake, one estimate approval and payment at the register. It does not cover answerEstimate DECLINED, a second WAITING_CUSTOMER_OK round (broken, see the finding above), cancelJob before pickup, markPickedUp for RETURNED_UNREPAIRED or unpaid jobs, intake validation errors (orphan customers, see above), or the claim-tag content beyond the 'CLAIM TAG' text.
- **Scenario:** The second-estimate dead end and the duplicate-customer bug were not caught by any test.
- **Fix:** Extend partners.spec with a decline, a re-estimate after approval, a counter cancellation and a no-payment hand-back, and an intake submitted first with a whitespace-only problem (assert a single customer).

### I4. Untested critical path: ticket lifecycle and role rules (status timestamps, closed-ticket lock, admin-only payment removal, concurrent edits)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/actions/tickets.ts:140` (bench-workflow; qa; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE (round3-tail: tests/roles.spec.ts: a tech has no People/company settings/Companies, part money fields are kept, refunds and removing a payment are refused, no void form, readyAt/closedAt/warrantyUntil set and cleared, a collected ticket's bill is locked). Left: voidSale's server-side requireAdmin is only checked through the UI; concurrent saveTicketText edits and the dashboard overdue/storage-fee warning are untested
- **Problem:** No spec changes ticket status except partners.spec (WAITING_CUSTOMER_OK/READY_FOR_PICKUP on a partner ticket). Nothing tests readyAt/closedAt/warrantyUntil on setStatus, reopening, the dashboard's overdue/storage-fee warning, the TECH closed-ticket rule in removeLine, removePayment's requireAdmin, or saveTicketText under concurrent edits. The bugs above in these paths all went unnoticed.
- **Scenario:** The round-1 TECH restrictions can be bypassed (addLine, negative addPayment) without any test failing.
- **Fix:** Add an e2e spec: owner closes a paid ticket; tech tries remove line, add discount, negative payment and remove payment (expect refusals); status round-trips check readyAt/closedAt and the invoice header.

### I5. Untested critical path: cross-shop isolation of bench pages, actions and photo API
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/app/api/photos/[id]/route.ts:6` (bench-workflow; qa; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round3-tail: tests/tenant.spec.ts with the second company from scripts/e2e-setup.ts: bench pages, /api/photos GET and POST, and replayed ticket/machine/customer/part actions with another company's ids all 404 or are refused, rows unchanged). Not covered: /repairs/[id] (needs a partner RepairJob)
- **Problem:** No spec signs in as a second shop and tries another shop's ids on /tickets/[id], /tickets/[id]/invoice, /machines/[id], /customers/[id], /repairs/[id], /repairs/[id]/tag, /api/photos/[id], /api/photos POST (machineId/ticketId), or the ticket actions (removeLine, removePayment, setStatus, toggleChecklist, deletePhoto, updateMachine, saveCustomer). Scoping is currently correct: my replays of removeLine and toggleChecklist with shop A ids from shop B returned 'Line not found.' / 'Ticket not found.'. But on the hosted multi-shop app this is the highest-impact class of regression and it has no guard.
- **Scenario:** A future edit that drops a shopId filter from, say, ownTicket or /api/photos/[id] would pass every existing test.
- **Fix:** Add a tenant-isolation spec that creates two shops (scripts/e2e-setup.ts twice), records shop A's ids, and asserts 404 or 'not found' for each route and replayed action as shop B.

### I6. Untested critical path: money math (ticket totals, register tax/discounts/returns/voids, statements) has no unit tests and only two happy-path e2e totals
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/register-math.ts:1` (build-test-infra; qa; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round-3 low pass, 30 Sep; build, migrations, drift check, unit tests, Playwright specs and hub smoke test pass)
- **Problem:** Round 1 rewrote register totals, discounts, tax and yard quantities 'the same way on client and server', and changed partner commission/statements. No test runner exists for src/lib/register-math.ts, ticket-math.ts or partner statements. E2E coverage is smoke.spec paying one ticket balance (amount not asserted) and partners.spec asserting one $144.00 sale and one $118.80/$146.80 statement. Nothing covers sales tax, line/whole-sale discounts, fabric in eighths, split tender and change, returns, voids, refunds, rounding at half cents, or money typed as 19.999 / 1,000.50 / $5.
- **Scenario:** A change to rounding or tax order produces a one-cent drift between client total and server-charged total, or a wrong sales-tax total on invoices. Nothing fails until a shop's books disagree.
- **Fix:** Add node:test or vitest unit tests for register-math/ticket-math/partner statement functions with table-driven cases (tax on/off per line, discounts, eighth-yard quantities, returns, rounding, parsing of user-typed amounts), and run them in CI.

### I7. Test gap: the 'Download to library' guards (private addresses, redirects, size limit, file names) have no tests
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/safe-fetch.ts:93` (hub-market; qa; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: tests/unit/safe-fetch.test.ts (7 tests). Left: inboxName (private in a "use server" file)
- **Problem:** hub.spec.ts only downloads an allow-listed localhost PDF. Nothing tests that loopback, RFC1918, link-local/metadata, IPv4-mapped IPv6 and names resolving to private IPs are refused. Also untested: redirects re-checked at each hop, a non-PDF body refused, the 300 MB cap, and inboxName sanitising hub-supplied fileName values (../, \, reserved names). This is the fetch guard round 2 rewrote. It worked in my manual checks, but a regression would be silent.
- **Scenario:** A future change to publicLookup or checkUrl re-opens SSRF from hub-supplied links and no test fails.
- **Fix:** Add unit tests for fetchPublic, stubbing dns.lookup and using a local redirecting server with FETCH_ALLOW_HOSTS set for the first hop only, plus inboxName cases.

### I8. Untested critical path: ticket money math (tax with discounts, commission, over-discounts, part stock return on removal)
- **Where:** stitchbench `src/lib/ticket-math.ts:14` (bench-workflow; qa; confirmed)

Nothing tests ticketTotals with a non-zero tax rate and a DISCOUNT (the proportional tax base added in round 1), trade-account and partner commission, negative or over-sized discounts, yard-quantity parts, removeLine returning stock, or that the jobSnapshot copy in partners.ts gives the same totals.

Nothing covers role limits (a TECH accepting orders or setting pay instructions) or receiving into stock with duplicate or consigned part numbers. These paths produced the medium findings above.
