- **Where:** stitchbench `tests/market.spec.ts:33` (gap; qa; confirmed)
- **Status:** OPEN (low pass: needs a new UI-driven or multi-company spec; not attempted in the low pass)
- **Problem:** Several cross-cutting hub<->bench contract paths in this group have no automated test: (1) the link checker's SSRF/redirect/private-address behaviour (stitchhub has LINK_CHECKER off in tests and smoke.mjs doesn't exercise it); (2) concurrent/stale edits to a community cross-reference entry (two members editing, lost updates) and to a marketplace listing (edit after an accept resets qty); (3) partner outbox recovery after a member is blocked then unblocked. These are exactly the flows where I reproduced data loss/oversell above. This complements, not duplicates, the reported market/partner/hub coverage gaps by naming the concurrency and checker contracts specifically.
- **Scenario:** A regression in checker address filtering, xref replace-vs-merge, or listing qty handling would ship undetected because no spec drives two members editing the same hub record or asserts the checker refuses private targets.
- **Fix:** Add a hub test that submits a private-address link and asserts it is not probed/leaked; a two-client xref/listing test asserting concurrent edits don't silently drop data; and a partner-sync test that blocks/unblocks a member and asserts queued messages recover.

### I15. Untested critical path: offline mode (round 2) is covered only by a phased spec whose driver script is not in the repo
- **Where:** stitchbench `tests/offline.spec.ts:1` (build-test-infra; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: README lists offline.spec and its phase driver
- **Problem:** offline.spec.ts must be 'Run in phases by an outside script that cuts the connection between them (OFFLINE_PHASE): prepare ... down (the hub refuses connections), hang (the hub accepts but never answers), back'. No such script exists in either repo, README does not list the spec among the opt-in suites, and it needs OFFLINE_STATE plus PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS. Without it the spec skips, so the round-2 behaviours ('No connection to the community hub' within seconds, outbox ordering after reconnect, Offline-since banner) are unverified by anything anyone can run.
- **Scenario:** A change to hub timeouts or the partner outbox makes pages hang for 30 s or reorder partner messages when the internet drops. The only test for it cannot be run by another developer or CI.
- **Fix:** Commit the phase driver (e.g. scripts/e2e-offline.mjs that starts a test hub, runs prepare, kills it for down, runs a TCP black-hole for hang, restarts it for back) and document it under Opt-in suites.

### I16. No automated tests cover register returns, refunds or register-math
- **Where:** stitchbench `tests/partners.spec.ts:155` (register-money; security, qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** OPEN (low pass: needs a new UI-driven or multi-company spec; not attempted in the low pass)
- **Problem:** The only register e2e flow is a cash sale in partners.spec.ts. Nothing exercises Return mode, cash or card refunds, exchanges, whole-sale discounts or TECH restrictions, and saleTotals/lineNet have no unit tests. That is how the round-1 change broke cash refunds without anyone noticing.
- **Scenario:** The next change to the tender checks or rounding ships without anything catching it.
- **Fix:** Add a register.spec.ts (sale with cash change, cash refund, card refund, exchange, tech attempting a refund or discount) and small tsx unit tests for saleTotals and lineNet rounding.

### I17. Several partner critical paths in scope have no automated test coverage
- **Where:** stitchbench `tests/partners.spec.ts:1` (partners; qa; confirmed)
- **Status:** OPEN (low pass: needs a new UI-driven or multi-company spec; not attempted in the low pass)
- **Problem:** tests/partners.spec.ts exercises one end-to-end happy path (set-up-sewing-shop, accept agreement v1 on both sides, consign+sell one item, one estimate approve, pay at register, one order place/deliver/receive, settle, end) plus a hosted-company-code path, and stitchhub test/smoke.mjs covers the relay routes. Within this review's scope the following critical paths are not covered by any spec: terms proposal + versioning + accept-a-change (proposeTerms/acceptTerms version bump — only v1 acceptance is tested); consignment shelf count (countConsigned) and consigned returns (returnConsigned/consignReturned, including the over-return cap); partner order accept and cancel transitions (only deliver/receive are tested); job cancel and estimate-declined; the message quarantine/retry/resend path (PartnerFailure/PartnerOutbox back-off, resendPartnerMessage) and the one-sync-at-a-time lease. These are the areas where the confirmed bugs above live.
- **Scenario:** A regression in terms versioning, consignment count/return math, order accept/cancel guards, or the retry/quarantine machinery would not be caught by CI, since no spec drives those flows.
- **Fix:** Add e2e coverage for at least: propose->accept a terms change (assert version bump and both sides converge), consignment count + return (assert shelf math and stock correction on both shops), order accept and both-side cancel, and a forced hub-refusal to assert a message is quarantined and can be resent.

### I18. Test coverage: library and figure server routes are barely tested, and the existing figure/shop-library specs aren't run (figure.spec fails on the pinned browser)
- **Where:** stitchbench `tests/shop-library.spec.ts:4` (files-library-desktop; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: figure.spec skip guard and README. Left: library route tests
- **Problem:** shop-library.spec.ts is skipped unless SHOP_LIBRARY=1, and it checks only two traversal strings (`../Windows/win.ini`, `CLAUDE.md`). Nothing tests:
- encoded or backslash traversal, symlinks or directories on GET /api/library/file;
- POST /api/library/file (scan to the shop PC _inbox: non-PDF, names, size caps, chunked bodies);
- POST /api/figures authorization (TECH vs OWNER vs another company, box validation, hub push);
- the /api/figures/<id> headers.

figure.spec.ts needs MANUAL_DIR, wasn't run in the baseline, and fails on the pinned Chromium 141 (see the pdf.js finding).
- **Scenario:** The cross-company figure overwrite and the chunked-upload bypass above passed review and CI unnoticed.
- **Fix:** Add request-level Playwright tests (page.request) against an instance started with a temporary LIBRARY_DIR: traversal matrix, POST scan cases, figure POST as TECH, owner and other-company users. Run figure.spec in CI with a bundled test PDF.

### I19. Untested critical path: tenant isolation (cross-company reads/writes by id) has no test at all
- **Where:** stitchbench `tests/smoke.spec.ts:1` (build-test-infra; qa; confirmed)
- **Status:** FIXED (round3-tail: tests/tenant.spec.ts + the second company in scripts/e2e-setup.ts). Not covered: partner-link, listing and order ids, and /api/export/* (per shop, no id)
- **Problem:** AGENTS.md's first rule, and the main subject of round 1 (8b3e5f2: 'Every market listing/order, image, export and CSV is scoped to the signed-in company'), is that every query filters by shopId and ownership is checked before touching a row by id. No spec signs in as a second company and tries another company's ticket/customer/machine/part/invoice ids, /api/photos/:id, /api/export/*, /api/sales, listing/order ids, partner-link ids or server actions with foreign ids. Every spec runs as a single E2E shop.
- **Scenario:** A future page or action forgets `shopId: user.shopId` (the pattern round 1 had to fix in ~30 files). No test fails, and a hosted multi-company install leaks or modifies another shop's customers and money records.
- **Fix:** Add a spec that creates two throwaway companies (scripts/e2e-setup.ts with two emails), records the ids that company A creates, and asserts that company B gets 404/UserError for every page, route handler and server action (replay the Next-Action with foreign ids).

### I20. Untested critical path: authentication, sessions and roles (login pacing, sessionVersion revocation, TECH/ADMIN limits, signup / first-run SETUP_TOKEN)
- **Where:** stitchbench `tests/smoke.spec.ts:12` (build-test-infra; qa; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE (round3-tail: tests/auth.spec.ts: login pacing after 5 wrong passwords, /signup 404, /companies 404 for a tech and for another company's owner; tests/roles.spec.ts for tech limits). Left: the TRUST_PROXY per-address limit (needs a proxy) and SETUP_TOKEN first-run sign-up (Windows program only)
- **Problem:** Round 1 added sign-in throttling and sessionVersion (sessions end when a user is removed or their password or role changes); round 2 changed it to per-account pacing plus a TRUST_PROXY-gated per-address limit; round 1 restricted Windows first-run sign-up to SETUP_TOKEN and the first company. No spec covers any of these. Specs only log in with valid credentials. walkthrough.spec signs in as a TECH only to take screenshots, and shop-library.spec's `/signup` 404 check runs only against the Windows program.
- **Scenario:** A regression lets a removed employee's existing session keep working, lets a TECH reach Settings -> People or install-admin hub actions, or reopens /signup on a hosted install with ALLOW_SIGNUP unset. No test notices.
- **Fix:** Add a spec that removes a user or changes their password or role and asserts that the old browser context is sent back to /login. Add 11 wrong passwords -> pacing message; TECH denied on admin-only actions; /signup 404 when ALLOW_SIGNUP is unset and after the first company.

### I21. Untested critical path: bench reports and 'Your data' exports (sales-log tax/commission/service totals, invoices owed, trade commission, customers/repairs/sales CSVs)
- **Where:** stitchbench `tests/smoke.spec.ts:100` (gap; qa; confirmed)
- **Status:** OPEN (low pass: needs a new UI-driven or multi-company spec; not attempted in the low pass)
- **Problem:** The only report assertions are smoke.spec.ts checking that /sales lists a customer name, walkthrough.spec.ts taking screenshots of /invoices and /sales, and inventory.spec.ts fetching /api/export/inventory. Nothing checks the Sales log figures (Sales tax, Service/Labor/Parts, Shop commission owed), the Invoices 'Still owed' total, trade-account commission on the customer page, or the customers, repairs and repair-payments CSV contents and date ranges. The three bugs above (tax missing for unrepaired/abandoned tickets, commission rewritten after an edit, the payments export limited to this month) would each have been caught by one assertion. This is separate from the reported gaps on register money paths and on ticket/register math.
- **Scenario:** A change to the sales-log aggregation or the export routes that drops tax, double-counts commission or narrows the export's date range passes every existing spec.
- **Fix:** Add an e2e (or unit test over the page data functions) that closes tickets in each closed status with taxable fees and a trade account, then asserts the Sales log cards, Invoices totals and each CSV's rows and date range.

### I22. Auth, sign-up, people and shop-settings paths have almost no automated tests
- **Where:** stitchbench `tests/walkthrough.spec.ts:40` (auth-accounts; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: see I20 (tests/auth.spec.ts)
- **Problem:** Coverage map for this area. walkthrough.spec adds a person and signs out and back in, but it's a screenshot tour with few assertions. shop-library.spec checks /signup returns 404 when sign-up is off. partners.spec creates a company from Companies with a partner code. smoke/market/inventory only sign in. Nothing tests: wrong-password pacing or lockout; session ending after Remove access, Reset password or Change password (other devices); /signup with ALLOW_SIGNUP, SETUP_TOKEN first-only, reserved platform emails, or a partner code on an install not on a hub; /companies returning 404 for non-platform users; techs being refused admin actions; shop-settings validation (tax, ticket counter, time zone); the ActionForm error path.
- **Scenario:** Regressions like the ones reported here (sign-up joining the install to a hub, pacing lockout, the counter rewind) ship unnoticed.
- **Fix:** Add an auth.spec covering: remove access, then the old session is sent to /login?ended=1 (page, API 401, server action); change password signs out a second context; a tech replaying saveShop gets the admin-only error; /companies is 404 for non-platform users; sign-up with a reserved email is refused; a partner code at sign-up on an install not on a hub is refused; blank or low next ticket number is refused.

### I23. No tests cover the fixes from the last round (import round-trip, feed holds, clientId retry) or the checker
- **Where:** stitchhub `test/smoke.mjs:247` (hub-core; code; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: smoke.mjs covers malformed input, bounds, link clashes and races, 413s, photo removal, ratings, history pages, join key; test/db.mjs checks export columns
- **Problem:** The smoke test only checks that the export is valid gzip and contains each row type. It never imports the export, never retries a note with the same clientId, never exercises holdFeeds/feedLimit with a concurrent in-flight write, and never runs checkLink. It also writes a permanent figure with a fixed id (0123456789abcdef01234567) to whatever hub it is pointed at, including production per the README, and every install then syncs it.
- **Scenario:** The regressions above (orphan rows on import, seq collisions, the missing client_id column, the checker overwrite) all passed `npm test`.
- **Fix:** Add an import round-trip into a fresh database (compare counts and seqs, and import twice for idempotency), a clientId retry test, a test that holds a transaction open and asserts /v1/changes doesn't pass it, and a checker test against a local HTTP server. Use a RUN-derived figure id so smoke runs don't leave a shared fixed figure behind.

### I24. Hub test gaps: smoke.mjs never tests import (hub moves), links import (catalog seeding), datasets, the link checker or several member-level denials, and it has side effects on a live hub
- **Where:** stitchhub `test/smoke.mjs:365` (build-test-infra; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: see I23; with IMPORT_HUB_URL the smoke test checks a hub move into an empty hub
- **Problem:** test/smoke.mjs is broad but only exports: the round-2 'hub moves keep change numbers' import path (POST /v1/admin/import) has no automated check, and that is how the client_id loss above went unnoticed. Also untested: POST /v1/admin/links/import (the only way to load hub_links.json), PUT/GET datasets, join-key change and old-key rejection, discord settings, admin post delete, /v1/links/:id/report, checker.mjs classification, and denials such as a member deleting another member's note or a partner-scope key calling member routes other than /changes. `npm run check` is only `node --check` syntax. README tells admins to run it against a production hub ('HUB_URL=https://your-hub ... npm test'). There it posts roughly 8 Discord notifications (joins, link add/fix, note, listing), leaves an ACTIVE partner link and a permanent test figure, and uses 4 of the 10 hourly joins of the shared 'local' register bucket.
- **Scenario:** A change to transfer.mjs breaks seq preservation or drops a column, and admins find out only after a real hub move when installs resync or duplicate data. Running the documented check on a production hub spams the community Discord.
- **Fix:** Extend smoke.mjs with: export -> import into a second DB (or the same one) asserting row counts, seq and client_id; links import of two rows plus duplicate skip; dataset put/get; member-level 403/404 cases. Add a SMOKE_QUIET flag that skips Discord notify, end the second partner link, and document that the check is for a test hub.

### I25. Test gap: hub smoke test doesn't cover body limits, blocked sellers, figure overwrites or photos of removed posts
- **Where:** stitchhub `test/smoke.mjs:153` (hub-market; qa; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: see I23 (removed listings delete their photos)
- **Problem:** smoke.mjs thoroughly covers the listing and order state machine, ratings and the changes feed. It never checks: that oversized bodies are refused on /register, /posts, /listings or /figures; that a blocked install's listings can't be ordered; that figures are first-write; or that deleted posts and removed listings stop serving their photos. Each of these failed in my runs above.
- **Scenario:** The DoS and moderation gaps above persist or come back unnoticed.
- **Fix:** Add those four cases to smoke.mjs once they are fixed.

### I26. Critical hub-move restore path (importStream), the link checker, and admin datasets are untested
- **Where:** stitchhub `test/smoke.mjs:386` (hub-core; qa; reproduced; confirmed)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: see I23 (refuses a hub with a Discord webhook unless SMOKE_ALLOW_DISCORD=1; ends its partner links)
- **Problem:** smoke.mjs exercises export (POST-nothing GET /v1/admin/export) and validates the gzip content, but never imports it back: `importStream` / `POST /v1/admin/import` (the entire 'moving or rebuilding a hub' feature that restores members, keys, links, seqs, orders and partner data) has zero test coverage, even though it is the most safety-critical operation for the community's data and was heavily reworked in commit 28842a1 (seq preservation, order/partner sequence carry-over, JSONB handling). Also untested: the background link checker (checker.mjs classify/probe/checkLink and its status transitions), and admin datasets PUT/GET /v1/admin/datasets and /v1/datasets/:name. A regression in the importer would silently corrupt a hub migration and only surface during a real move.
- **Scenario:** A future edit to transfer.mjs (column list, conflict target, seq/JSONB handling) passes `npm test` but breaks real imports, so a shop that rebuilds its hub from an export loses or mis-restores data with no CI signal.
- **Fix:** Add a smoke step that pipes the export into a second throwaway hub via POST /v1/admin/import and asserts links/posts/parts/orders/partner messages and seq positions survive and an existing install key still authenticates; add unit coverage for checker.classify and a checkLink status-transition case.

### I27. The hub (or whoever controls it) can forge any partner message, and it stores partner data in the clear
- **Where:** stitchhub `src/partners.mjs:4` (partners; security; plausible)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: docs say partner messages are stored unsigned and the hub operator could forge them. Left: end-to-end signing
- **Problem:** Partner messages are not signed end to end. The receiving install trusts fromSide, toShopRef, linkId and link records exactly as the hub sends them. A compromised hub, or a user tricked into a hostile long 'stitchpartner:' code while not yet on a hub, can inject settle.paid, job.paid, order.update DELIVERED, consign.* and link.created (new links on any shop whose id it knows). The file header says 'The hub doesn't read them', but customer short names, machine details, problems, intake photos and money figures are stored as plain jsonb for 365 days and are included in hub exports. The bench also stores the hub-supplied `side` without normalising it (partners.ts:222 and 685 pass out.side and l.side straight to saveLinkFromHub).
- **Scenario:** A hub operator inserts a settle.paid on a link and the repair shop's balance changes, with nothing showing it did not come from the partner.
- **Fix:** Longer term: exchange per-link keys at redeem time (inside the code both shops share) and sign or encrypt message bodies (for example Ed25519 signatures plus an AEAD). At minimum, correct the 'hub doesn't read them' claim in the docs and agreement text, and validate `side` from the hub as repair|retail.

### I28. Test gap: cross-reference remove/restore, merge undo and 'where to buy' editing round-trip are untested
- **Where:** stitchhub `test/smoke.mjs:106` (hub-commerce; qa; plausible)
- **Status:** PARTLY DONE: see I23
- **Problem:** Hub smoke covers xpart add/edit/revert/merge and the admin imports, but not DELETE/restore of xparts, revert of a merge target, concurrent create with the same number, history size, or usage aggregation limits (summed duplicates, sum overflow). The StitchBench e2e edits sources only with the simple 'Vendor, url, price' form and never re-saves a non-USD, comma-containing or noted source. It never removes and restores an entry, and never exercises pushUsage or the Most-used community page.
- **Scenario:** The data-loss and corruption bugs above (merge undo, sources round-trip, usage overflow) are not caught by either suite.
- **Fix:** Hub smoke: delete+restore an xpart, merge then revert the target and assert no numbers are orphaned, send 2 months of summed usage near the cap and assert /usage/top is 200. App e2e: edit an entry whose source has a comma vendor/URL and CAD currency and assert it is unchanged, and remove then restore an entry from the UI.


## Refuted by verifiers

- The 16 MB server-action body limit also applies to the anonymous login and sign-up actions (`next.config.ts:7`, security): The finding gets the effective limit wrong, and its fix would not reduce anonymous memory exposure. (1) /login and /signup are matched by src/proxy.ts. In Next 16.2.6, when the proxy runs, the request body is teed into in-memory PassThrough buffers (node_modules/next/dist/server/body-streams.js getC
- A tech can bill a stocked part at any price on a ticket, including $0.01 or a negative price; the register forbids this (`src/actions/tickets.ts:298`, security): The code does accept any price, but the TECH gains nothing it doesn't already have. On an open ticket every role can add a DISCOUNT line of any size through the 'Add fee or discount' form, and the result is the same net charge. The register's TECH price rule is also cosmetic, because completeSale le
- The repair-payments CSV (/api/sales) is not limited to owner/admin, unlike every other export (`src/app/api/sales/route.ts:8`, security): TECH access to /api/sales is by design. The Sales log page (src/app/(app)/sales/page.tsx) uses only requireUser, appears in the nav for every role (nav.tsx:49, no role gate), and its header has a "Download CSV" button that links to `/api/sales?from=...&to=...` (sales/page.tsx:50-52). A tech is meant
- The first-deploy seed writes the generated owner password into the deploy logs (`prisma/seed.ts:166`, security): The code does what the reviewer says: seed.ts:145 generates a password when SEED_OWNER_PASSWORD is unset and seed.ts:166 prints it once. It also runs on every deploy through `npm start` → `release` → `tsx prisma/seed.ts --if-empty`, and prints only when the User table is empty. It is still not a rea
- Manual library route serves any PDF under LIBRARY_DIR, including private folders and symlink/junction targets, to any signed-in user (`src/lib/library-dir.ts:15`, security): The behaviour is real, but it isn't a vulnerability. Serving the whole library folder to every signed-in shop user is the stated design: library-dir.ts:5-6 says the server reads the library from disk 'so every device on the shop network can open manuals'. Traversal, encoded, absolute and NUL tricks 
- Backups hold the live hub install key and every password hash in plain gzip, in a folder Windows often syncs to OneDrive (`desktop/backup.mjs:14`, security): Keeping HubState and User in backups is deliberate and needed for what backups are for. INSTALL.md promises that 'a backup restored onto a new PC brings all of it back'. restore() (backup.mjs ~158-161) resets the hub cursors so the same install id re-syncs its partner messages, and both joinHub and 
- Blocking a member doesn't revoke access while the shared join key is unchanged: the blocked shop re-registers in one request (`src/server.mjs:537`, security): The code works as described: blocking sets installs.blocked on one install, and the shared join key can register a new install. But this is the documented design of a shared join key, not a vulnerability, and the finding overstates the cost of the fix. Rotating the join key (PUT /v1/admin/join-key, 
- Account creation checks email uniqueness outside the insert: a concurrent duplicate gives a 500 instead of 'already has an account' (`src/lib/shops.ts:44`, code): The code does check the email first and insert later, without mapping P2002. But the scenarios the reviewer gives are covered elsewhere. A double-click can't cause it: the addUser and companies forms use <Submit>, which is disabled while pending (useFormStatus, action-form.tsx:61-68). Also, the Next
- Library file serving follows symlinks out of LIBRARY_DIR and sends PDFs without nosniff or sandbox headers (`src/app/api/library/file/route.ts:14`, code): Both behaviours exist, but neither can be reached by an attacker. (1) Symlinks and junctions are followed, as I confirmed. But no path in the app creates a link: uploads and downloadManualLink write regular files with sanitized names into _inbox. Only the PC owner can put a junction inside C:\\Manua
- Backups contain the hub install key (a bearer credential) and password hashes in plain gzip files under Documents (`desktop/backup.mjs:14`, code): The description is accurate: SKIP leaves out neither HubState (with installKey) nor User.passwordHash. But this is by design. A backup is a full disaster-recovery copy of the shop database, and INSTALL.md documents restoring it onto a new PC with the hub connection kept. The suggested DPAPI/safeStor
- Tech-role users can download the full repair-payments CSV (customers, serial numbers, amounts) although every other export is owner/admin-only (`src/app/api/sales/route.ts:8`, qa): There is no privilege escalation. The Sales log page (/sales, sales/page.tsx:11) is a requireUser page, listed in the nav for everyone, with any date range. It shows the same payments (date, ticket, customer, via account, machine, method, amount) and itself renders the 'Download CSV' button to every
- recordSettlement has no idempotency guard: two submits create two settlement rows and double-count the payment (`src/actions/partners.ts:107`, qa): This isn't a concurrency bug. Each submit is a separate, deliberate payment entry. The same-form double submit the title suggests is already blocked: ActionForm returns early while pending and Submit is disabled. The failure scenario needs two people or tabs each typing and recording the same real-w
- Partner message channel fully trusts the hub: a compromised/hostile hub can forge links, inventory, repair jobs and money settlements, and reads all partner data in plaintext (`src/lib/partners.ts:669`, qa): This describes the architecture, not a defect. The hub is the designated relay and link authority: it mints codes, creates links, issues install keys and delivers every message. By design it is trusted to author link.created/link.ended and to route by toShopRef. Neither the code nor the docs promise
- Manual library GET follows symlinks/junctions out of LIBRARY_DIR, returns an empty reply for a folder named *.pdf, and rejects real files whose names start with '..' (`src/lib/library-dir.ts:19`, qa): The behaviour is described correctly, but it isn't a real security problem. Nothing reachable over the web can create a link in the library. The only server writes into LIBRARY_DIR are POST /api/library/file and downloadManualLink, and both write regular files into _inbox with sanitized names and fl
